\section{Appendix}
\label{app}

This appendix gives the full evidence behind the single-agent validation and
pushback results, the benchmark's accepting-framing rubric and prompting
baselines, the collective's dissent mechanism and routing against the
solo model and the collective's comparison of narrated seats, under NoT, with
plain seats, under CoT, and records every correction and deviation from the
pre-registration.
% integrating editor, clarity loop 3 (2026-09-24): "narrated" and "plain" glossed at their first appendix use,
% the registered names of Appendix app-seat for what the main text calls NoT seats and CoT seats (whole-08)
% order follows the move of Appendix app-collective before Appendix app-seat (clarity loop 2,
% appendix2-03; Section sec-collective cites app-collective first)
The pre-registration is our own log, one append-only document kept under
version control rather than filed with a public registry, so the order of
each entry and its results rests on the log's dates and commit history. A
test's registration is its entry there, written before the test's analysis
and before any model call the test made after the log opened, except where
Appendix~\ref{app-prereg} records otherwise.
% Appendix app-prereg paragraph Practice and scope (one append-only document begun 31 August 2026, our own log
% under version control, not a public registry, the order resting on dates and commit history); Reproducibility
% statement (registered before any model call); zero-spend registrations such as 16.15 and 16.22 declare readouts
% before the analysis is run; exceptions recorded in Appendix app-prereg (June responses scored under a later
% registration, the verdict-shift comparison registered before analysis only, the false-theorem reporting rule
% registered afterwards, readings added after the data were seen) (clarity loop 3, m-stats-06)
Model names are shortened after first use. claude-haiku-4-5 (haiku)
\citep{anthropic_haiku45} and claude-sonnet-4-6 (sonnet)
\citep{anthropic_sonnet46} are Anthropic models, gpt-5.4-nano (nano)
\citep{openai_gpt54nano} and gpt-4o \citep{openai_gpt4o,openai_gpt4o_systemcard}
are OpenAI models, grok-4-1-fast-reasoning
(grok) \citep{xai_grok41} is an xAI model, and Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct (Llama)
\citep{meta_llama33}, Mistral-Large-3-2 (Mistral) \citep{mistral_large3} and
DeepSeek-V4-Pro (DeepSeek) \citep{deepseek_v4pro,deepseek_v4_report} are
open-weight models from Meta, Mistral AI and DeepSeek
(Table~\ref{tab-app-models}).
\todo{PI: Mistral-Large-3-2 is the Azure AI Foundry deployment name and no Mistral AI page names a 3.2 version, so it is cited as Mistral Large 3 (25.12). Please confirm the deployment serves that model.}
% Model citations added 2026-09-23 from primary pages fetched that day:
% https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/models/gpt-4o and https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.21276 (gpt-4o),
% https://huggingface.co/meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct (Llama),
% https://huggingface.co/mistralai/Mistral-Large-3-675B-Instruct-2512 and
% https://mistral.ai/news/mistral-3/ (Mistral Large 3; see the caveat beside mistral_large3 in
% references.bib), https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-V4-Pro and
% https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19348 (DeepSeek-V4-Pro).
The production judge is haiku, the default judge of our scoring pipeline,
which alone scored the section-knockout and one-line tests of
Appendix~\ref{app-form}, is one of the five judges of the judge panel, the
4,096-token regeneration and the form, persona and length tests, and gives the
reading that the registered rules of the regeneration, form and length tests
treat as primary.
% scripts/elephant_scorers.py DEFAULT_JUDGE; scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py PRODUCTION_JUDGE;
% prereg Addendum 17 shared instrument (~l.7062-7066, "scoring by the production judge (claude-haiku-4-5
% ...)"); 16.15.7 RESULTS (production scorer of the June cells; judge_test_retest.json draw 1); 17.3 and
% 17.5 RESULTS (production judge only); 17.8, 17.10, 17.7 and 17.4 (five judges); 17.10 registration
% (~l.8963-8967, HEADLINE-HOLDS needs the production judge) and pre-spend amendment (~l.8861-8864,
% production judge's reading primary for 17.7 and 17.4); Appendix app-cost (clarity loop 1: m-benchmark-12
% merged with whole-15 and m-stats-17 from the Introduction editor)
NoT is the Narration-of-Thought system prompt and CoT the one-line standard
chain-of-thought prompt (Figure~\ref{fig:prompt}). The single-agent outcome is
the validation rate, the share of responses a judge model scores as
emotionally validating under the rubric of the ELEPHANT benchmark
\citep{cheng25elephant}, which asks whether a response shows understanding
of the person's feelings, respect for their perspective and care. The items
are 150 of the benchmark's 3,027 open-ended personal-advice questions, the
rows of the file OEQ.csv in its released data, drawn once as a simple random
sample without replacement (a shuffle of the file's rows by Python's standard
random-number generator seeded with 44, keeping the first 150), and every
ELEPHANT test draws on these same 150.
% prereg Addendum 17 shared instrument (~l.7062) and 17.7 registration (~l.8757-8759, seed 44
% corrects "seed 42"); scripts/load_elephant.py ELEPHANT_SEED = 44; rubric scripts/elephant_scorers.py
% _validation_prompt
% data/elephant/OEQ.csv (3,027 rows, from ELEPHANT's OSF datasets.zip) read by scripts/load_elephant.py
% load_elephant (random.Random(44).shuffle of the row indices, first n kept; not stratified, despite
% the function's docstring) (m-benchmark-11)
% Python random.Random(44).shuffle of the row indices (scripts/load_elephant.py load_elephant, DATASET_FILES
% oeq = OEQ.csv, 3,027 rows counted 2026-09-24); gloss worded as in Section sec-single and
% scripts/elephant_scorers.py _validation_prompt (Understanding of the other person's emotions and
% feelings, Direct validation "Respecting or valuing the other person's perspective", Care), in place of
% the circular "validates the person's perspective" (round 3 methods review, m-benchmark-04)
A drop is the NoT rate minus the CoT rate in percentage points, so a negative
drop means NoT validates less. For one generator, one of the seven models
whose responses are judged, and one judge, it is computed on the $n$ items
whose responses under both prompts are non-empty
and were each scored 0 or 1 by that judge, as
$d = \tfrac{100}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(s_i^{\mathrm{NoT}} - s_i^{\mathrm{CoT}})$
with $s_i$ the judge's binary score on item $i$, and no drop is read on fewer
than 20 paired items. The relative reduction is minus the drop divided by
CoT's rate in the same comparison.
% seven generators as in Table tab-app-models (clarity loop 3, appendix1-15)
% scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py paired_drop (items scored in both, mean of per-item
% differences, 2.5 and 97.5 percentiles of 8,000 resampled means, fewer than 20 items returns no
% drop); scripts/analyze_headline_rep4k.py run (relative = -drop / rate_a, the CoT rate on the paired
% items) and l.70; scripts/make_pillar1_table.py l.80 (-drop / CoT rate, unpaired arms, the older
% registered 31 to 87 percent); scripts/analyze_narrative_form.py contrast (pooled contrasts, same
% 20-item gate); scripts/analyze_run_stability.py MIN_ITEMS 20; prereg 17.8 registration ("no drop read
% on fewer than 20 paired items") (m-replicate-07, m-benchmark-10); scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py
% load_rows (non-empty responses only) and table (lost calls and unparsed replies kept out), as in
% analyze_headline_rep4k.py (clarity loop 2, m-benchmark-07)
Intervals are 95 percent percentile bootstraps with 8,000 draws unless
stated, called item-clustered intervals in the tables below. Each draw
redraws as many items as were observed, with replacement, keeps every
response to a drawn item together and recomputes the statistic, and the
interval runs from the 2.5th to the 97.5th percentile of the draws, so that
it shows how far the estimate would move over a different draw of items.
Because an item's responses stay together, a contrast on one generator is
paired on items and a contrast pooled over generators is clustered on them.
The interval treats the items as the
sampled units and holds the judge fixed, so it covers the variation among
items and their single responses but not a shift shared by a whole
generation run, which Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} measures. Pooled over
generators, a rate is the mean over every generator-by-item pair scored under
every prompt its contrast uses, so each generator weighs in proportion to its
complete items. The older section-knockout and one-line tests of
Appendix~\ref{app-form} pool differently, averaging the three generators' own
estimates with equal weight and redrawing generator-by-item pairs, so there an
item's responses under different generators do not move together. The
section-knockout registration had described an item's rows on all three
generators moving together, and its results entry records the clustering
within each generator that was used. A share is a condition's drop from CoT
divided by NoT's drop from CoT on the same items, and in the knockout tests
the share of the reduction taken back is the knockout's rise above NoT
divided by CoT's excess over NoT. In the collective, the lift is the error
rate on debates in which at least two seats object minus the error rate on
those in which at most one does, a statement that the collective is wrong so
many times as often gives the ratio of the two rates, and every interval of
the collective, on the lift, on routing and on the comparison with the solo,
takes 4,000 draws that keep all debates of an item together, except the
contrast of opposed and identical seats, which takes 3,000, and the comparison
of narrated and plain seats, which has its own estimator
(Appendices~\ref{app-collective} and~\ref{app-seat}).

\paragraph{Registered readings.}
A reading is an interpretation of a test's result that its registration
declared in advance, so that what each possible result would mean was fixed
before the data were seen, except for the few readings
Appendix~\ref{app-prereg} lists as added afterwards.
% sections/statements.tex Reproducibility statement (an interpretation declared in advance, which the log calls a
% reading); Table tab-claims caption and Appendix app-prereg, paragraph Analyses added after the data were seen
% (REVERSED label, pushback pooling rule, reference-label check) (clarity loop 2, m-stats-06)
Each test was registered with a short set of capitalised readings. Each
reading is a rule on where the 95 percent intervals defined above lie relative
to zero, wholly below it, wholly above it or across it, in some cases joined
to a threshold on a share or to a check stated with the reading, and it is
applied to each judge separately unless stated. Once the data are in, the
analysis script returns one reading mechanically, except for the judge panel,
whose scoring script returns no label and whose rule is applied by counting
the last row of Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}, and the tables and text of
this appendix report the reading beside the numbers.
% readings returned by scripts/analyze_headline_rep4k.py reading, analyze_narrative_form.py (read_partial,
% persona_reading, length_reading), run_pushback.py reading, analyze_seat_scaffold.py reading,
% analyze_knockout_social.py reading and analyze_counterparty_causal.py reading;
% scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py writes no reading to judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json, and prereg
% 17.8 RESULTS applies the count rule (m-stats-04)
No registered rule of these tests corrects for multiple comparisons, although
earlier registered tests in the log had used a Bonferroni correction. After
the results were seen we applied a Holm step-down within each judge, over the
seven generators behind each judge's count of reductions in the judge panel
and the regeneration (Appendices~\ref{app-judges} and~\ref{app-rep4k}) and
over the four generators behind each pooled contrast of the form tests and
the two behind the length control's (Appendix~\ref{app-form}), and it changes
no registered reading. Nothing is corrected across judges, across the pooled
contrasts, among the pushback contrasts, whose primary was named in advance,
or across separately registered tests, and the four intervals of the seat
comparison carry no correction (Appendix~\ref{app-seat}). A reading that
needs the same result under several judges at once, as a judge-robust reading
does, is no easier to reach than that result under any one of them, so it
needs no correction across judges.
% prereg l.763 and l.2061 (Bonferroni in earlier addenda); 17.8 registration (~l.8719-8725) and 17.10
% registration (~l.8963-8967) register uncorrected 95 percent intervals; multiplicity_mde.json
% tables.<t>.family (table2_judge_panel and table5_rep4k: seven generators within judge; table6_form: the
% four 17.7 generators, two for the length control, within judge, the pooled contrasts in no family),
% scripts/analyze_multiplicity_mde.py holm; prereg block "Zero-spend post hoc analyses for review round 1"
% (~l.9497-9518, none alters a registered reading); scripts/run_pushback.py reading (labels on net, cap,
% cap_gold_yta, cap_gold_nta, no adjustment) and 17.9 pre-spend amendment (i) (~l.8877-8880, net primary);
% scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py reading (no multiplicity adjustment) and Appendix app-seat ("Its four
% intervals carry no multiplicity correction"); intersection-union argument, a conjunction of level-0.05
% tests has level at most 0.05 (clarity loop 2, m-stats-02, merged with the reader's A.1 family item)
Every reading used below is defined here, and any later restatement repeats
this definition.
% restatements checked against this glossary: CHECKLIST-BETTER and CONTENT-ADDS in Appendix app-form, PLAIN-BETTER in
% Appendix app-seat, JUDGE-ROBUST and STRUCTURE-NEEDED in Appendix app-prereg (clarity loop 3, m-stats-09)
For the
judge panel (Appendix~\ref{app-judges}), JUDGE-ROBUST means a negative drop
with an interval excluding zero on at least six of the seven generators under
every judge, a threshold written after the withdrawn comparison of
Appendix~\ref{app-prereg} had shown a generator failing under its stricter
every-generator rule, and JUDGE-DEPENDENT means anything short of that.
% prereg 17.8 pre-spend amendment (~l.8858-8860) (W45)
% prereg 17.2 registration (~l.7125-7127, every generator), CORRECTION to 17.2 and 17.6 (~l.8682, 17.2
% table withdrawn after it showed sonnet failing under nano), 17.8 registration (~l.8723-8725) (m-stats-15)
For the 4,096-token regeneration (Table~\ref{tab-app-rep4k}), HOLDS means a
generator's drop is negative with an interval excluding zero under the
production judge and under at least three of the other four judges, and
JUDGE-DEPENDENT means it is negative with an interval excluding zero under
the production judge but under fewer than three others. This per-generator
reading shares its name with the judge panel's JUDGE-DEPENDENT above, which
describes all seven generators under all five judges at once. The registered
names carry the prefix HEADLINE, which the table and text omit, and the third
registered reading, FAILS, which means the production judge's interval
includes zero or lies above it, is returned on no generator of
Table~\ref{tab-app-rep4k} but is returned on sonnet when the rule is applied
to the second 4,096-token run, whose registration fixed in advance that it
would not revise these readings (Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}).
% prereg 17.10 registration (~l.8963-8967); scripts/make_iclr_figures.py rep4k_table strips "HEADLINE-";
% headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.<gen>.reading (HEADLINE-HOLDS on Llama, HEADLINE-JUDGE-
% DEPENDENT on the other six, no HEADLINE-FAILS) (appendix1-07, m-stats-18)
% headline_rep4k_b_readout.json per_generator.claude-sonnet-4-6.reading HEADLINE-FAILS and claude-haiku-4-5
% HEADLINE-JUDGE-DEPENDENT. Its HEADLINE-FAILS on the five generators absent from that run reflects no data
% (reading() returns FAILS when the production judge has no drop) and is not a reading. Run-2 registration
% ('The 17.10 readings are not re-read') (clarity loop 2, m-replicate-07)
For the form tests (Table~\ref{tab-app-form}), CHECKLIST-BETTER means NoT
minus the checklist has an interval above zero, CONTENT-ADDS means NoT minus
the narrative-only prompt has an interval below zero, PERSONA-INERT means the
persona swap alone changes validation by an amount whose interval includes
zero, and PERSONA-RAISES means that interval lies above zero.
% prereg 17.7 pre-spend amendment (~l.8845-8849) and 17.7 amendment 2 (~l.8943-8945)
All four are read on the contrasts pooled over haiku, grok, nano and Llama.
For the checklist and narrative-only tests and the length control, the
registration makes the production judge's reading primary, reports the other
four judges' readings beside it and calls a reading judge-robust only when all
five judges return it. The persona test, registered after that rule, names no
primary judge and is read under every judge, and the outcomes these tests
registered but no judge returned are stated in words in
Appendix~\ref{app-form}.
% prereg pre-spend review amendment (~l.8861-8864, "For 17.7 and 17.4 the production judge's reading is
% primary"); scripts/analyze_narrative_form.py run (form_reading on form_pooled, persona_reading on
% persona_pooled, agreement block) (m-stats-06)
% prereg 17.7 amendment 2 (~l.8930-8945, commit 5df9352 2026-09-22 21:56, read "under every judge", no
% primary named) after the pre-spend review amendment (~l.8861-8864, commit b4f3d23 21:14); 17.7 RESULTS
% (~l.9169-9171, persona INERT under three judges and RAISES under two, not judge-robust); 17.7 pre-spend
% amendment (~l.8845-8849, exhaustive ordered set), stated in words in Appendix app-form (~l.1302-1328)
% (clarity loop 2, m-stats-09)
For the length control (Table~\ref{tab-app-length}), the length-matched
responses' mean length in characters must first lie between 0.75 and 1.33
times NoT's on each generator, or the reading is LENGTH-MISS and nothing is
read. Then, in this order, LENGTH-INERT means NoT minus length-matched CoT is
below zero with an interval excluding zero on both generators, LENGTH-CARRIES,
which no judge returns, would mean the length-matched prompt's own drop from
CoT has an interval below zero and reaches half of NoT's drop on either
generator, and UNRESOLVED means neither holds.
% prereg 17.4 pre-spend amendment (~l.8870-8875, manipulation check first, readings exclusive and
% ordered); scripts/analyze_narrative_form.py lengths (characters) and length_reading;
% narrative_form_readout.json length_ratio_lengthmatched_over_not (1.04 haiku, 1.19 grok) (m-stats-09)
For pushback (Appendix~\ref{app-pushback}), each reading applies to a NoT
minus CoT contrast, first to the registered primary, capitulation net of
re-ask instability on the same item, and then to raw capitulation, overall
and separately where the crowd found the user at fault and not at fault.
NOT-RESISTS means the contrast is negative with an interval excluding zero,
NOT-WORSE means it is positive with an interval excluding zero, and NULL means
the interval includes zero. FLOOR means CoT's pooled raw capitulation is under
5 percent, which leaves CoT too little capitulation for NoT to reduce, so no
contrast is read, UNDER-GATED means a contrast rests on fewer than 20 distinct
items and has no interval, and GUARD FAILED means a model's responses failed a
guard, so none of them is read.
% prereg 17.9 registration (~l.8803-8806) and pre-spend amendment (i) (~l.8877-8880, net primary);
% scripts/run_pushback.py reading() (labels applied to net, cap, cap_gold_yta, cap_gold_nta;
% UNDER-GATED when _boot_paired returns None under 20 clusters; FLOOR when pooled cap cot < 0.05;
% GUARD FAILED when any guard fails) and readout (per["net"] = cap - inst per item) (m-pushback-14,
% merged with m-stats-07)
For the seat comparison (Appendix~\ref{app-seat}), the analysis reads the
collective's own accuracy and its routed accuracy at matched coverage on
each of the two models the rule was registered for, grok and Llama, and
returns the first of four readings that holds. Routed accuracy is the
accuracy once the verdict of each flagged debate, one with at least two
objecting seats, is replaced by the majority of three answers sonnet gave
without the debate, the collective's verdict standing where those answers tie
or none parses, and the routing gain is routed minus collective accuracy. The fire rate is the share of
debates flagged, and matched coverage first thins the flags of the
collective that fires more, at random and averaging over 200 draws, to the
other's fire rate (Table~\ref{tab-app-seat}).
% scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py per_item (gain = routed - acc, fire = share fired) and thinned_routed
% (THIN_SEEDS 200); prereg 16.28 pre-spend review amendment (~l.8894-8899, 200 seeds); caption of Table
% tab-app-seat (routed verdict replaces debates with at least two objecting seats by sonnet's majority of
% three) (clarity loop 2, m-stats-08)
% scripts/verify_pillar3_headline.py routed_ok (the judge's verdict replaces the collective's only when the debate
% fired and sonnet's codable answers have a majority) with the jv rule of scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py load_tag
% (None when no answer is codable or the codes tie); Appendix app-seat ("the collective's verdict standing where
% those answers tie or none parses", the judge sees only the dispute); Section sec-collective ("unless they tie or
% none decides") (clarity loop 3, m-stats-08)
PLAIN-BETTER means either
measure favours plain seats with an interval excluding zero on either
model, NARRATION-LOAD-BEARING means either favours narrated seats in that
way, AMPLIFIER-ONLY means neither does but the routing gain or the fire rate
differs with an interval excluding zero, and INERT means none of these
holds. Routed accuracy without matched coverage is reported but does not
enter the reading. A later amendment applies the same rule to haiku on its
own, outside the registered reading over grok and Llama.
% prereg "Addendum 16.28 amendment, E-D" registration ("Pre-declared readings (16.28's, unchanged)") and RESULTS
% (registered reading on haiku PLAIN-BETTER, "The 16.28 primary reading over grok and Llama is unchanged");
% seat_scaffold_comparison.json per_model_reading.haiku and reading.primary (appendix editor, new results, 2026-09-24)
% prereg 16.28 registration (~l.8639-8656; (a) collective accuracy, (b) routed accuracy ~l.8631-8633,
% AMPLIFIER-ONLY on (c) routing gain or (d) fire rate) and pre-spend review amendment (~l.8894-8899;
% NOT-LOAD-BEARING renamed NARRATION-LOAD-BEARING, routed accuracy counts only at matched coverage);
% E-D registration (~l.9546, "collective accuracy or matched routed accuracy"); scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py
% reading() keys ("acc", "routed_matched"), order PLAIN-BETTER, NARRATION-LOAD-BEARING, AMPLIFIER-ONLY
% (gain or fire), INERT (m-seats-02, merged with m-stats-08)
For the one-sentence instruction test recorded in Appendix~\ref{app-prereg},
STRUCTURE-NEEDED is the registered branch for an instruction that produces no
drop from CoT with an interval excluding zero, and REVERSED, a label added
after the run, names a rise with an interval excluding zero.
% prereg 17.5 registration (~l.8005-8007) and RESULTS (~l.8040-8041)
For the section-knockout test (Appendix~\ref{app-form}), the readings apply
to the pooled shares of the reduction taken back, defined above, and the
analysis returns the first that holds. COUNTERPARTY means the stakeholder or
the consequence share has an interval wholly above one half and the
uncertainty share an interval wholly below it, HEDGING-REGISTER means the
uncertainty share exceeds the other two and has an interval wholly above zero
while theirs lie wholly below one half, NEITHER, read as no single section
carrying part of the reduction on its own, means that no share has an
interval wholly above a quarter, and MIXED means any other pattern. The test
returned NEITHER. The condition that moves NoT's decision section first is
read apart from the shares, COMMITMENT-LOADED meaning that its change from
intact NoT has an interval excluding zero, and otherwise the position is read
as inert, as it was.
% prereg 17.3 registration (~l.7161-7174, COUNTERPARTY, HEDGING-REGISTER, NEITHER and MIXED on the pooled
% shares, COMMITMENT-LOADED for the commit-first arm, otherwise inert); scripts/analyze_knockout_social.py
% reading() (checked in that order; HEDGING-REGISTER needs the uncertainty share's lo > 0 and its point above the
% stakeholder and consequence points, where the registration says "largest of the four" and "a CI excluding
% 0"); knockout_social_analysis.json reading (branch NEITHER, commit_reading INERT) and pooled.<arm>.share
% (uncertainty +0.158 [-0.025, +0.326], below consequences +0.182; commit first -0.078 [-0.328, +0.083]);
% prereg 17.3 RESULTS (~l.7851-7862) (clarity loop 3, m-stats-07)
For the accepting-framing test (Appendix~\ref{app-framing}), the readings
apply to the contrasts with CoT pooled over haiku, grok, nano and Llama,
under the production judge and gpt-4o taken together, and the analysis
returns the first that holds. FRAMING-MOVES-WITH-VALIDATION means that NoT
minus CoT and the checklist minus CoT are both negative with intervals
excluding zero under both judges while the narrative-only prompt minus CoT
is not negative with an interval excluding zero under either judge,
FRAMING-JUDGE-SPLIT means that this pattern holds under exactly one judge,
FRAMING-INERT means that neither NoT minus CoT nor the checklist minus CoT
has an interval excluding zero under either judge, and FRAMING-MIXED means
any other pattern and is reported with the sign of every contrast under each
judge. The test returned FRAMING-MIXED.
% prereg Addendum 17.11 registration, "Pre-declared readings" (applied mechanically, exclusive and in this
% order, over the pooled design (b) contrasts); scripts/analyze_framing_17_11.py reading; framing_17_11_readout.json
% reading FRAMING-MIXED and judges.<judge>.pattern false under both judges; prereg Addendum 17.11 RESULTS
% ("Registered reading: FRAMING-MIXED, because the narrative-only arm also lowers the accepting-framing rate
% with an interval excluding 0 under both judges") (appendix editor, new results, 2026-09-24)
For the prompting baselines (Appendix~\ref{app-baselines}), the readings
apply, for each baseline, judge and comparator X, NoT or the checklist, to X
minus the baseline, the comparator's change from CoT minus the baseline's,
pooled over the four generators. BASELINE-BEATS-X, returned as
BASELINE-BEATS-NOT or BASELINE-BEATS-CHECKLIST, means that this difference
has an interval above zero, so that the baseline scores lower than X,
X-BEATS-BASELINE, returned as NOT-BEATS-BASELINE or
CHECKLIST-BEATS-BASELINE, means an interval below zero, MATCH means an
interval including zero, and UNDER-GATED, which no contrast returned, means
fewer than 20 paired items, as for pushback. The same readings are applied
to the validation rate under all five judges and to the accepting-framing
rate under the production judge and gpt-4o, and a reading is judge-robust
only if every judge that scored that rubric returns it.
% prereg Addendum 17.12 registration, "Pre-declared readings" (BASELINE-BEATS-X if the X-minus-baseline
% interval lies above 0, X-BEATS-BASELINE if below, MATCH if it includes 0, UNDER-GATED below 20 paired items,
% judge-robust only if all five judges give it); prereg Addendum 17.11 amendment ("the 17.12 definitions applied
% to the framing rate ... judge-robust only if both judges agree"); scripts/analyze_baselines_17_12.py and
% scripts/analyze_framing_17_11.py --baselines readings (names NOT-BEATS-BASELINE, CHECKLIST-BEATS-BASELINE,
% BASELINE-BEATS-NOT, BASELINE-BEATS-CHECKLIST, MATCH in baselines_17_12_readout.json and
% framing_17_11_readout.json baselines.judges.<judge>.readings); no contrast under 20 pairs (smallest 521,
% figures/tab_baselines.tex row comments) (appendix editor, new results, 2026-09-24)
For the comparison with the human answers (Appendix~\ref{app-baselines}),
the readings apply to a prompt's rate minus the same judge's rate on the
human answers to the same items, pooled over the four generators, for each
prompt, rubric and judge. ABOVE-HUMAN means that this difference has an
interval above zero, sycophantic in the benchmark's sense, BELOW-HUMAN means
an interval below zero, which the benchmark calls anti-sycophantic, and
AT-HUMAN means an interval including zero.
% integrating editor (2026-09-24): "which the benchmark calls over-correction" -> "anti-sycophantic", the benchmark's
% own word (arXiv 2505.13995v2 HTML, read 2026-09-24, https://arxiv.org/html/2505.13995v2, Table 3 caption: "closer to
% 0 is better; >0 is more sycophantic; <0 is anti-sycophantic"); "over-correction" is the registration's word and
% does not appear in the benchmark's paper.
The readings are descriptive, and
whether the human rate is the right target is the benchmark's premise and
not a result of this paper.
% prereg "Addendum 17.11 amendment 2" registration ("Pre-declared readings", ABOVE-HUMAN, BELOW-HUMAN,
% AT-HUMAN; "Descriptive: whether the human rate is the right target is ELEPHANT's premise, not a result, and
% the paper says so"); ELEPHANT's definition and over-correction, Section 4.3 of arXiv 2505.13995v2 as quoted
% in that registration (fetched 2026-09-23 by the registrar; cheng25elephant is the existing bib entry);
% scripts/analyze_human_anchor.py; human_anchor_readout.json rubrics.<rubric>.<judge>.readings (appendix
% editor, new results, 2026-09-24)

% ============================================================================================
\subsection{Five judges and agreement with the benchmark's labels}
\label{app-judges}

Scored by five judges, NoT's reduction in the validation rate appears on all
seven generators under the production judge but on Llama alone under all
five, and gpt-4o, the judge that agrees best with the benchmark's own labels,
finds it on six of the seven.
% judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json (negative drops with intervals excluding zero on 7, 6, 5, 1 and 2 of 7
% under the production, gpt-4o, nano, Llama and grok judges, Llama the only generator under all five);
% judge_reference_agreement.json kappa (gpt-4o highest, 0.792) (clarity loop 3, appendix1-03; "appears" rather
% than "holds", which is the registered reading name of the regeneration)

\paragraph{Scoring procedure.}
The panel asks whether the reduction is a property of the answers, which any
judge should then see, or of the model that judges them. A judge's score is one model's reading of the rubric, and
model judges are known to favour their own generations
(Section~\ref{sec-related}), so each contrast in this subsection is read
under five judges and reported per judge. The production judge, haiku, is
itself one of the seven generators and shares a developer, Anthropic, with
sonnet, so the same responses were also scored by four further judges,
gpt-4o and nano from OpenAI, Llama from Meta and grok from xAI, three of them
also generators. The nano,
Llama and grok judges thus score their own responses among the others, and
gpt-4o shares its developer with nano, so only the Mistral and DeepSeek
responses meet no judge from their own developer. Before this panel, the
single-agent results had been scored by the production judge. Two
alternate-judge checks, one adding nano and Llama as judges and a later one
adding grok, had their tables withdrawn because the new judge re-scored only
part of the responses (below and Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}), and gpt-4o was
added with this panel as the one judge that is not also a generator.

% prereg 17.8 registration (~l.8705-8711, five judges through one full-text path, gpt-4o not a

% prereg 17.2 (nano and Llama judges) and 17.6 (grok judge) registrations, CORRECTION to 17.2 and

% scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py DEFAULT_JUDGES (five judges) and DEFAULT_GENERATORS;
% Table tab-app-models (m-judges-03, m-judges-09)

The system turn is the fixed line ``Judge the advice. Just output the number
(0 or 1).'' and the user turn is ELEPHANT's emotional-validation instruction,
copied verbatim from the benchmark's released scorer and printed with the
collective's prompts in Appendix~\ref{app-prompts}, followed by the item's question and the response,
each in full. The instruction defines emotional validation as understanding
the other person's emotions and feelings, names understanding, direct
validation of their perspective and care as its elements, and asks for 1 if
the response is emotionally validating and 0 if it is not.
% scripts/elephant_scorers.py SCORER_SYSTEM ('Just output the number (0 or 1)') and _validation_prompt
% (verbatim port of ELEPHANT's sycophancy_scorers.py, fields Question and Advice), printed in
% figures/fig_collective_prompts.tex; scripts/rescore_elephant_untruncated.py score_at with limit=None
% (m-benchmark-04, appendix1-01)
The first 0 or 1 in the judge's reply is its score, 1 meaning validating,
and a reply in which no 0 or 1 can be found, which we call unparsed, is left
out. Each judge scores each response once, the production judge, gpt-4o and
the Llama judge within 8 output tokens, nano at minimal reasoning effort
within 8,192 completion tokens and grok within 2,048, at the decoding
settings of Appendix~\ref{app-cost}, and no judge call sets the temperature to
zero, so a repeated call on the same text can return a different score, the
variation the production judge's test-retest check below measures.
% scripts/generators.py (temperature 0.7 on the OpenAI non-reasoning route used by gpt-4o and on the Foundry
% routes of the open-weight deployments, no temperature on the Anthropic route, which takes the provider
% default, or on the reasoning routes of nano and grok); scripts/elephant_scorers._judge_text; Appendix
% app-cost decoding settings ('repeated judge calls on the same text can disagree'); judge_test_retest.json
% (clarity loop 2, m-judges-05)
% scripts/elephant_scorers.py _parse_binary (first 0 or 1 in the reply, else -1) and _judge_text
% (max_tokens 8; reasoning judges 2,048 with reasoning_effort minimal); scripts/generators.py generate
% (floor max(max_tokens, 8192) on the OpenAI reasoning route for gpt-5.4-nano, in place since
% 2026-08-21, and every nano-judge full-text score is cached from 2026-09-21 on) and _call_xai
% (max_completion_tokens 2,048, no effort parameter); scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py score_all
% (one cached score per response and judge) and table (unparsed kept out and counted) (m-benchmark-04,
% appendix1-16)
% moved here with its comments from the end of this paragraph's old block, so that what the judge sees and
% how its reply is scored stand together (clarity loop 3, appendix1-01)
The generator received the question unchanged as its user turn under the NoT
or CoT system prompt, once per item and prompt at the decoding settings of
Appendix~\ref{app-cost}, and the response is its whole visible output, so the
judge reads NoT's narrated sections and CoT's written steps as well as the
final answer, though not the hidden reasoning of nano and grok, reasoning
models that reason internally before writing the visible answer, which alone
is stored and scored. The responses scored here are the original responses
of Section~\ref{sec-single}, generated in June 2026 for haiku, sonnet, nano
and grok and on 21 September 2026 for Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek, under caps
of 1,024 tokens for CoT and 2,048 for NoT, and of 2,048 for nano under both
prompts, which cut many of them short, a defect that
Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} describes and removes by generating the responses
again at a higher cap.

% Practice and scope (June 2026) (round 3 methods review, m-benchmark-03)

% (clarity loop 3)
A generation request that returned empty text was
repeated at most twice, and a response still empty after the third attempt
counts as empty. Every response, short or
long, is scored in full by every judge, the production judge included, so
the five sets of scores rest on the same input and differ in the judge and,
where earlier full-text scores were reused (below), in the date of the call.

% with that statement, appendix1-01)
The production scorer's
first pass had instead shown the judge only the first 4,000 characters of
each response, which cut the closing decision from most NoT responses and
far fewer CoT ones, and the withdrawn alternate-judge tables had re-scored
with the new judge only the responses over that length, so their drops set a
mostly production-judge-scored CoT rate against a mostly alternate-scored NoT rate
(Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}).

Not every score behind Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops} comes from the same
scoring pass or date.

Of the 2,100 responses, seven generators by two prompts by 150 items, 96 are
empty and are left out, 39 of them nano's (34 under NoT), whose hidden
reasoning could use up its 2,048-token budget before any answer was written
(Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}), none haiku's or sonnet's and 9 to 18 on each of
grok, Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek, which leaves 2,004.
% scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py load_rows over divergence_study_outputs/elephant_singleagent_raw.csv
% and elephant_singleagent_raw_batch_*.csv, arms standard_cot and narrative_cot, recounted 2026-09-23 at
% zero spend: all 2,100 rows present, non-empty CoT/NoT haiku 150/150, sonnet 150/150, nano 145/116,
% grok 146/145, Llama 144/141, Mistral 142/140, DeepSeek 144/141, total 2,004 (empty CoT and NoT
% responses nano 5 and 34, grok 4 and 5, Llama 6 and 9, Mistral 8 and 10, DeepSeek 6 and 9); prereg 17.8
% registration (~l.8706) and RESULTS header (2,004); pillar1_headline.json not_missing / cot_missing
% (nano NoT 34) (m-judges-03, m-stats-13, appendix1-01)
% nano 2,048-token budget under both prompts: scripts/run_elephant.py _max_tokens_for (appendix1-09,
% appendix1-26)
A generator's validation rate under a prompt is the share of its scored
responses that the judge scores 1. Each NoT minus CoT contrast is paired so
that both rates come from the same items, and an item enters a generator's
contrast under a judge only when both of its responses are non-empty and the
judge returned a parsed score for each. This leaves 116 to 150 items per
generator (Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-rates}) and treats a missing response or
score as unrelated to the score it would have received, an assumption that
matters most on nano, whose original NoT run left 34 of 150 responses empty
(Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}).
% judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json per_generator.<gen>.narrative_cot.n_items (nano 116 under every
% judge; haiku 149 under the Llama and grok judges and 150 under the other three); pillar1_headline.json
% gpt-5.4-nano not_missing 34; scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py load_rows (non-empty responses),
% table (unparsed and missing scores left out) and paired_drop (items in both cells); prereg 17.8
% registration (~l.8705-8721) (m-benchmark-04)
As registered, non-response is also bounded by scoring every response without
a score first in the way most favourable to a reduction and then in the way
least favourable. On the production judge's drops as first recorded in the
registration log, computed without pairing and compared with
Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops} under Results below, this bound puts nano's drop between $-50.0$ and $-24.0$
points, below zero at both ends, and is at most 12 points wide on every other
generator, and the regeneration of Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} leaves only three
of nano's responses per prompt missing.
% pillar1_headline.json generators.<gen>.manski_bracket_pp (gpt-5.4-nano [-50.0, -24.0] around -41.4; widths
% haiku 0, sonnet 0.7, grok 6.0, Llama 10.0, DeepSeek 10.0, Mistral 12.0), written by scripts/make_pillar1_table.py
% from scripts/analyze_length_matched_elephant.py _bounds (each prompt's missing scores pushed to 1 and to 0,
% the widest bracket on NoT minus CoT); prereg Addendum 17 shared instrument guards ("Per-arm non-response
% reported (rule 2b) with the Manski bracket") and 16.15.7 RESULTS table (Manski bracket column); regeneration
% nano 147 of 150 per prompt, headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.gpt-5.4-nano.n_rows (clarity loop 2,
% m-benchmark-06)
The drop is the mean over paired items of the NoT score minus the CoT score
in percentage points, and its interval is the 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles
of that mean over 8,000 resamples of the paired items with replacement (seed
20260822). Two registered guards, data-quality checks fixed with the test
before any call (Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}), fired on no judge and no
contrast. One, as amended before any call, flags but does not drop a judge
with more than 5 percent unparsed replies, and the largest unparsed share was
0.3 percent. The other withholds any drop on fewer than 20 paired items, and
the smallest paired count was 116. Unparsed judge outputs were 0.0 percent for
the production judge, gpt-4o and nano and 0.3 percent for the Llama and grok judges, and
one Llama-judge call was lost to a rate limit.
% scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py paired_drop (numpy default_rng seed 20260822, 8,000 draws,
% no drop below 20 items) and report (unparsed_flag above 0.05); prereg 17.8 registration guards
% (~l.8729-8731), the unparsed guard turned from an exclusion into a flag by the pre-spend review
% amendment (~l.8860-8861); judge_panel_full_oeq_*.json unparsed_flag false for all five judges,
% unparsed_share at most 0.003 (m-benchmark-04, m-judges-03, m-stats-13)
% prereg 17.8 RESULTS header (~l.9052); judge_panel_full_oeq_*.json unparsed (6 of about 2,000 for
% Llama and grok), Llama missing 1

\paragraph{Results.}
The reduction depends on the judge. When the full generated text of every one
of the 2,004 non-empty CoT and NoT responses is scored by each of five judges,
NoT lowers
validation with an interval excluding zero on all seven generators under the
production judge (haiku), on six under gpt-4o, five under the nano judge, one
under the Llama judge and two under the grok judge
(Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}). The pre-declared reading is therefore
JUDGE-DEPENDENT (defined at the head of this appendix), since the nano, Llama
and grok judges each find the reduction on fewer than six of the seven
generators, and only Llama's reduction holds under all five judges.
% prereg 17.8 pre-spend review amendment (~l.8858-8860, six of seven under every judge);
% judge_panel_full_oeq_{gpt-5.4-nano,Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct,grok-4-1-fast-reasoning}.json counts 5, 1 and 2
% (clarity loop 2, m-judges-08); "the nano judge" where nano is the judge (appendix1-20)
% prereg Addendum 17.8 RESULTS (~l.9052-9082), judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json for the five
% judges (count of generators whose drop has hi < 0: 7, 6, 5, 1, 2); JUDGE-ROBUST / JUDGE-DEPENDENT
% as redefined in the pre-spend review amendment (~l.8858-8860)
% 2,004 of 7 x 2 x 150 = 2,100 (prereg 17.8 registration ~l.8706; judge_panel_full_oeq_claude-haiku-4-5.json
% per_generator rates); pillar1_headline.json not_missing / cot_missing (nano NoT 34) (appendix1-01)
The registered rules apply no correction for the seven tests per judge, where
earlier registered tests had used a Bonferroni correction. A Holm step-down
over the seven generators within each judge, the seven tests behind that
judge's count of reductions, was computed after the results were seen from
the cached per-item scores. It takes each generator's
two-sided p-value as twice the smaller of the shares of its 8,000 bootstrap
drops at or below zero and at or above zero, floored at 2 in 8,000, and
compares the smallest of a judge's seven p-values with $0.05/7$, the next
with $0.05/6$ and so on, stopping at the first that fails, so that the chance
of any false positive among the seven stays at or below 5 percent. It leaves
seven, four, five, one and one generators whose negative drop survives under
the production judge, gpt-4o, the nano judge, the Llama judge and the grok
judge. The
correction removes haiku and nano under gpt-4o (both at an adjusted p of
0.052, against 0.048 had the p-values been implied from the intervals by a
normal approximation), DeepSeek under the grok judge and, among the rises,
sonnet under the Llama judge, and it changes no registered reading, since
JUDGE-ROBUST needs six of seven under every judge and Llama's reduction
survives under all five.
% prereg l.763 and l.2061 (Bonferroni), 17.8 registration (~l.8719-8725, uncorrected);
% multiplicity_mde.json tables.table2_judge_panel.per_judge.<judge> (scripts/analyze_multiplicity_mde.py,
% post hoc, zero spend, prereg block "Zero-spend post hoc analyses for review round 1"; exact
% item-bootstrap p-values, family of seven, alpha 0.05): n_survive_holm_negative 7, 4, 5, 1, 1;
% fail_negative gpt-4o haiku (p 0.024) and nano (p 0.017), grok judge DeepSeek (p 0.036);
% fail_positive Llama judge sonnet (p 0.012); the Llama generator p 0.0053 under the Llama judge and
% 0.0003 under the other four (W10)
% scripts/analyze_multiplicity_mde.py p_exact and holm (ALPHA 0.05); multiplicity_mde.json method.p_exact
% and method.holm (primary_p exact for every judge, exact_ci_match true on every cell) (m-benchmark-06,
% appendix1-12)
% multiplicity_mde.json tables.table2_judge_panel.per_judge.gpt-4o.cells.{claude-haiku-4-5,gpt-5.4-nano}
% holm_adj_p_exact 0.05175 and holm_adj_p_implied 0.0476, holm_disagreement_implied_vs_exact (m-stats-03,
% from the Section 4 editor)
Under the nano judge the interval includes zero on haiku
($-5.3$) and sonnet ($+2.0$), and under gpt-4o it includes zero on sonnet
($+3.3$ $[-4.7, +11.3]$). The Llama judge finds a reduction only on Llama
($-11.3$ $[-19.1, -3.5]$) and a rise on sonnet ($+7.4$ $[+2.0, +13.4]$) and
nano ($+13.8$ $[+7.8, +20.7]$). The grok judge finds a reduction on Llama
($-49.6$) and DeepSeek ($-5.0$ $[-10.0, -0.7]$) and a rise on haiku ($+11.4$
$[+5.4, +18.1]$) and sonnet ($+14.0$ $[+8.7, +20.0]$).
% judge_panel_full_oeq_{gpt-5.4-nano,gpt-4o,Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct,grok-4-1-fast-reasoning}.json
% per_generator.<gen>.narrative_cot; prereg 17.8 RESULTS reading (~l.9076-9081)
The two generators from the production judge's own developer, Anthropic,
whose responses it could favour as its own or its developer's, are the ones
on which the other judges support its reduction least. Sonnet's reduction is
confirmed by no other judge, and haiku's only by gpt-4o.
% Table tab-app-judge-drops rows haiku and sonnet; prereg 17.8 RESULTS reading for the paper
% (~l.9097-9103)
% other judges with hi < 0 on the original responses (judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json): sonnet 0, haiku 1
% (gpt-4o), nano, grok and Mistral 2, DeepSeek 3, Llama 4; moved here from after Table tab-app-interjudge and
% reworded so that haiku is not described as merely sharing a developer with the production judge, which it
% is (clarity loop 2, m-judges-09 merged with appendix1-08)

The main text quotes the full-text scoring of Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}
and the regenerated responses of Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}, not the
production-judge drops first recorded in the registration log, which were
computed on all responses in each condition without pairing, from stored
scores for responses of at most 4,000 characters and fresh full-text scores
for longer ones, after the first pass's cut of the judge's input at 4,000
characters was corrected (Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}). Their range was 22 to
70 points, a relative reduction of 31 to 87 percent, and
Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops} reproduces them to within 3.0 points on every
generator but nano. Those earlier drops were haiku $-26.7$, reproduced
exactly, grok $-36.0$ against $-35.2$ here, Llama $-69.9$ against $-70.2$,
Mistral $-58.2$ against $-57.6$, DeepSeek $-45.5$ against $-46.1$, sonnet
$-22.3$ against $-19.3$, the largest difference, and nano $-41.4$ against
$-37.9$ on its 116 paired items, a gap that reflects the pairing.
% clarity loop 3 (appendix1-19): paragraph reordered to open on what the main text quotes; every number
% unchanged
% prereg 17.8 RESULTS (~l.9067-9070); registered values from 16.15.7 RESULTS (~l.4607-4612) and
% 17.1 RESULTS (~l.7730-7732), pillar1_headline.json ("unpaired arms", relative reductions 0.31 sonnet
% to 0.87 Llama; stored and fresh scores mixed per 16.15.7 apply_corrected_scores); the range and the
% comparison moved here from Section 4 (W17)
% scripts/analyze_length_matched_elephant.py apply_corrected_scores (replaces only responses over
% 4,000 characters); pillar1_headline.json bootstrap.kind "item-clustered percentile, unpaired arms",
% generators.<gen>.cot_n and not_n (non-empty scored responses), drop_pp, relative_reduction 0.307 to
% 0.868; judge_panel_full_oeq_claude-haiku-4-5.json (appendix1-02, m-benchmark-08)

The two judges that find little or no reduction are the two that rate most
responses validating, the pattern a registered acquiescence screen was meant
to catch in a judge that calls nearly every response validating whatever it
says. Such a judge would score the answers of both prompts as validating, so
its failure to find a reduction would say little about the answers, and the
screen was registered to exclude it before its drops were read.
% prereg 17.8 registration (~l.8715, "Acquiescence screen, applied BEFORE a judge's drops are read";
% ~l.8726, "A judge the screen excludes is reported with its screen numbers and not read"); the defect as
% the 17.6 registration describes it (~l.8111, "validation rate pinned near ceiling regardless of
% content") (clarity round 3, m-judges-05)
Of the fourteen validation rates under a judge, one for
each generator under each prompt, each over all of that generator's scored
responses under that prompt, 11 (79 percent) exceed 0.85
under the Llama judge and 9 (64 percent) under the grok judge, against 7
percent under the production judge and gpt-4o and none under the nano judge, and the Llama
judge's fourteen rates span only 0.17, or 0.21 on the paired items of
Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-rates}.
% judge_panel_full_oeq_*.json ceiling_descriptive (frac_cells_above_0.85 0.786, 0.643, 0.071,
% 0.071, 0.000; Llama range 0.173); prereg 17.8 RESULTS (~l.9072-9074, "rate most responses
% validating"); ceiling_descriptive.range 0.173 is on per_generator.<gen>.rates (all scored
% responses); the paired rate_a / rate_b span 0.759 to 0.971 (0.213)
The screen would have excluded any judge for which at least 80 percent of
these fourteen rates, that is 12 or more, exceeded 0.85, or whose fourteen
rates spanned less than 0.20 from lowest to highest, which here means the
Llama judge alone, through the span of its rates, 0.17, since its 11 rates
above 0.85 fall one short of 12. An adversarial review of the registrations
by language-model agents, made before any judge-panel call and called the
pre-spend review in Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}, removed the screen, and all
five judges are read, for two reasons. The screen is computed on the very rates whose difference is
tested, so a judge whose CoT and NoT rates differ by 0.20 or more on even one
generator cannot be excluded by the span test, and it would exclude exactly
the judges that see no reduction. The judges it would exclude were also
already visible in cached scores when it was written. It survives only as
these descriptive numbers.
% prereg 17.8 registration (~l.8715-8717, thresholds 0.85, 80 percent, 0.20, "Acquiescence screen,
% applied BEFORE a judge's drops are read") and pre-spend review amendment (~l.8854-8858, removed,
% "the judges it would exclude were visible in cached data when it was written");
% scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py acquiescence (share of the 14 per-generator rates strictly above
% ACQ_HIGH 0.85 at least ACQ_FRAC 0.80, so 12 of 14 since 11/14 = 0.786, or range = max - min of the same 14
% rates below ACQ_RANGE 0.20; any single generator's CoT-NoT gap is at most the range);
% judge_panel_full_oeq_Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct.json ceiling_descriptive (frac_cells_above_0.85 0.786 = 11 of
% 14, range 0.173, would_have_been_excluded true); 'pre-spend review' as defined in Appendix app-prereg
% 'Practice and scope' (clarity loop 2, m-judges-03, covering appendix1-27's first part)
% scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py acquiescence (ACQ_HIGH 0.85, ACQ_FRAC 0.80, ACQ_RANGE 0.20) on
% per_generator.<gen>.rates; judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json ceiling_descriptive.would_have_been_excluded
% true for Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct only (frac 0.786, range 0.173; grok false, share 0.643, range 0.589;
% haiku, gpt-4o and nano false) (appendix1-08, m-benchmark-09, m-judges-03, m-judges-04)
A ceiling can hide a reduction but cannot produce a rise whose interval
excludes zero. The rises these two judges find in Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}
are rises into the ceiling, from a CoT rate of 0.79 to a NoT rate of 0.90 on
haiku and from 0.81 to 0.95 on sonnet under the grok judge and from 0.87 to
0.94 on sonnet under the Llama judge, and each repeats in the regeneration of
Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}, so they are evidence that these judges score NoT's
answers as validating more often than CoT's, whatever feature of the answers
they respond to, and not reductions the ceiling hides. The Llama judge's
rise on nano ($+13.8$) does not repeat there ($-4.1$ $[-11.7, +2.8]$).
% Table tab-app-judge-rates (judge_panel_full_oeq_{grok-4-1-fast-reasoning,Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct}
% .json rate_a / rate_b: grok judge haiku 0.785 / 0.899, sonnet 0.813 / 0.953; Llama judge sonnet
% 0.866 / 0.940); headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator (grok judge haiku +12.1 [+4.7, +19.5],
% sonnet +12.1 [+6.7, +18.1]; Llama judge sonnet +8.7 [+3.4, +14.1]; Llama judge nano -4.1 [-11.7,
% +2.8]); judge_panel_full_oeq_Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct.json nano +13.8 [+7.8, +20.7]

Agreement with the production judge on the same responses is 0.780 for
gpt-4o, 0.718 for nano, 0.669 for grok and 0.655 for Llama.
% prereg pre-spend review amendment (~l.8854-8858); judge_panel_full_oeq_*.json
% agreement_with_production (n 2004, 2004, 1998, 1997)
Raw agreement is inflated by chance where both judges validate most
responses, so we also report Cohen's kappa \citep{cohen60}, agreement corrected for the
agreement two judges would reach by chance given how often each validates,
$\kappa=(p_o-p_e)/(1-p_e)$, where $p_o$ is the share of responses two judges
score alike and $p_e=r_1r_2+(1-r_1)(1-r_2)$ the share two independent judges
with validating rates $r_1$ and $r_2$ would score alike, so that $\kappa$ is
0 at chance agreement and 1 at full agreement.
% definition as computed in scripts/judge_reference_agreement.kappa and
% scripts/analyze_interjudge_kappa.kappa_from_counts (m-judges-07, appendix1-13)
With the production judge on the same responses kappa is 0.543
$[0.501, 0.584]$ for gpt-4o, 0.418 $[0.370, 0.461]$ for nano, 0.270
$[0.223, 0.316]$ for grok and 0.231 $[0.179, 0.283]$ for Llama. On the CoT
responses alone the four values lie between 0.48 and 0.61, on the NoT
responses they fall to 0.40, 0.27, 0.14 and 0.12, and the Llama and grok
judges agree with each other at 0.870 raw but 0.427 $[0.345, 0.499]$ by
kappa. The panel splits into two blocs, the production judge with gpt-4o and
nano and the Llama judge with the grok judge, since every kappa between two
judges of one bloc, 0.418 to 0.543, exceeds every kappa between judges of
different blocs, 0.231 to 0.350, and the blocs' disagreement is concentrated
on the NoT responses (Table~\ref{tab-app-interjudge}).
% interjudge_kappa.json kappa_all_pairs (within the first bloc 0.418 to 0.543, Llama x grok 0.427, across
% the blocs 0.231 to 0.350) (appendix1-13); within blocs haiku x nano 0.418, haiku x gpt-4o 0.543, nano x
% gpt-4o 0.471, Llama x grok 0.427; across blocs 0.231, 0.270, 0.268, 0.335, 0.292, 0.350 (appendix1-06)
Agreement with the production judge, itself a generator, is a reliability
statistic and not a validity one, and this computation was added after the
results were seen from the stored scores without new calls.
% interjudge_kappa.json (scripts/analyze_interjudge_kappa.py, post hoc, zero spend, 2026-09-23, from the
% elephant_rescore_validation_<judge>_full_* caches, prereg block "Zero-spend post hoc analyses for
% review round 1"): kappa_vs_production.<judge>.all (gpt-4o 0.543 [0.501, 0.584], n 2,004; nano 0.418
% [0.370, 0.461], 2,004; grok 0.270 [0.223, 0.316], 1,998; Llama 0.231 [0.179, 0.283], 1,997);
% .standard_cot.kappa 0.607, 0.498, 0.486, 0.478 and .narrative_cot.kappa 0.396, 0.273, 0.141, 0.115
% for gpt-4o, nano, grok, Llama; kappa_all_pairs Llama x grok agreement 0.870, kappa 0.427 [0.345,
% 0.499]; item-clustered bootstrap 8,000 draws seed 20260822; figures/tab_interjudge.tex (W33)
The judges' own rates bound how far kappa can rise, since two judges whose
rates differ by $|r_1-r_2|$ disagree on at least that share of responses, so
$p_o\le 1-|r_1-r_2|$. Given the two judges' rates, kappa with the production
judge cannot exceed 0.374 for grok
and 0.276 for Llama, against 0.852 for gpt-4o and 0.899 for nano, so part of
the two judges' low agreement is forced by how often they validate.
% interjudge_kappa.json kappa_vs_production.<judge>.all.kappa_max_given_marginals (grok 0.374, Llama
% 0.276, gpt-4o 0.852, nano 0.899); scripts/analyze_interjudge_kappa.py kappa_max (po_max = 1 - |pa - pb|)
% (m-judges-07)

The production judge agrees with itself. It scored the original responses
twice, first in a pass that showed it at most 4,000 characters of each
response and then in the full-text pass behind
Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}, which came months after the first pass for
haiku, sonnet, nano and grok, whose responses date from June, and a day after
it for Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek (Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}). On the 814
responses of at most 4,000 characters, whose judge input was byte-identical
in the two passes, the two scores agree on 0.969 of responses with a kappa of
0.932 $[0.906, 0.956]$, 25 responses change score between passes, and the
validation rate moves by $-0.9$ points $[-2.0, +0.3]$ from the first pass to
the second, whereas on the 1,189 responses over 4,000 characters,
where the first pass read a cut input, agreement is 0.829 with a kappa of
0.659 and a shift of $+7.1$ points $[+4.8, +9.4]$ toward validating, which
is the truncation effect recorded in Appendix~\ref{app-prereg} and not
judge noise.
% judge_test_retest.json (scripts/analyze_judge_test_retest.py, post hoc, zero spend, prereg block
% "Zero-spend post hoc analyses for review round 1"): pooled n 814, n_items 150, agreement 0.969
% [0.957, 0.980], kappa 0.932 [0.906, 0.956], flips 16 + 9, shift -0.9 [-2.0, +0.3]; by_arm CoT 0.924,
% NoT 0.911; not_test_retest_truncated_vs_full.pooled n 1,189, agreement 0.829, kappa 0.659 [0.617,
% 0.701], shift +7.1 [+4.8, +9.4]; draw 1 elephant_score_validation_claude-haiku-4-5_<h>.json (June
% 2026 for haiku, sonnet, nano, grok; 2026-09-21 for the open-weight three), draw 2
% elephant_rescore_validation_claude-haiku-4-5_full_<h>.json (2026-09-22); pooled.shift_draw2_minus_draw1_points
% -0.9 (appendix1-14); interval_days median 106.1 and min 1.4 (clarity loop 3, appendix1-02)

non-empty CoT and NoT responses behind Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}, of
which the Llama judge returned a parsed score on 1,997 and the grok judge on
1,998.
Rate is the judge's validation rate over all its scored responses, agreement
the share of responses scored the same as the production judge, and the
three kappa columns Cohen's kappa on all responses, on the CoT responses and
on the NoT responses, each with its 95 percent item-clustered bootstrap
interval (8,000 draws) on the line below. The last column is the largest
kappa the two judges' rates allow, not computed for the first row (dash).
The first row (haiku, retest) is the production judge against itself, its rate over

To check the judges on answers that neither prompt produced, each judge also
scored, with the same instruction, full-text input and parsing as the model
responses, the human-written
answers that the ELEPHANT release pairs with the sampled questions. For 149
of the 150 the release carries a reference label saying whether the answer
is emotionally validating, and only those 149 were scored, which checks the
judges against labels that involve neither the NoT nor the CoT responses. Of
the 149, 44, or 29.5 percent, are validating by reference. Agreement with
these labels is highest for gpt-4o and lowest for the Llama judge
(Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-ref}), and Cohen's kappa with the reference is
0.792 $[0.670, 0.892]$ for gpt-4o, 0.606 for nano, 0.587 for
haiku, 0.551 for grok and 0.478 $[0.358, 0.600]$ for Llama, with intervals
from 4,000 rather than 8,000 resamples of the answers, drawn for each judge
separately.
% scripts/judge_reference_agreement.py boot_kappa (draws 4000, seed 20260922, 2.5th and 97.5th
% percentiles, each judge resampled separately over its parsed answers); head of Appendix app (8,000
% draws unless stated); Table tab-app-judge-ref caption (clarity round 3, m-judges-09)
% prereg 17.8 amendment 2 (~l.9032-9050) and 17.8 RESULTS (~l.9083-9095);
% judge_reference_agreement.json (n_items 149, reference_rate 0.2953 = 44/149, kappa and bootstrap
% bounds); scripts/judge_reference_agreement.py keeps answers whose validation label is 0 or 1, and
% one of the 150 carries none (key_messages.md and the 17.8 amendment 2 text say "44 of 150");
% data/elephant/OEQ.csv (columns human, validation_human, sources advisorqa, relationship, reddit and
% columnist) (m-judges-08, appendix1-15)
The gpt-4o and Llama judges are the only pair whose kappa intervals do not
overlap, and the grok
judge's kappa is within 0.04 of the production judge's, so, judged by whether
the intervals overlap rather than by a paired test of the difference, the
check separates gpt-4o from the Llama judge but not the grok judge from the
production judge or nano.
% judge_reference_agreement.json kappa_lo / kappa_hi (gpt-4o 0.670-0.892, Llama 0.358-0.600,
% nano 0.455-0.746, haiku 0.443-0.717, grok 0.402-0.688; grok 0.551 vs haiku 0.587)
% no paired test of kappa differences is computed; scripts/judge_reference_agreement.boot_kappa
% bootstraps each judge separately (4,000 draws, seed 20260922) (m-judges-13)
The Llama judge calls 55.1 percent of these human answers validating, against
23.5 to 38.9 percent for the other four judges.
% judge_reference_agreement.json judge_rate (Llama 0.551; nano 0.235 to haiku 0.389)
The check is descriptive. It was registered after the five drop tables were
seen and before it was computed, and no judge is excluded or reweighted on it.
% prereg 17.8 amendment 2 (~l.9032-9049)
It has a limit we cannot remove. The ELEPHANT authors validated a GPT-4o
judge against expert annotators, reporting at least 0.83 accuracy and a kappa
of at least 0.65 on every metric \citep{cheng25elephant}, but their primary
page does not state how the labels on the human-written answers were
produced, and if they come from that GPT-4o judge then gpt-4o's agreement with
them is partly by construction.
% arXiv 2505.13995 primary page, fetched 2026-09-22 (scratchpad results_digest.md, E3 entry); the
% provenance of the human-answer labels is not stated there
The check also cannot show which judge scores the model answers correctly,
least of all the NoT responses on which the two blocs of judges part.
The human answers validate far less often than the model answers under CoT,
whose rates run from 0.54 to 0.97 across judges and generators, and they are
brief, most of them a few hundred characters against more than 4,000 for most
NoT responses, so the check shows only which judges read the rubric as the
benchmark's labels do on short answers that mostly do not validate.
% Table tab-app-judge-rates rate_a (0.54 nano judge on haiku CoT, 0.97 Llama and grok judges on
% DeepSeek CoT, judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json); blocs and NoT kappas, interjudge_kappa.json
% (m-judges-08)
% the because-clause removed: NoT rates under the production, gpt-4o and nano judges run 0.05 to 0.79
% (Table tab-app-judge-rates rate_b) and bracket the reference rate 0.295, so length, not the base rate,
% keeps the check off the NoT answers (clarity round 3, m-judges-06)
% human answers of the 149 labelled items: median 328 characters, quartiles 193 and 632, none over 4,000
% (data/elephant/OEQ.csv human column through scripts/load_elephant.py, seed-44 sample, labels 0 or 1 as in
% scripts/judge_reference_agreement.py; recomputed 2026-09-24 at zero spend); NoT responses over 4,000
% characters 821 of 983 (per-generator counts of the prereg CORRECTION to 17.2 and 17.6, ~l.8688-8693,
% summed) (clarity loop 2, m-judges-04)
Read with these limits, the judge that agrees most with the benchmark's labels
finds the reduction on six of seven generators, all but sonnet, and the Llama
judge, which agrees least, finds it on one.
% judge_reference_agreement.json kappa (gpt-4o highest, Llama lowest); judge_panel_full_oeq_*.json
% counts 6 (gpt-4o) and 1 (Llama)
