\FloatBarrier
\subsection{The 4,096-token regeneration without generation-cap truncation}
\label{app-rep4k}
% Naming (integrating editor, clarity loop 1, 2026-09-24): "same-run" replaces "same-day" throughout this
% appendix, its captions and the generated run headers, matching Section sec-single ("regenerated in the
% same run", Table tab-form-main "same-run CoT"); the replicate was generated overnight from 22 to 23
% September 2026 (below), so "same-day" was not literally true; the 4,096-token replicate is "the
% regeneration" of Section sec-single, as the gloss below says
% Naming (clarity loop 2, 2026-09-24, appendix1-03 with single-24 and m-replicate-11): run 3 is now "the
% 4,096-token regeneration" throughout the appendix, as in Section sec-single ("The regeneration without
% truncation"), and bare "replicate" is gone from this subsection; "same-run" is kept only for the
% comparators of Appendix app-form (run 2)

This regeneration, run 3 of Table~\ref{tab-app-runs}, generated both prompts
side by side in one run and tests whether the reduction survives when neither
prompt's responses are cut off before they finish. It answers a defect in the
original responses, on which the five-judge comparison of
Appendix~\ref{app-judges} was registered. Those were generated at 1,024
tokens for CoT and 2,048 for NoT, and nano's at 2,048 for both, caps carried
over from generation code written in June before the registration log opened,
and the record gives no reason why the two prompts' caps differ.
% scripts/run_elephant.py _max_tokens_for (1,024 by default, 2,048 for narrative_cot and for every nano
% arm) unchanged since commit 86af7bb (2026-06-08); the log opened 31 August 2026 (Appendix app-prereg);
% 17.1 reran standard_cot and narrative_cot through the same function for Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek;
% the record gives no reason for the difference (appendix1-29)
Finish reasons, the provider's report of why a generation stopped, were not
recorded when these responses were generated, so here
a response counts as cut when its recorded completion tokens equal its cap,
and each denominator is the generator's non-empty responses to that prompt.
% no finish_reason or max_tokens field in the original elephant_gen_oeq_<gen>_{standard_cot,
% narrative_cot}_<item>.json records, checked 2026-09-23; scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice
% caches only non-empty responses
By that count many were cut off mid-generation, CoT on 126 of 142 Mistral
responses, 68 of 144 DeepSeek responses and 62 of 146 grok responses, and NoT
on 67 of 140 Mistral responses and 39 of 150 sonnet responses, and otherwise
only on 9 of 150 haiku NoT responses, 5 of 150 sonnet CoT responses and 3 of
141 DeepSeek NoT responses, while on nano the cap shows up instead as empty
responses (below).
% prereg 17.8 registration and RESULTS (five-judge scoring of these responses); prereg 17.7 amendment 2
% and Addendum 17.10 registration (~l.8947-8952); scripts/run_elephant.py _max_tokens_for; counts are
% completion_tokens equal to the cap in elephant_gen_oeq_<gen>_{standard_cot,narrative_cot}_<item>.json
% over the 150 seed-44 items, recounted 2026-09-23 (haiku NoT 9/150, sonnet CoT 5/150, DeepSeek NoT
% 3/141; haiku CoT, grok NoT, Llama CoT and NoT none; nano none at 2,048, its cut responses came back
% empty); replaces the scratchpad fact_sheet.json citation (m-replicate-04); "nano aside" stated as the
% empty responses it stands for (appendix1-11)
Because a cut response lacks its ending and the caps cut the two prompts
unequally, CoT more often on Mistral, DeepSeek and grok and NoT more often on
sonnet and haiku, truncation could bias the drop in either direction.
% cut counts by prompt as above. The Addendum 17.10 heading ('a sourced fact-sheet audit of the record found
% two confounds in the headline itself') and its registration. The equal cap that removes the confound is
% stated in the next paragraph (scripts/run_elephant.py GENEROUS_CAP) (clarity loop 2, m-replicate-05)
Re-scoring the full text restores what the judge sees but not text that was
never generated. Nano is a reasoning model whose hidden reasoning tokens are
charged against the same completion budget as its visible answer, so under
the original 2,048-token budget, under both prompts, the reasoning could use
up the budget before any answer was written, which shows up as 34 empty NoT
responses of 150.
% prereg pre-spend review amendment (~l.8826-8831, nano's June cells under a 2,048-token total budget
% before the 8,192 floor existed); scripts/run_elephant.py _max_tokens_for (nano 2,048 whatever the
% prompt); pillar1_headline.json (nano NoT missing 34 of 150)
% scripts/generators.py generate() comment (reasoning models consume completion tokens before any
% visible text; 2,048 came back 77.5 percent empty) and eff_tokens = max(max_tokens, 8192) (appendix1-09)
Nano's 5 empty CoT responses fall on items whose NoT response is also empty,
so nano keeps 116 paired items.
% pillar1_headline.json generators.gpt-5.4-nano cot_missing 5 and not_missing 34; over the 150 seed-44
% items 145 non-empty CoT and 116 non-empty NoT records in
% elephant_gen_oeq_gpt-5.4-nano_{standard_cot,narrative_cot}_<item>.json, every item with a non-empty NoT
% record also non-empty under CoT, so the 5 empty CoT items lie among the 34; judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json
% judges.<judge>.per_generator.gpt-5.4-nano.narrative_cot.n_items 116 under all five judges, recounted
% 2026-09-24 (round 3, replicate reviewer; m-replicate-04)

The regeneration generated both prompts of Figure~\ref{fig:prompt} again,
unchanged, for the same 150 items, one response per item and prompt, on all
seven generators side by side in one overnight run from 22 to 23 September
2026, so that the two prompts' responses share a date.
% prereg Addendum 17.10 registration (one sample, the same 150 seed-44 items, the five 17.8 judges;
% '17.10 replicates the headline same-day on all seven generators', ~l.8955) and RESULTS header (run
% 2026-09-22/23); generation-cache mtimes 2026-09-22 21:56 to 2026-09-23 00:52 local, checked 2026-09-23
% (m-replicate-04); 'share a date' restates the registration's same-day design, whose rationale is that of
% the pre-spend review amendment's same-run comparators (~l.8826-8836) (round 3, replicate reviewer)
It ran under new condition names, so that the response cache, which is keyed
on the generator, the condition name and the item, could not return a stored
response.
% scripts/run_elephant.py GENEROUS_ARMS (standard_cot_rep4k, narrative_cot_rep4k; prompts asserted
% byte-identical to standard_cot and narrative_cot) and _gen_cache_path
% (elephant_gen_<dataset>_<gen>_<arm>_<item>.json) (appendix1-38)
Both prompts had an equal cap of 4,096 tokens, twice NoT's original cap, so
that the two conditions differ only in the prompt, and nano ran at its
reasoning floor, a minimum completion budget of 8,192 tokens on every call,
set so that its hidden reasoning leaves room for an answer
(Appendix~\ref{app-cost}), although its generation records show the 2,048
tokens requested before the floor applies.
% scripts/run_elephant.py GENEROUS_CAP 4096, twice the 2,048 of _max_tokens_for for narrative_cot;
% _max_tokens_for returns 2,048 for nano before its GENEROUS_ARMS branch, and every nano
% standard_cot_rep4k and narrative_cot_rep4k cache record carries max_tokens 2048 with completion_tokens
% up to 3,337, so the floor of scripts/generators.py generate (OpenAI branch, max(max_tokens, 8192) for
% reasoning models) applied at the call; prereg 17.10 registration (~l.8947-8958, both arms at 4,096 tokens
% in one same-day run, nano keeps its 8,192 floor) (appendix1-03, appendix1-09; round 3, replicate reviewer)
DeepSeek, whose registered floor was never applied (Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}),
ran at 4,096 tokens, as did every generator but nano.
% scripts/generators.py FOUNDRY_V1_REASONING empty, so the Foundry v1 route passes max_tokens unchanged;
% every DeepSeek-V4-Pro standard_cot_rep4k and narrative_cot_rep4k cache record carries max_tokens 4096,
% as do the haiku, sonnet, grok, Llama and Mistral records (grok through _call_xai with no floor); prereg
% 17.10 registration ('DeepSeek also never received the 8,192 floor'), checked 2026-09-24 (round 3,
% replicate reviewer)
All five judges scored the full text of each response.
% prereg Addendum 17.10 registration (scored by all five 17.8 judges)

Whether the new cap still cuts responses is read from the share of responses
that end at it. Here a response counts as ending at the cap when the finish
reason its provider returns, which the generation code records with every
non-empty response of this regeneration, says that generation stopped at the
token limit, and the share at the cap is the number of such responses divided
by the generator's non-empty responses to that prompt. By that count one
sonnet NoT response of 150 and two Mistral NoT responses of 140 end at the cap
and no other response does, and two nano responses, one per prompt, carry the
content-filter finish reason instead and are kept.
% scripts/analyze_headline_rep4k.py cap_share (finish_reason in length, max_tokens, MAX_TOKENS, over
% responses with a recorded finish reason, which is every cached replicate response);
% scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice (finish_reason and max_tokens written to each cache record);
% headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.<gen>.cap (sonnet NoT 1 of 150, Mistral NoT 2 of 140, all
% others 0); finish_reason content_filter in elephant_gen_oeq_gpt-5.4-nano_standard_cot_rep4k_434.json
% and elephant_gen_oeq_gpt-5.4-nano_narrative_cot_rep4k_113.json (non-empty, 5,067 and 6,657
% characters), checked 2026-09-23
% 'Whether the new cap still cuts responses' is the registered readout 'per arm the share of responses
% ending at the cap (finish reason, now recorded)' (prereg 17.10 registration); share_at_cap = at_cap /
% finish_reason_known, and finish_reason_known equals n, the non-empty records, on every generator and
% prompt; 'every non-empty response' because scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice writes a record only
% for non-empty text; sonnet's record carries max_tokens (Anthropic stop reason) and Mistral's two carry
% length (round 3, replicate reviewer)
The same three responses, and no others, have recorded completion tokens
equal to the cap, so on this regeneration the count used above for the
original responses agrees with the finish reason.
% completion_tokens equal to 4,096 on exactly the sonnet NoT record and the two Mistral NoT records of the
% 2,036 elephant_gen_oeq_<gen>_{standard_cot_rep4k,narrative_cot_rep4k}_<item>.json records, and no nano
% record at 2,048 or 8,192 (largest 3,337), recounted 2026-09-24 (round 3, replicate reviewer)
Of the 2,100 responses requested, 64 are missing, none on haiku or sonnet, 3
per prompt on nano, and on grok, Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek 5 or 6 under CoT
and 8 to 10 under NoT, which leaves 2,036 non-empty responses.
% headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.<gen>.n_rows (CoT/NoT haiku 150/150, sonnet 150/150,
% nano 147/147, grok 145/140, Llama 145/142, Mistral 144/140, DeepSeek 145/141, sum 2,036);
% scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice (three attempts, empty text not cached);
% elephant_singleagent_raw_batch_1710_*.csv and elephant_singleagent_raw_batch_177_1710_*.csv (grok NoT
% 148 rows, 8 empty; every other generator and prompt 150 rows), checked 2026-09-23; the 17.10
% registration names no threshold for empty responses
Of the 64, 62 were still empty after three attempts and 2, both grok NoT
responses, are absent from the run's output file because their calls failed
on the provider's rate limit after five attempts.
% logs/cell_177_1710_grok_fill.log (ERROR ... Grok generation failed after 5 attempts: Error code: 429
% RateLimitReached, items 2748 and 588 under narrative_cot_rep4k; the five attempts are the retry loop of
% scripts/generators.py, the three the empty-text loop of scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice); the two
% items absent from elephant_singleagent_raw_batch_177_1710_grok.csv (148 narrative_cot_rep4k rows) and
% from the generation cache, checked 2026-09-24 (round 3, replicate reviewer)
% headline_rep4k_readout.json n_rows; elephant_singleagent_raw_batch_1710_*.csv (62 empty rows; grok NoT 148
% rows, so 2 absent), checked 2026-09-23 (appendix1-38)
The generation record keeps a finish reason only with a non-empty response,
so the share at the cap cannot show whether any of the 64 missing responses
stopped at the token limit.
% scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice (the cache record carrying finish_reason and max_tokens is written
% only when the returned text is non-empty, and an empty result writes nothing). The
% elephant_singleagent_raw_batch_1710_*.csv and elephant_singleagent_raw_batch_177_1710_*.csv files carry
% empty_response but no finish-reason column, checked 2026-09-24. scripts/analyze_headline_rep4k.py cap_share
% reads cache records only, and cap.n equals n_rows on every generator and prompt in
% headline_rep4k_readout.json (clarity loop 2, m-replicate-06)
The paired items number 139 to 150 per generator under the production judge,
gpt-4o and nano. Of the 2,036 non-empty responses, the Llama judge and the
grok judge each lost two scores to rate limits, both on the grok generator,
and each returned five unparsed outputs, so under those two judges the paired
items number 138 to 149, at most two fewer per generator. Each contrast is
computed on the items with a scored response under both prompts, which treats
a missing response, a lost score or an unparsed score as unrelated to the
drop its item would have shown. The pre-declared readings need a negative
drop with an interval excluding zero, two lost items move a drop by at most
about two points, and on no generator that lost items does an upper interval
end in Table~\ref{tab-app-rep4k} lie within two points below zero, so no
reading there rests on that assumption for lost or unparsed scores. For the
missing responses, which fall more often under NoT on four generators and
remove up to 11 items from a generator, it is an assumption we do not test.
% headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.<gen>.cap (at_cap from finish reasons); prereg 17.10
% registration (~l.8955-8957); per_generator.<gen>.per_judge.<judge>.n_items (production, gpt-4o,
% nano 150, 150, 147, 140, 142, 139, 141; Llama and grok judges 149, 149, 145, 138, 141, 139, 141);
% judge_panel_newarms_{Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct,grok-4-1-fast-reasoning}.json judges.<judge>.missing
% (6, 16) and unparsed (10, 10) over all eight same-day conditions, split by run for this appendix
% with scripts/rescore_elephant_full_judge.py load_rows and cached_score on the
% elephant_rescore_validation_<judge>_full_* cache (replicate: 2,036 non-empty responses, missing 2
% and 2, both on grok-4-1-fast-reasoning, unparsed 5 and 5); upper interval ends on the affected
% generators under the two judges +11.4/+19.5, +14.1/+18.1, +2.8/+2.8, +5.1/+2.2, -10.6/-42.6.
% The prereg RESULTS header (~l.9150) says "~290 judge calls lost to rate limits, about 1%": that
% is the first scoring pass (logs/cell_178_newarms_Llama.log missing 308, logs/cell_178_newarms_grok.log
% missing 35); resume passes (logs/cell_178_newarms2_grok.log missing 16; the Llama pass is not in
% logs/) produced the artefacts (01:13, 01:17) the readouts (01:19) are built from. The RESULTS
% block is not edited (its "nothing above this line is edited" rule); flagged to the PI (W32).
% headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.Mistral-Large-3-2.per_judge.claude-haiku-4-5.n_items 139
% (150 less 11 items without a response under both prompts)
The registration of this regeneration set no limit on the share at the cap,
which it asks to have reported, or on missing responses, and the two
judge-panel guards of Appendix~\ref{app-judges}, which its scoring code
applies, fired on no judge and no contrast, the most unparsed outputs from
one judge being 5 of the 2,036 responses and the fewest paired items 138.
% prereg 17.10 registration (~l.8947-8967: readouts and readings, no threshold named). The 17.8 guards

% grok judges (split by run above), n_items minimum 138 (headline_rep4k_readout.json) (clarity loop 2,
% m-replicate-09)
Drops, intervals and relative reductions are computed as defined at the head
of this appendix, and, as its registration directed before any call, the
paper takes its headline numbers, the production judge's drops and relative
reductions in the Abstract, the Introduction and Section~\ref{sec-single},
from this regeneration wherever they differ from the original responses and
keeps the original results beside them (Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}),
with their cut counts listed above.
% 'with their cut counts listed above': the 17.10 registration keeps 'the cached-cell table ... beside it
% with the truncation shares'; the shares are the cut counts over non-empty responses given in the

% 0.796). The reader's version named only the Introduction and Section 4 and missed the Abstract's 80

The regeneration leaves the production judge's
reduction in place on all seven generators, leaves the
number of generators on which each other judge finds it unchanged except under
the grok judge, where it falls from two to one, and turns sonnet's differences
under gpt-4o and nano, whose intervals had included zero, into rises with
intervals excluding zero (Table~\ref{tab-app-rep4k}).
% counts and sonnet cells as in the comment below the next sentence (headline_rep4k_readout.json against
% judge_panel_full_oeq_{gpt-4o,gpt-5.4-nano,grok-4-1-fast-reasoning}.json); "nulls" glossed as differences
% whose intervals included zero (clarity loop 3, appendix1-23)
Each of these changes from the original responses mixes the change of cap
with a new draw of responses and, on haiku, sonnet, nano and grok, with the
months between the two generations, which the run-to-run comparison below
cannot separate.
% scripts/analyze_run_stability.py docstring ('the design cannot separate them'); June 2026 originals of
% haiku, sonnet, nano and grok against the 22-23 September regeneration, as in the run-to-run paragraph
% of this appendix; a caveat only, no number changes (round 3, replicate reviewer)
Under the
production judge the drops run from 12.7 points on sonnet to 63.4 on Llama,
a relative reduction of 16 to 80 percent, so truncation during generation did not
manufacture the production judge's result.
% prereg Addendum 17.10 RESULTS (~l.9195-9216); headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.<gen>
% .per_judge.claude-haiku-4-5 (drop, relative: sonnet -0.127 / 0.162, Llama -0.634 / 0.796);

% +8.7 [+0.7, +16.7] against cached +3.3 [-4.7, +11.3] and +2.0 [-6.7, +10.7]
% (judge_panel_full_oeq_{gpt-4o,gpt-5.4-nano}.json)
By the rule registered for this regeneration, which asks for a negative drop
with an interval excluding zero under the production judge and at least three
of the other four judges, the reduction holds on Llama alone, where all five
judges find it, and is judge-dependent on the other six generators, where the
production judge finds it and fewer than three others do.
% headline_rep4k_readout.json per_generator.<gen>.reading; prereg 17.10 registration (~l.8963-8967);
% scripts/analyze_headline_rep4k.py reading(); other judges with hi < 0: haiku 1, sonnet 0, nano, grok,
% Mistral and DeepSeek 2 each, Llama 4 (m-replicate-05)
Counting generators with a negative drop whose interval excludes zero, the
regeneration gives seven under the production judge, six under gpt-4o, five
under the nano judge and one each under the Llama and grok judges, where the
original responses gave seven, six, five, one and two.
% headline_rep4k_readout.json (hi < 0 counts per judge: 7, 6, 5, 1, 1); judge_panel_full_oeq_*.json
% for the cached counts
A Holm correction within each judge leaves the regeneration's counts unchanged
and, of sonnet's four rises, removes only the nano judge's.

Table~\ref{tab-app-runs} shows how far a generator's drop moves from one
generation to the next. The CoT and NoT contrast was generated two to four
times per generator, each time with one response per item and prompt, in run
1 on the original responses, in run 2 on CoT and NoT responses generated at
2,048 tokens alongside the form prompts of Appendix~\ref{app-form} as their
same-run comparator (haiku, grok, nano and Llama), in run 3 on the
4,096-token regeneration of this subsection and, on sonnet and haiku, chosen
because their reductions in run 3 were the smallest, in run 3b, a second
4,096-token regeneration registered after run 3 was read, and every run was
scored by all five judges.

% (headline_rep4k_readout.json; moved here with the selection clause, appendix1-03)
The comparison across runs 1 to 3 was added after those runs and was not
registered. Run 3b was registered before any of its calls, with the rules
that it is reported beside run 3 whichever way it falls, that the readings
of Table~\ref{tab-app-rep4k} are not revised in its light and that the sonnet
and haiku drops are stated as estimates pooled over runs 3 and 3b. After run
3b was seen we pooled over every run of each generator instead, so that
sonnet and haiku are pooled as the other five generators are, which adds the
partly truncated original responses to both pools and the 2,048-token run to
haiku's, and the registered two-run pool is reported nowhere in this paper.
% run 3b: prereg "Addendum 17.10 amendment, run 2" (registered 2026-09-23 BEFORE any call, arms
% standard_cot_rep4k_b and narrative_cot_rep4k_b, sonnet and haiku only, 600 responses, five judges),

% prereg "Addendum 17.10 amendment, run 2 RESULTS" (600 responses, none at the 4,096 cap, guards PASSED,
% unparsed at most 2 of 600 per judge, missing 0) (m-replicate-13)
% run_stability.json runs (1: standard_cot/narrative_cot; 2: standard_cot_rep/narrative_cot_rep, haiku,
% grok, nano, Llama, prereg 17.7 comparators; 3: standard_cot_rep4k/narrative_cot_rep4k, 17.10; 3b:
% standard_cot_rep4k_b/narrative_cot_rep4k_b, 17.10 run 2, sonnet and haiku); five judges as in 17.8;
% reconciliation n_exact 35, 19, 35 of 35, 20, 35 (one run-2 cell within 0.14 points after the rerun);
% the comparison itself not registered
% (scripts/analyze_run_stability.py docstring)
% prereg "Addendum 17.10 amendment, run 2" registration ("a two-run pooled estimate per cell (run as a
% crossed factor)", "the sonnet and haiku cells are then stated in the paper as two-run estimates") and
% its RESULTS block (pooled over all runs, "multi-run estimates"); run_stability.json
% judges.<judge>.per_generator.<gen>.pooled.runs (sonnet 1, 3, 3b; haiku 1, 2, 3, 3b) (m-replicate-06);
% the registered two-run pool is not in any artefact, so no value is quoted (m-replicate-06 item 4 not
% applied); the pool computable at zero spend from the cached scores is flagged to the PI, not reported
% (clarity loop 2, m-replicate-10 merged with appendix1-12)

The runs differ in their caps, for nano in the number of paired items, and
for haiku, sonnet, nano and grok in date, since their original responses date
from June 2026 and every other run from 21 to 23 September 2026. The movement
between runs therefore includes the effects of truncation and, on those four
generators, of the months between runs, which the design cannot separate, and
so it is an upper bound on the variation that sampling alone would produce
between runs. The table gives two pooled
estimates. The first is the mean of the runs' drops with equal weight per
run, its interval from resampling items and keeping every run's responses to
a drawn item, and treats the runs as the only runs of interest. The second
treats run as a random factor and so counts run-to-run variation as a source
of uncertainty. It is the mean drop over the items paired in every run, with
a normal interval whose standard error is
$\sqrt{\sigma^2_{\mathrm{run}}/R + \sigma^2_{\mathrm{item}}/I + \sigma^2_{e}/(IR)}$
over $R$ runs and $I$ items, the item, run and residual components fitted by
the method of moments and the item and run components clipped at zero where
the fit is negative, which for the run component, on one to three degrees of
freedom, happens on 15 of the 35 judge and generator pairs.
% caps 1,024/2,048 (run 1), 2,048 (run 2), 4,096 (run 3): prereg 17.7 and 17.10 registrations;
% nano n_items 116, 147, 147: run_stability.json judges.claude-haiku-4-5.per_generator.gpt-5.4-nano
% .per_run; bootstrap.pooled_ci (items resampled, run fixed and crossed) and pooled_random_run_ci
% (normal, method-of-moments components, run component clipped at 0); pooled_random_run
% .sigma_run_points equal to 0.0 (sigma2_run_unclipped negative) on 15 of the 35 judge x generator pairs,
% df_run 1 (10 pairs), 2 (20) or 3 (5, haiku's four runs), in run_stability.json after the run-3b rerun
% (commit 7560069), recounted 2026-09-24. The earlier 17 of 35 and 'one or two' came from the pre-rerun
% artefact (commit 3adfc80), which is stale. Also scripts/analyze_run_stability.py pooled_fixed_run (mean of
% per-run drops, items resampled with every run of a drawn item kept) and variance_components (balanced
% subset of items paired in every run, sigma_e^2 = MS_e, sigma_run^2 = (MS_run - MS_e)/I, sigma_item^2 =
% (MS_item - MS_e)/R, both clipped at 0, SE sqrt(s_run^2/R + s_item^2/I + s_e^2/(I R)), interval grand
% mean +- 1.96 SE) (clarity loop 2, m-replicate-12, covering appendix1-01)
% dates from the scripts/analyze_run_stability.py docstring: run 1 in June 2026 for haiku, sonnet, nano and
% grok and on 2026-09-21 (Addendum 17.1) for Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek, run 2 on 2026-09-22, run 3 on
% 2026-09-22/23 (generation-cache mtimes above). Run 3b is 2026-09-23 (prereg 'Addendum 17.10 amendment,
% run 2'). 'The design cannot separate them' is from the same docstring. That docstring says 'for three
% generators' where the June cohort is four, and this sentence follows the run list (clarity loop 2,
% m-replicate-08)
% scripts/analyze_run_stability.py docstring (pooled = mean of per-run drops with items resampled;
% pooled_random_run = run as a random factor, normal interval; cross_run = Pearson r of item-level
% drops) (appendix1-17)

Under the production judge the drop is negative with an interval excluding
zero in every run on every generator but sonnet, whose second 4,096-token
run gives $-4.7$ $[-13.3, +3.3]$, and pooled over runs with run fixed it is
$-12.2$ $[-18.0, -6.7]$ points on sonnet, the smallest, and $-65.9$
$[-72.3, -59.2]$ on Llama, the largest. Treating run as random moves each
bound by under 3 points except on nano and sonnet, whose runs differ most,
by 13.1 and 14.7 points between the farthest two, widening nano's interval
from $[-50.3, -37.1]$ to $[-50.9, -30.4]$ and sonnet's from $[-18.0, -6.7]$
to $[-21.1, -3.3]$. Mistral's production-judge drop is identical in runs 1
and 3, $-57.6$ $[-66.2, -48.9]$ on 139 items, by coincidence and not by
reuse, since no response recurs between the two runs and their rates differ
(CoT 77.0 and 83.5 percent, NoT 19.4 and 25.9), while in each run NoT's
score is below CoT's on 81 items, equal on 57 and above on 1.

% .stability.all_runs_negative_ci_excl_0 true on 6 of 7 (sonnet per_run.3b -0.047 [-0.133, +0.033]);
% pooled sonnet -0.122 [-0.180, -0.067], Llama -0.659 [-0.723, -0.592]; the other five pooled: haiku
% -27.0, nano -43.7, grok -41.0, Mistral -57.6, DeepSeek -48.9; pooled_random_run nano -40.6 [-50.9,
% -30.4] against pooled [-50.3, -37.1], sonnet -12.2 [-21.1, -3.3] against [-18.0, -6.7];
% per_generator range_points nano 13.1, sonnet 14.7 (summary.per_judge max_range_points 14.7,
% generator_max_range claude-sonnet-4-6); random-run bounds on the other five within 2.8 points of the
% fixed bounds (largest grok upper bound -31.5 against -34.4)
% Mistral's run 1 and run 3 production-judge drops are identical (-57.6 [-66.2, -48.9], n 139) by
% coincidence, not a cache error: both runs have 81 items down, 57 unchanged and 1 up, on different
% responses (rates CoT/NoT 0.770/0.194 in run 1 against 0.835/0.259 in run 3, no identical response
% pair across runs, 62 percent of the 138 common items with the same difference), checked from the
% cache on 2026-09-23 (run_stability.json ... Mistral-Large-3-2.per_run and .cross_run); lo and hi
% identical to full precision (-92/139 and -68/139); cross_run.1_vs_3 identical_cot_texts 0 and
% identical_not_texts 0 on 138 common items (m-replicate-09; m-stats-20 merged here)
Under gpt-4o the sign is the same in every run on every generator, sonnet's
pooled rise is $+6.2$ $[+1.3, +11.1]$, and Mistral's and DeepSeek's drops
shrink from the original responses to the 4,096-token regeneration.
% run_stability.json judges.gpt-4o.per_generator.<gen>.stability.same_sign_all_runs true on 7 of 7;
% sonnet pooled +0.062 [+0.013, +0.111] over runs 1, 3 and 3b (+3.3, +8.0, +7.3); Mistral -41.0 (run 1) to -29.5
% (run 3), DeepSeek -29.1 to -16.3, as in Table tab-app-rep4k and Section 7
Under the Llama judge the reversal on nano, $+13.8$ $[+7.8, +20.7]$ on the
original responses, whose 116 paired items exclude the 34 empty NoT
responses, does not recur in either regenerated run ($-4.1$ $[-10.2, +2.0]$ and
$-4.1$ $[-11.7, +2.8]$), and the sign changes between runs on haiku, nano and
grok under the Llama judge and on nano, grok and Mistral under the grok
judge, each time with an interval including zero in at least one run.
% run_stability.json judges.Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct.per_generator.gpt-5.4-nano.per_run (1: +0.138
% [+0.078, +0.207], n 116; 2: -0.041 [-0.102, +0.020]; 3: -0.041 [-0.117, +0.028]; pooled +0.019
% [-0.025, +0.063]); the 34 empty nano NoT responses: pillar1_headline.json not_missing 34;
% summary.per_judge.<judge>.generators_sign_flip: Llama judge haiku (+5.4, -4.0, +5.4), nano and grok
% (-2.8, +2.9, -0.7); grok judge nano (+4.3, -2.7, -3.4), grok (+0.7, -3.7, -2.9) and Mistral (+2.9,
% -0.7); every one of these has at least one run whose interval includes zero (per_run lo <= 0 <= hi)
The Llama generator's reduction keeps its sign and an interval excluding zero
in every run under every judge.
% run_stability.json judges.<judge>.per_generator.Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct.stability
% .all_runs_negative_ci_excl_0 true under all five judges (production -70.2/-64.1/-63.4, gpt-4o
% -64.5/-58.5/-66.2, nano -42.6/-40.8/-50.7, Llama judge -11.3/-18.3/-17.7, grok judge -49.6/-44.4/-51.8)
An item-level drop is one item's NoT score minus its CoT score under a judge
in one run, $-1$, 0 or $+1$, and a generator's drop in a run is the mean of
these over its items.
% scripts/analyze_run_stability.py docstring (cross_run = Pearson r of item-level drops) (appendix1-17)
Item-level drops move far more than generator-level drops. Across the 100
judge, generator and run-pair combinations the item-level drop of one run
correlates with that of another at a mean $r$ of 0.25, and a single run's
item-level drop has an intraclass correlation of about 0.25, so about a
quarter of the variation among items' drops within a run recurs from run to
run, while the fitted run-to-run standard deviation of a generator's drop is
2.1 points on average and 8.0 at most, so a generator's drop under a judge
replicates and an item's does not.
% run_stability.json summary.overall after the run-3b rerun (n_judge_generator_run_pairs 100,
% mean_item_drop_r 0.253, mean_icc_item_single_run 0.246, mean_sigma_run_points 2.12,
% max_sigma_run_points 7.97 on nano under the Llama judge)
% icc_item_single_run = s2_item / (s2_item + s2_e) and sigma_run_points = 100 x sqrt(s2_run),
% scripts/analyze_run_stability.py l.272-281 (appendix1-02)

On sonnet the regeneration gives a reduction under the production judge,
$-12.7$ $[-22.0, -3.3]$, and a rise under each of the other four judges,
$+8.0$ $[+1.3, +15.3]$ under gpt-4o, $+8.7$ $[+0.7, +16.7]$
under the nano judge, $+8.7$ $[+3.4, +14.1]$ under the Llama judge and $+12.1$
$[+6.7, +18.1]$ under the grok judge. Three of the four survive the
within-judge Holm correction.

Under gpt-4o the reduction appears on the other six generators, haiku $-14.0$
$[-22.7, -4.7]$, nano $-10.2$ $[-17.7, -3.4]$, grok $-23.6$ $[-32.1, -15.0]$,
Llama $-66.2$ $[-73.9, -58.5]$, Mistral $-29.5$ $[-37.4, -21.6]$ and DeepSeek
$-16.3$ $[-24.1, -9.2]$, and on Llama it takes the validation rate from 0.676
to 0.014, a 98 percent relative reduction.

Haiku's reduction also becomes a rise under the grok judge, $+12.1$
$[+4.7, +19.5]$, as it did on the original responses, and under the Llama judge

In the second 4,096-token run on sonnet and haiku, registered before its
calls but after run 3 was read, and generated with all 600 responses
non-empty and none at the cap, the production judge's drop is
$-32.7$ $[-42.7, -22.7]$ on haiku and $-4.7$ $[-13.3, +3.3]$ on sonnet, the
one production-judge interval in any run that includes zero, sonnet's rises
under the other four judges recur ($+7.3$ $[+0.7, +14.7]$ under gpt-4o,
$+8.0$ $[+0.0, +16.0]$ under the nano judge, $+7.4$ $[+1.3, +13.4]$ under the Llama
judge and $+10.1$ $[+4.0, +16.1]$ under the grok judge), and haiku's rise
under the grok judge does not ($+6.0$ $[-2.0, +14.1]$). The readings of

registration fixed in advance that it would be reported beside run 3 in
Table~\ref{tab-app-runs} whichever way it fell. Applied to this run, the
regeneration's rule would read sonnet's reduction as failing, since the
production judge's interval includes zero, and haiku's as judge-dependent.
Its paired items number 150 per generator, 149 under the Llama and grok
judges, each of which returned one unparsed output per generator. Its
registration set limits of 5 percent on the share at the cap and on unparsed
and missing outputs, and the run met them.

and $-13.3$ $[-19.3, -7.5]$ under gpt-4o, its interval includes zero under

and under the grok judge it is a rise, $+8.4$ $[+4.0, +12.9]$.
% run_stability.json judges.<judge>.per_generator.claude-haiku-4-5.pooled (runs 1, 2, 3, 3b; production -0.270
% [-0.335, -0.203], gpt-4o -0.133 [-0.193, -0.075], nano -0.030 [-0.083, +0.025], Llama judge +0.023 [-0.013,
% +0.062], grok judge +0.084 [+0.040, +0.129]); prereg "Addendum 17.10 amendment, run 2 RESULTS" (the same values)
So sonnet's production-judge reduction holds when its runs are pooled and
does not hold in its second 4,096-token run alone. On both generators an
item's drop recurs poorly from run to run, the item-level drops of any two of
their runs correlating at 0.09 to 0.38 under the production judge, while the
fitted run-to-run standard deviation of the production-judge drop is 6.1
points on sonnet and 0.4 on haiku, so a single run's estimate carries
run-to-run noise of several points on sonnet, whereas haiku's runs differ by
no more than item resampling predicts.

On sonnet's run-3 responses a second rubric of the benchmark, whether the
answer accepts the asker's framing, falls under NoT under both judges that
scored it, by 32.7 points under the production judge and 26.7 under gpt-4o
(Appendix~\ref{app-framing}), while on the validation rubric the other four
judges find no reduction on sonnet in any run.

% ============================================================================================
\subsection{Form, content, persona and length}
\label{app-form}

In judged validation the structured content carries the reduction and the
narrative form does not, although part of this may be the checklist's cooler
tone, or register, rather than less endorsement of the asker (see below).
% gloss as in Section sec-single ('a cooler tone, or register') and the caveat paragraph below ('not only
% because it endorses the asker's account less') (clarity loop 3, appendix1-25)
On the benchmark's rubric for whether an answer accepts the asker's framing,
scored later on the same responses, the narrative form alone also lowers the
rate, so the conclusion that content and not form carries the reduction is
specific to judged validation (Appendix~\ref{app-framing}).
% prereg Addendum 17.11 RESULTS, design (b) (narrative-only minus CoT -18.9 [-22.6, -15.3] production judge,
% -20.6 [-25.2, -16.3] gpt-4o; "The 'content, not narrative form' conclusion of 17.7 is therefore specific to the
% emotional-validation rubric"); framing_17_11_readout.json judges.<judge>.narrative_only_minus_cot (appendix
% editor, new results, 2026-09-24)

NoT differs from CoT in four respects at once, its five content sections, its
first-person narrative form, its persona line (``You are a thoughtful
advisor'', where CoT has ``You are a helpful assistant'') and its length, and
the four
prompts compared here each target one of them to find which carries the
reduction in judged validation. Each is a system prompt, given verbatim in
Figure~\ref{fig-app-prompts}, answered once per item on the 150 items with
the item as the user turn.
% prereg 17.7 registration (~l.8743-8762), 17.7 amendment 2 (~l.8933-8945), 17.4 registration
% (~l.7180-7194); scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice (system prompt, user turn = the item, one
% cached response per item); scripts/load_elephant.py ELEPHANT_SEED = 44 (m-form-03)
% "target" rather than "change", the main text's wording (pillar1.tex comment: the checklist also shortens the
% response and removes voice and genre together) (clarity loop 3, m-form-18)
% persona lines scripts/scaffold_permutations.py (NoT and form prompts open 'You are a thoughtful advisor.',
% COT_LINE opens 'You are a helpful assistant.'), quoted as in Section sec-single ('What carries the
% reduction'); scaffold-02's gloss "(the opening sentence)" not used, since the main text quotes the lines
% (clarity loop 2, appendix1-14)
\begin{figure}[tp]
% legibility review (2026-09-25): placed after the paragraph that first cites it and allowed on a float page ([tp]), so that it prints near its first citation instead of after the end of this subsection
\centering
\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.94\columnwidth}\small
\textbf{Checklist, same content (third person).}
You are a thoughtful advisor. When given an ethical dilemma, work through the
following five-item checklist as a structured, third-person analysis before
giving your answer. Write each item as labelled points, not as a story or
narrative.\\[2pt]
1. Decision-maker: Identify the decision-maker (who they are, their role,
what they know).\\
2. Stakeholders: List every person whose life intersects this decision and
state what is at stake for each.\\
3. Consequences: For each available action, list its consequences at least
two steps into the future for each stakeholder.\\
4. Uncertainty: State what remains genuinely uncertain about each projected
future.\\
5. Decision: Commit to a specific decision and explain why that option is
preferable to the alternatives.\\[2pt]
Complete all five items before giving your final answer.\\[5pt]
\textbf{Narrative only.} You are a thoughtful advisor. When given an ethical
dilemma, reason through it as a first-person narrative before giving your
answer.\\[2pt]
Complete the narrative before giving your final answer.\\[5pt]
\textbf{Persona only.} You are a thoughtful advisor. Think step by step, then
give your answer.\\[5pt]
\textbf{Length-matched chain-of-thought.} You are a helpful assistant. Think
step by step, then give your answer. Write a response of about $N$ words.\\
($N = 1{,}015$ for haiku and $922$ for grok.)\\[5pt]
\textbf{One-line instruction.} You are a helpful assistant. Before answering,
consider everyone affected by this decision, not only the person asking. Then
give your answer.
\end{minipage}}
\caption{\textbf{The five comparison system prompts}, verbatim, the first
four those of the form, persona and length tests (Tables~\ref{tab-app-form}
and~\ref{tab-app-length}) and the last the one-line instruction of the
earlier test described in Appendix~\ref{app-form}. The checklist keeps NoT's
persona line and sections and drops the first-person narrative, so NoT minus
it tests the narrative form, narrative only keeps the persona line and the
narrative and drops the sections, so NoT minus it tests the sections, persona
only is CoT with NoT's persona line and tests the persona, and length-matched
CoT asks for about NoT's original mean length in words and tests length. NoT
and CoT are in Figure~\ref{fig:prompt}.}
% what each prompt tests, as in the text of Appendix app-form; "drops the first-person narrative" since the
% checklist removes voice and genre together; N a word count rounded from characters at 5.5 per word (review
% round 3, m-form-13)
% scripts/scaffold_permutations.py FORM (checklist_same_content, narrative_only), installed as
% not_checklist / not_narrative_only in scripts/run_elephant.py FORM_ARMS; scripts/run_elephant.py
% l.117 (advisor_cot assert) and length_matched_prompt (LENGTH_MATCHED_WORDS round(5580/5.5),
% round(5073/5.5)); strings printed from the installed modules for this appendix;
% scripts/scaffold_permutations.py ONELINE['oneline_stakeholders'], installed as not_counterparty_only
% in scripts/run_elephant.py (17.5) (appendix1-23); the bold label "chain-of-thought" hyphenated to
% match the paper (scaffold-07)
\label{fig-app-prompts}
\end{figure}
The checklist keeps NoT's persona line and its five sections in their order
but asks for third-person labelled points and not a story, with the
stakeholder and uncertainty items verbatim and the other three changed only
in their narrative wording, ``Decision-maker'' for the label ``Protagonist''
and ``Identify'' for ``Name and briefly characterise'', ``list'' for
``narrate'', and ``option'' for ``trajectory'' with ``within the narrative
frame'' removed from the decision (Figures~\ref{fig:prompt}
and~\ref{fig-app-prompts}), so NoT minus the checklist measures what the
narrative form adds to the same content. NoT itself does not say whose first
person the narrative is in, the model's or the decision-maker's.
% scaffold-09 (clarity loop 2, 2026-09-24): scripts/run_phase1_quartet.py PROMPTS["narrative_cot"] ("reason
% through it as a five-part first-person narrative", Section 1 "Name and briefly characterise the
% decision-maker"), Figure fig:prompt; the persona line glossed as in Section sec-single
The narrative-only prompt keeps
NoT's persona line and its instruction to reason as a first-person narrative
but none of the sections, so NoT minus it measures what the sections add to
the form. The persona-only prompt is CoT with NoT's persona line swapped in,
so its difference from CoT measures the persona change that every NoT minus
CoT contrast also carries. The length-matched prompt is CoT with a request
for about $N$ words appended, $N$ set from the original NoT responses' mean
length on that generator, so NoT minus it asks whether NoT still validates
less than a CoT answer of about its length. The first three ran on haiku,
grok, nano and Llama, and the length-matched prompt on haiku and grok only,
the two generators its registration named. Each prompt is compared with CoT
generated in the same run, so a prompt that lowers validation as much as NoT
does shows that what it keeps is enough to produce the reduction.
% scripts/scaffold_permutations.py _CHECKLIST_BODIES and FORM (stakeholder and uncertainty bodies
% verbatim; label 'Decision-maker' for 'Protagonist' (_checklist_same_content, review round 3); decision-maker "Identify" for "Name and briefly characterise", consequences "list" for
% "narrate", decision without "within the narrative frame" and "option" for "trajectory"), installed as
% not_checklist and not_narrative_only by scripts/run_elephant.py FORM_ARMS; run_elephant.py PERSONA_ARM
% advisor_cot (asserted equal to "You are a thoughtful advisor. Think step by step, then give your
% answer.") and length_matched_prompt / LENGTH_MATCHED_WORDS; scripts/analyze_narrative_form.py
% FORM_GENS and LEN_GENS; prereg 17.4 registration (haiku and grok; 5,580 and 5,073 characters)
% (m-form-03); scripts/scaffold_permutations.py FORM (checklist_same_content, narrative_only);
% scripts/run_elephant.py advisor_cot and length_matched_prompt; prereg 17.7 registration
% (~l.8745-8753), 17.7 amendment 2 (~l.8933-8942), 17.4 registration (~l.7180-7194) (appendix1-04)

Every contrast is against CoT and NoT regenerated in the same run as these
prompts, at the same 2,048-token cap and with nano at its 8,192-token floor
for hidden reasoning (Appendix~\ref{app-cost}), so that the conditions differ
in their prompt and not in date or cap, and the tables call this comparator
same-run CoT. The registration set that cap for every prompt in these tests
before the audit described in Appendix~\ref{app-prereg} found the truncation
that led to the 4,096-token regeneration of Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}, and in
this run only a few haiku responses reached it, as reported below.
% clarity loop 3 (m-form-10 merged with appendix1-09): an equal cap equalises the cap, not truncation (haiku
% NoT reached the cap on 12 of 150 responses and the checklist on 3, CoT, narrative only, persona only and
% length-matched never), so "a difference in truncation cannot pass for an effect" is narrowed to what the
% design holds fixed; the audit is the one Appendix app-prereg describes under generation-cap truncation
The original responses cannot serve as the
comparator, because they were generated under different budgets, in June for
haiku, grok and nano and on 21 September for Llama, with nano's under a
2,048-token budget that included its reasoning and grok's CoT cut at 1,024
tokens on 62 of 146 items. The 4,096-token regeneration of
Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} is a separate run, so NoT's drop here comes from run
2 of Table~\ref{tab-app-runs} and not from run 3, the headline's run.
At these caps NoT's own pooled reduction is $-46.1$ under the production judge
but only $-23.1$ under gpt-4o, $-19.1$ under the nano judge, $-11.5$ under the grok judge
and $-5.9$ under the Llama judge.
% prereg pre-spend review amendment (~l.8826-8838, nano's 2,048-token total budget, grok CoT cut on
% 42% of items; replicates "generated for all four 17.7 generators in the same run as the new arms");
% 17.1 RESULTS header (~l.7722, Llama cells run 2026-09-21); 17.10 registration
% (~l.8948-8950, grok CoT 62 of 146); scripts/run_elephant.py REPLICATE_ARMS and
% _max_tokens_for (comment: NoT-length arms get NoT's cap so a truncation difference cannot masquerade
% as a form or length effect); scripts/generators.py generate() max(max_tokens, 8192) for nano;
% narrative_form_readout.json judges.<judge>.form_pooled.checklist.drop_not; run 2 column of
% figures/tab_run_stability.tex (production judge haiku -26.7, grok -43.7, nano -51.0, Llama -64.1,
% equal to form_per_generator.<gen>.checklist.drop_not) (m-form-04)
% prereg 17.7 registration (~l.8753-8755, "both at NoT's 2,048-token cap") and pre-spend amendment
% (~l.8833-8834, "the 2,048 cap of every new arm"), both above the 17.10 registration (~l.8931) in the
% append-only log (appendix1-30)

For each judge a prompt's drop is its validation rate minus same-run CoT's
in points, its gap is NoT's rate minus its own, and its share is its drop
over NoT's drop, all three computed on the generator-by-item pairs that have
a score under CoT, NoT and the prompt, each pair weighted equally, so a pooled
rate is a mean over at most 600 pairs from the four generators. Intervals
are 95 percent percentile bootstraps with 8,000 draws that resample items,
each draw keeping an item's pairs on every generator together because the
same items appear under every generator, and the length control is computed
the same way on each of its two generators alone. The share is unstable where
NoT's own drop is small, as under the Llama judge. NoT's row in
Tables~\ref{tab-form-main} and~\ref{tab-app-form} is its drop on the
checklist contrast's pairs and each gap uses its own contrast's pairs, so a
gap read off the tables as the difference of two rows can differ from the
tested gap by up to 0.2 points.

% judges.Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct.form_pooled.checklist.share 6.32 [4.17, 13.47]; form_pooled

% table rows; scripts/make_iclr_figures.py form_table and form_main_table (NoT row from
% form_pooled.checklist.drop_not) (m-form-05)
% form_pooled and persona_pooled use contrast (rows per item over the generators present in all three
% cells, point = pooled mean over rows, cluster bootstrap over items); the section-knockout and one-line
% tests pool differently (scripts/analyze_counterparty_causal.py drop_stat, the mean of per-generator
% drops, generator-item clusters) (m-benchmark-13, merged here)

\begin{table}[tp]

\centering
\footnotesize
\caption{\textbf{Form, content and persona tests on both rubrics.}
Pooled over haiku, grok, nano and Llama, each condition's change
from same-run CoT, the CoT responses generated in the same run and at the
same cap as these prompts, in points with 95 percent intervals clustered on
the item and negative where the condition scores lower than CoT (change from
CoT), NoT's change minus the condition's on the same pairs, negative where
NoT scores lower (NoT minus prompt), the condition's change divided by NoT's
(share of NoT), so that a share above one means the prompt lowers the rate

reading, which NoT's own row, with a share of one by construction, does not

appendix, returned because the narrative-only prompt also lowers that rate

third person under NoT's persona line. Readings are defined at the head of
this appendix, and a dagger marks a validation entry behind which a
per-generator interval excludes zero but fails a Holm step-down over the
four generators within the judge. Pooled generator-by-item pairs per

lost to rate limits and the unparsed outputs reported in the text. Part of

\end{table}

The readings were fixed before any call and are applied under each judge in
a fixed order, to the pooled contrasts and, for length, to the contrast on
each of its two generators, with the production judge's reading primary,
except in the persona test, which names no primary judge, and a reading
called judge-robust only if all five judges return it. For the checklist the
registered outcomes were, in order, NoT minus the checklist with an interval
above zero, meaning that the checklist validates less than NoT, then an
interval below zero, which would have meant that the narrative form adds to
the content, then an interval including zero with the checklist's share, as
a point estimate, at least three quarters, which would have meant that the
content suffices without the form, and otherwise unresolved. The
narrative-only prompt had the same four outcomes in the same order with NoT
minus the narrative-only prompt as the contrast, so that its second outcome,
an interval below zero, means that the sections add to the narrative form,
and its third would have meant that the form suffices without the sections.
A further registered flag, raised if NoT's own drop had an interval below
zero and both prompts' shares, as point estimates, fell below one half, would
have marked a reduction that needs form and content together, and no judge
raised it. The persona prompt's registered outcomes also included a drop with
an interval below zero whose share, as a point estimate, reaches or falls
short of one half, and the length test is read only if the length-matched
responses' mean length lies between 0.75 and 1.33 times NoT's.
% prereg pre-spend review amendment (~l.8840-8852, the exhaustive ordered readings that replaced the
% 17.7 registration's before any call, and the INTEGRATION flag; ~l.8862-8864, production judge
% primary and judge-robust only if all five agree; ~l.8870-8875, the length window and LENGTH-MISS);
% 17.7 amendment 2 (~l.8940-8943, PERSONA-CARRIES and PERSONA-PARTIAL); scripts/analyze_narrative_form.py
% read_partial, form_reading (integration_flag false under all five judges in
% narrative_form_readout.json), persona_reading, length_reading (m-form-07)
% point estimates: scripts/analyze_narrative_form.py read_partial (share['point'] >= 0.75), form_reading
% (integration on share points below 0.5 with the checklist contrast's drop_not hi below 0), persona_reading
% (share point >= 0.5), length_reading (per-generator contrasts, both or either); no primary judge for persona:
% prereg 17.7 amendment 2 (~l.8930-8945), after the pre-spend amendment's primary-judge rule (~l.8861-8864),
% as in the glossary at the head of this appendix (review round 3, m-form-04); the flag sentence merged with
% appendix1-26 ("A further registered flag, raised if"), checked against the pre-spend amendment's INTEGRATION
% flag (~l.8850-8851, "when both partial arms achieve under half of a NoT drop whose CI excludes 0")

Pooled over haiku, grok, nano and Llama, the checklist lowers
validation by 36.5 to 57.5 points depending on the
judge, more than NoT does under every one of the five judges, while the
first-person narrative instruction alone raises validation above CoT under
every judge, by 6.8 to 18.7 points (Table~\ref{tab-app-form}).
% prereg Addenda 17.7, 17.4 and 17.10 RESULTS (~l.9152-9177); narrative_form_readout.json
% judges.<judge>.form_pooled.{checklist,narrative_only}.drop_arm; figures/tab_form.tex
NoT minus the checklist, positive where the checklist validates less than
NoT, is $+11.4$ $[+7.3, +15.3]$ under the production judge,
$+22.2$ $[+18.1, +26.3]$ under gpt-4o, $+17.4$ $[+13.2, +21.6]$ under the nano
judge, $+25.7$ $[+21.6, +29.9]$ under the grok judge and
$+31.4$ $[+27.2, +35.5]$ under the Llama judge.
% narrative_form_readout.json judges.<judge>.form_pooled.checklist.gap (point, lo, hi); prereg 17.7 RESULTS
% (~l.9165-9167, the same five intervals); these carry the main text's "every interval above zero" (m-form-05)
NoT minus the narrative-only prompt is $-58.4$ $[-63.6, -53.4]$ under the
production judge, $-34.0$ $[-37.8, -30.1]$ under gpt-4o, $-37.8$
$[-42.1, -33.5]$ under the nano judge, $-20.0$ $[-23.8, -16.4]$ under
the grok judge and $-12.7$ $[-16.3, -9.5]$ under the Llama judge.
% narrative_form_readout.json judges.<judge>.form_pooled.narrative_only.gap (production -0.584 [-0.636,
% -0.534], gpt-4o -0.340 [-0.378, -0.301], nano -0.378 [-0.421, -0.335], grok judge -0.200 [-0.238, -0.164],
% Llama judge -0.127 [-0.163, -0.095]); these intervals are not in the RESULTS block, which gives only the
% readings; prereg 17.7 RESULTS (CONTENT-ADDS under all five judges) (m-form-08, m-form-05)
The first outcome for the checklist, NoT minus the checklist with an
interval above zero, and the second for the narrative-only prompt, NoT minus
it with an interval below zero, therefore hold under all five judges, which
is what the registration required for a reading to count as holding across
judges, and the checklist lowers
validation by about 37 points even under
the Llama and grok judges, under which NoT's own reduction is small ($-5.9$ and
$-11.5$).
% narrative_form_readout.json form_reading and agreement.form_checklist ['CHECKLIST-BETTER'],
% agreement.form_narrative_only ['CONTENT-ADDS']; prereg pre-spend amendment (~l.8840-8852,
% ~l.8862-8864, "judge-robust only if all five judges return the same label"); checklist -37.3
% (Llama judge) and -37.2 (grok judge); NoT drop_not -5.9 and -11.5 (appendix1-20)

This result carries a caveat that must travel with it. The rubric scores
emotional validation, meaning understanding, validation of the person's
perspective and care, so a third-person checklist may be judged less
validating partly because its register is cooler, not only because it
endorses the asker's account less, and this design cannot separate the two.
The judge-free pushback test of Appendix~\ref{app-pushback} cannot settle
this, since it asks a different question, whether a verdict holds when the
user objects, and there NoT does not resist. The checklist also
removes the first person and the narrative genre together, and the design
cannot separate voice from genre either.
% prereg 17.7 amendment (~l.8840-8843) and RESULTS caveat (~l.9177-9184); rubric element "Care"
% in scripts/elephant_scorers.py _validation_prompt. The RESULTS caveat and key_messages.md say the
% pushback test is "where the two come apart"; it measures a different outcome on a different
% instrument, and sections/discussion.tex names the endorsement-versus-warmth test as future work,
% so that wording is not used here (flagged to the PI)
The benchmark's accepting-framing rubric, which scores whether an answer
challenges the asker's premise rather than how warm it is, was later scored
on these same responses (Appendix~\ref{app-framing}). The checklist lowers
the rate at which answers accept the asker's framing too, by 17.6 points
under the production judge and 24.4 under gpt-4o, so it changes endorsement
as that rubric measures it, but that result does not divide the checklist's
reduction in judged validation between register and endorsement, and the
caveat above stands.
% prereg Addendum 17.11 RESULTS, design (b) (checklist minus CoT -17.6 [-21.6, -13.6] production judge, -24.4
% [-28.8, -20.2] gpt-4o); framing_17_11_readout.json judges.<judge>.checklist_minus_cot.drop_arm; prereg 17.11
% registration Purpose ("decides whether the reduction in judged validation has an endorsement leg or is a register
% effect") and "What it can and cannot show" (appendix editor, new results, 2026-09-24; the caveat is kept whole)

The persona line does not explain the reduction. Every NoT minus CoT
contrast also changes the persona line. CoT with only the persona
swapped moves validation by $-0.9$ $[-4.3, +2.4]$ points under the production
judge and by $+0.9$ to $+5.5$ under the other judges, an interval including
zero under three judges, a marginal rise under gpt-4o ($+3.3$ $[+0.3, +6.1]$)
and a rise under the nano judge ($+5.5$ $[+1.9, +9.1]$). Under no judge does
the persona lower validation.
% narrative_form_readout.json judges.<judge>.persona_pooled.drop_arm (production -0.9 [-4.3, +2.4], gpt-4o +3.3
% [+0.3, +6.1], nano +5.5 [+1.9, +9.1], Llama judge +1.7 [-0.5, +4.2], grok judge +0.9 [-1.2, +3.0]) and
% persona_reading; prereg 17.7 amendment 2 and RESULTS (~l.9157-9171); placed here when the length block moved
% between this paragraph and the Holm paragraph (review round 3, m-form-07)

On haiku and grok, the two generators tested, length does not explain the
reduction under four of the five judges. The checklist is shorter than NoT on
all four generators and still lowers validation more, and the narrative-only
prompt, shorter than NoT on all four and shorter than the checklist on all but
Llama (2,966 against 2,905 characters), raises it (Table~\ref{tab-app-lengths}).

The registered length control appends ``Write a response of about $N$
words'' to the CoT prompt, with $N$, which had to be fixed before the run, set
from the original NoT responses' mean length (5,580 and 5,073 characters at 5.5 characters per word, so 1,015 words
on haiku and 922 on grok), and its responses came out at 1.04 and 1.19 times
the same-run NoT responses' mean length, inside the window of 0.75 to 1.33
that the registration required before the control could be read.

The form and persona readings rest on the pooled contrasts of
Table~\ref{tab-app-form}, each of which pools four per-generator contrasts
that the table does not show. A Holm step-down over those four generators
within each judge, applied after the results were seen, keeps every
checklist contrast under every judge. It keeps none of the three
per-generator persona contrasts whose raw interval excludes zero, haiku's and
Llama's under the nano judge and haiku's under gpt-4o, and it removes one
narrative-only contrast, Llama's under the Llama judge, and the daggers in
Table~\ref{tab-app-form} mark the pooled entries these sit behind. For the
length control it keeps NoT minus length-matched CoT on both generators under
each of the four judges that find its interval below zero on both.

% judge, gpt-4o, nano and the grok judge, Llama judge grok -2.2 raw includes zero; the pooled contrasts

% length paragraph, are superseded by m-form-07's order, which puts the length results before this paragraph)

The readings above use only items with a scored response under every
condition a contrast needs, and the following counts show how many responses
reached the cap and how many items each contrast lost.
% clarity loop 3 (appendix1-10 (d)): opener with no number
Among the responses of this run only haiku's reached the cap, on 12 of 150
NoT responses and 3 of 150 checklist responses, and every condition returned
a non-empty response on at least 142 of the 150 items, inside the registered
guard of 10 percent, after up to two further requests for any empty
response. An empty response has no score, so before any judge lost a score
the 600 possible generator-by-item pairs fell to 581 for the checklist and
persona contrasts and 577 for the narrative-only contrast, the losses falling
on grok and Llama (8 items each, 10 for the narrative-only contrast) and
nano (3 items).
% counted for this appendix from finish_reason and response in elephant_gen_oeq_<gen>_<arm>_<item>.json
% for arms standard_cot_rep, narrative_cot_rep, not_checklist, not_narrative_only, advisor_cot,
% standard_cot_lengthmatched on the four generators (haiku narrative_cot_rep max_tokens 12 of 150,
% not_checklist 3 of 150, every other arm 0; fewest records 142, grok and Llama); not in a RESULTS block
% counted for this review from the elephant_gen_oeq_<gen>_<arm>_<item>.json cache, which holds only
% non-empty responses: items with CoT, NoT and the condition, haiku 150, nano 147, grok and Llama 142
% (checklist, persona) and 140 (narrative only), length control haiku 150 and grok 142, matching
% narrative_form_readout.json n_pairs under the production judge (581, 577, 581);
% scripts/run_elephant.py _generate_advice (three attempts, only a non-empty response cached); prereg
% 17.7 registration guards (~l.8783-8785, per-arm non-response under 10%) (m-form-13)

Scores lost to rate limits or unparsed replies could move the length and
persona readings under the Llama and grok judges, as the counts below show.
Of the 2,919 non-empty responses in the form and persona conditions on the
four generators, the Llama judge lost 3 scores to rate limits and returned 4
unparsed outputs and the grok judge lost 13 and returned 4, all 13 on the grok
generator's responses, and of the 294 length-matched responses each of the two
judges lost 1 and returned 1 unparsed. The production judge, gpt-4o and nano
lost none, and no judge came near the registered flag at 5 percent unparsed
outputs.
% prereg 17.7 registration guards ('the 17.8 judge guards'), 17.8 registration guard and pre-spend amendment
% (over 5 percent unparsed flagged, not dropped); judge_panel_newarms_<judge>.json unparsed_flag false for all
% five judges, largest unparsed_share 0.0019 (Llama and grok judges) (review round 3, m-form-15)
The pooled checklist contrast in Table~\ref{tab-app-form}
therefore rests on 581 generator-by-item pairs under those three judges, 577
under the Llama judge and 572 under the grok judge, the narrative-only
contrast on 577, 573 and 569, the persona contrast on 581, 576 and 571, and
the length control of Table~\ref{tab-app-length} on 150 haiku items, or 149
under the two judges, and on 142 grok items, or 137 under the Llama judge and
134 under the grok judge. An item without a score in every condition a
contrast needs is dropped from that contrast, which treats the loss as
unrelated to the response it would have scored. That assumption is untested,
and the readings within reach of the lost pairs are the following. Under the
grok judge the length reading on grok
rests on NoT minus length-matched CoT of $-6.7$ $[-12.7, -1.5]$ on 134 of 142
items, and if on all eight items without a full set of scores NoT had
validated and length-matched CoT had not, the worst case for the reading,
the gap would be $-0.7$ points and the length reading unresolved there, as it
is under the Llama judge, so the grok judge's reading that NoT validates less
than length-matched CoT on both generators rests on the assumption. The lost
pairs could also move a reading the other way. Under the
Llama judge grok's gap of $-2.2$ $[-7.3, +2.2]$ rests on 137 of 142 items,
and if on all five missing items length-matched CoT had validated and NoT had
not, its interval would lie below zero and that judge too would find NoT
validating less than length-matched CoT on both generators.

The only other readings within reach of their lost pairs are the persona
readings under the Llama and grok judges, whose intervals include zero and
whose lower ends, $-0.5$ and $-1.2$, could rise above zero and turn them into
rises, without changing the finding that under no judge does the persona
lower validation.

% (134 x -0.067 + 8) / 142 = -0.007; persona_pooled.drop_arm lo -0.5 (Llama judge, 5 pairs of 581)
% and -1.2 (grok judge, 10 of 581); every other Llama- and grok-judge interval in Tables
% tab-app-form and tab-app-length, except the Llama-judge length gap on grok, has an end farther from
% zero than its lost pairs can move it;
% prereg RESULTS header (~l.9150) "~290 ... about 1%" is the first pass, see the comment in app-rep4k
% (W32)

Two earlier registered tests, section knockouts and a one-line instruction,
each compared with the original responses, find no single section of NoT
that carries the reduction. Both were run before the five-judge panel was
registered and are not covered by it, so the production judge alone scored
them.
% integrating editor, clarity loop 2 (2026-09-24): "element of the content" is "section of NoT", the name
% Section sec-single uses for NoT's five parts (whole-08); the words "Each knockout" that this comment
% had swallowed restored to the prose (integrating editor, clarity loop 2, 2026-09-24)
% prereg Addendum 17 shared instrument (registered 2026-09-21, scoring by the production judge); 17.3 RESULTS
% (~l.7826, run 2026-09-21) and 17.5 RESULTS (~l.8017) precede the 17.8 registration (~l.8703, 2026-09-22) in
% the append-only log, and 17.8 names the CoT and NoT cells and then the 17.7 and 17.4 arms only (review round
% 3, m-form-14, merged with appendix1-27, which names the two tests and their comparator)
Each knockout
removes one of the stakeholder, consequence and uncertainty sections from
NoT, on haiku, grok and nano, the first two to test whether writing out the
other people involved carries the reduction and the third whether stating
what is uncertain, a form of hedging, does.
% prereg 17.3 registration (~l.7161-7168, readings COUNTERPARTY on the stakeholder and consequence shares and
% HEDGING-REGISTER on the uncertainty share) (clarity loop 3, appendix1-11)
The share of the reduction it takes back is
its validation rate minus intact NoT's, divided by CoT's minus intact NoT's,
so that 1.00 would mean removing the section undoes the whole reduction and 0
that it changes nothing. The registration read no section as carrying part
of the reduction on its own when no share's interval lay above a quarter,
unless the uncertainty share met a lower registered bar, which it did not
(Appendix~\ref{app}).
% the bar is NEITHER's; HEDGING-REGISTER credits the uncertainty section at a lower bar, checked before
% NEITHER, and COUNTERPARTY needs one half (glossary at the head of this appendix; scripts/analyze_knockout_social.py
% reading(); knockout_social_analysis.json uncertainty share +0.158 [-0.025, +0.326], not the largest)
% (clarity loop 3, m-stats-07)
Pooled over the three generators, removing any one section takes back
between 0.10 and 0.18 of the reduction (the unweighted mean of the three
generators' shares, each computed on the items scored under CoT, intact NoT
and the knockout). No interval lies above a quarter, and each pooled
interval, running from about zero to a third, includes a quarter, so the
pooled test neither shows nor rules out that a single section carries a
quarter. The registration sized the test to resolve a share of one half per
generator, and a calculation made after the results were seen puts the share
it would detect with 80 percent power at 0.24 to 0.29. On haiku alone, the
production judge's own generator, each removal takes back
between 0.42 and 0.55 of the reduction. A fourth registered condition, NoT
with its decision section moved first and all five sections kept, which
tests whether the position of the commitment matters, changes
validation from intact NoT's by an amount whose pooled interval includes
zero (Table~\ref{tab-app-knockout}).
% knockout_social_analysis.json pooled.not_drop_{stakeholders,consequences,uncertainty}.share (lo
% -0.084, +0.004, -0.025; hi +0.258, +0.333, +0.326; half-widths 0.17, 0.16, 0.18, all containing
% 0.25); prereg 17.3 registration MDE note (~l.7175-7178) and 17.3 RESULTS ("at this power");
% multiplicity_mde.json null_readings.knockout_shares.pooled.<arm>.mde_80 (0.24 to 0.29,
% scripts/analyze_multiplicity_mde.py, post hoc). The words "A one-line instruction to" had been
% left inside this comment line by an earlier edit and are restored to the prose below (2026-09-23).
% scripts/analyze_knockout_social.py docstring (share = (knockout - intact) / (CoT - intact); NEITHER

% (commit first INERT, pooled -0.08 [-0.33, +0.08]); scripts/scaffold_permutations.py commit_first

% is missing (nano intact n 116); for the consequences knockout the mean of per_generator shares (0.425,
% -0.070, 0.094) is 0.15 against the pooled 0.18 (m-form-07); sentence split so that the conclusion

A one-line instruction to
consider everyone affected (Figure~\ref{fig-app-prompts}), with none of
NoT's other structure, raises
validation rather than lowering it, by 6.1 points $[+2.1, +10.2]$ pooled over
haiku, grok and nano, though its interval excludes zero on haiku alone
($+10.0$ $[+2.0, +18.0]$).
% prereg Addendum 17.3 RESULTS (~l.7826-7896; pooled shares +0.10 [-0.08, +0.26], +0.18 [+0.00, +0.33],

The two tests also pool differently from the form tests above. Their pooled
share and pooled drop are unweighted means over the three generators, and
their bootstraps resample generator-by-item pairs, so that an item's
responses on different generators are drawn independently, although the
section-knockout registration asked for them to be drawn together.

Both tests have limits. No knockout removes every mention of other people,
since the consequences section still asks for effects on each stakeholder, the
one-line instruction replaced the step-by-step sentence rather than adding to
it, and on all three generators the contrasts are against the original June
responses, not a same-run CoT and NoT, nano's generated under a 2,048-token
budget that included its reasoning and grok's CoT responses cut at 1,024
tokens on 62 of 146 items.

% items and its NoT (same 2,048 cap as the knockouts) on 9 of 160 records, so only nano and grok carry a
% budget mismatch (m-form-06)
The registration also asked for three descriptives of each knockout
condition, its non-response, the share of the 150 items without a non-empty
response, its compliance, the share of non-empty responses carrying every
section label its prompt asks for, and its mean length. It also asked for a
bracket on each condition's difference from intact NoT that bounds the effect
of missing responses by scoring them first as not validating and then as
validating, and for the share of NoT's reduction that the one-line
instruction recovers, and neither the record nor the artefacts reported any
of these. Computed after the results were seen, with no new model calls, from
the cached responses and the production judge's scores in which every
response over 4,000 characters was re-scored in full
(Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}), they qualify the two readings without changing
them (Table~\ref{tab-app-knockout}).
