% Moved here from sections/pillar3.tex by the integrating editor (2026-09-23) for the page limit.
\subsection{The collective's dissent and routing against the solo model}
\label{app-collective}

Routing asks whether the collective's own dissent is worth acting on, by
sending only the debates it flags to a stronger judge, much as a model
cascade sends a cheap model's uncertain cases to a stronger model, and the
comparisons with the same model answering alone, the solo, ask whether the
collective adds anything that the solo, or routing on the solo's own sample
disagreement, a split of its three answers on fault, does not.
% purpose sentence (m-routing round 2): Section sec-related (cascades escalate a cheap model's uncertain
% cases, self-consistency the single-model baseline); prereg 16.22 R3 ~l.6719-6726 (m-routing-16)
This subsection states how each quantity of Section~\ref{sec-collective} is
computed and what each exclusion removes, and gives the readouts behind its
claims, from the paired comparison of the collective with the solo to the
evidence on what drives the dissent sensor and the routing of the solo's own
answers.
% clarity loop 1: opening and run-in headings merged from m-routing-18 and appendix2-09; every % comment
% moved with its sentence

In each debate three copies of one model under the NoT system prompt, an
advocate for the writer, an advocate for the other party and a neutral seat,
state and defend verdicts on one first-person conflict account, and a fourth
copy with no side, the moderator, proposes a verdict in a synthesis of their
final positions and, after each seat labels that synthesis, writes the
integrated verdict scored as the collective's, in 17 calls per debate. A
seat objects when its label accepts the synthesis only with a modification
or rejects it, the counter flags a debate in which two or more seats object,
the counter's lift is the share of flagged debates whose integrated verdict
is wrong minus that share among unflagged ones, and routing replaces a
flagged debate's verdict with a stronger judge's, the paragraphs below
stating each step in full. Grok with the exchange kept, the primary
condition of Table~\ref{tab-routing}, was run first, and the flag's
threshold and its split by verdict type were proposed and first read on its
debates, so the other five conditions of the table are their prospective
test (Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}).
% round 3 methods review (m-collective-05): roadmap so that 'Item sets and exclusions' can use debate, flag and lift
% before the paragraphs that define them; scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py ROLES (writer_advocate, counterparty,
% neutral_adjudicator), agent_system (NoT on seat calls, narrative_cot on every collective of Table tab-routing),
% run_deliberation (synthesis, r3 labels, integration; mod_model = moderator_model or model), objected = label in
% (ACCEPT_WITH_MODIFICATION, REJECT) l.1211 (an unparsed label is no objection); counter n_objectors >= 2 (prereg 16.1
% ~l.3042-3084); lift as in 'The flag's lift' (scripts/analyze_router_decomposition.py _lift_stat)
% clarity loop 3 (appendix2-01, merged with m-collective-05 into one paragraph): scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py
% docstring (three seats and a moderator, 17 = 3+3+3+1+3+1+3 calls), objected_r3 l.1211 (ACCEPT_WITH_MODIFICATION or
% REJECT); counter n_objectors >= 2 (prereg 16.1 ~l.3042-3084); routing scripts/verify_pillar3_headline.py load and
% routed_ok; grok with the exchange kept collected by 2026-09-01 (prereg E5 RESULTS ~l.526), before every other
% condition of Table tab-routing (exchange cut first run 2026-09-12, 16.10 RESULTS ~l.4136, extended under 16.25;
% haiku, Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek 2026-09-14 to 2026-09-22); threshold and verdict-type split proposed and first
% read on grok (prereg 16.1 2026-09-09, 16.12 and 16.15.1 2026-09-12), the other five rows their prospective test as
% in Section sec-collective and Appendix app-prereg; "primary collective" as in the Table tab-app-models caption

\paragraph{Item sets and exclusions.}
The collective's item set, the crowd-labelled set of
Appendix~\ref{app-pushback}, holds 249 Scruples accounts, 99 on which at least 90
percent of at least 50 votes found the writer alone at fault and 150 on which
they found the other party alone at fault, each posed in the third person and
with the user as the writer, two wrappers around the same account
(Appendix~\ref{app-prompts}), so that the verdict's shift toward the user can
be read (Appendix~\ref{app-seat}).
% whole-12 (clarity round 3, 2026-09-24): Section sec-single's name for the same Z3 criterion shift ("the
% verdict's shift toward the user, blaming the writer less when the writer is the user than in the third
% person"); Appendix app-seat and the Z3 paragraph already use it and state that the collective's version is
% measured as a share on crowd-at-fault items, not by c; "the crowd-labelled set of Appendix app-pushback", which
% introduces the name (clarity loop 3, appendix1-12)
Of the 32,766 accounts in the Scruples Anecdotes release, a loader that
makes no model call keeps those that describe a past event rather than a
hypothetical one and meet the vote rule, strips from the body a trailing
block of edits, updates or summary, the post's own question to the forum,
vote tallies, links and forum chatter, and drops an account whose body
states a verdict, whose trailing block exceeds 10 percent of it, whose
removed text exceeds 15 percent of it, in which forum text survives inside
a long sentence, or which falls outside 400 to 6,000 characters once
stripped, and every model reads the stripped body without the post's title.
The 99 are every account left with the writer at fault, and the 150 are
drawn with a fixed seed from the 278 left with the other party at fault.
% round 3 methods review (m-collective-03): scripts/load_scruples.py load_scruples_consensus (historical_only keeps
% post_type HISTORICAL, min_votes 50, min_consensus 0.90 on the modal label, AUTHOR -> YTA and OTHER -> NTA slices,
% strip_meta, then random.Random(seed).sample only when n < eligible) and strip_meta (trailing EDIT/ETA/UPDATE/TL;DR
% block cut, 'AITA for' sentences, vote tallies, URLs and short platform or title-reference sentences removed; drops
% verdict_token_in_body, edit_block_substantive > 0.10, over_stripped > 0.15, residual_meta, too_short < 400, too_long
% > 6,000 characters; docstring: no model call, the title 'which the model is never shown'); scripts/run_crowdgold_aita.py
% load_items and items_from_frame (post_text only) with n_yta 99, n_nta 150, seed 44 (scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py
% main); recounted 2026-09-24: 32,766 anecdotes scanned, 99 eligible writer-at-fault, 278 eligible other-party-at-fault,
% the seed-44 draw reproduces the 249 item ids of cg_deliberation_haiku_249_rows.csv
Before any debate a
content-filter screen drops every item on which the platform's content filter
had refused any of that model's cached single-agent NoT answers to the same
account, whatever their framing or sample, because refusals fall on the same
items and a debate sends that account to the model in each of its 17 calls.
The screen reads whatever answers are cached when a model's debates are
launched, three per framing on every model but haiku, which has one per
framing and no refusal. Its reach therefore grows with the cache, and on grok
the step from one to three cached answers per framing raised the items it
drops from 30 to 39. It keeps 210 items on grok, 86 of them with the writer
at fault, 249 on haiku (99), 225 on Llama (92), 217 on Mistral (88) and 210 on
DeepSeek (84), so the item sets differ slightly across models.

Each registration fixed the number of debates per item and framing within
its spending ceiling, four on grok, two on Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek and one
on haiku, and, where registered in advance, added debates when fewer than 60
debates were flagged in the set on which a lift is read, the registered
minimum for reading a lift, the error rate of flagged debates minus that of
unflagged ones.

% below
This happened once among the conditions of Table~\ref{tab-routing}. Grok
with the exchange cut was first run under an earlier registration with one
debate per item and framing, which flagged 54 of its 419 codable debates,
fewer than the minimum, and its lift over all verdicts was recorded but not
read. Its other three debates per item and framing were registered after
that result and then run, so 420 of its 1,680 debates predate the
registration under which the other 1,260 were run.

% grok collective's reuse of its first 420 debates is stated in Appendix app-seat
On grok
the debates number 1,680 with the exchange kept, each seat reading the other
two seats' earlier statements before its rebuttal and its final position,
and 1,680 with the exchange cut, so that no seat reads another's text. They
number 900 on Llama, 868 on Mistral, 840 on DeepSeek and 491 on haiku.
Each repeated debate on an item and framing samples all 17 calls afresh at
the model's decoding settings (Appendix~\ref{app-cost}).
% round 3 methods review (m-collective-13): scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py do_call (sample_idx keys the cache path
% and is passed to generate_any), CALLS_PER_CELL 17; decoding settings per provider route in Appendix app-cost
% (scripts/generators.py)
Haiku's seven missing debates, all with the user as the writer, failed on
connection errors after the generation script's five attempts, and under the
pre-registration's standing rule for such failures, which never reruns a
failed debate and compares conditions only on what completed, they were not
run again, so its analysis uses the 491 that completed. The debates that the
exchange-cut, Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek runs lost to outages of the network
link were instead run again, under amendments recorded before each rerun
that allow a rerun only after such an outage, which the log judges a failure
of the machine's connection rather than of a model call and so unrelated to
any debate's outcome, and keep the standing rule for failed model calls. The
first of these amendments was recorded after haiku's missing debates had
been handled under the standing rule.

% log records them as connection (DNS) failures handled before the link-outage class existed
Dropping the debates in which the moderator's
synthesis or integrated verdict names none of the four outcomes, the writer,
the other party, both or neither at fault, because it gives no readable
verdict line or declares the account undecidable, removes 3 on grok with the
exchange kept, 5 with it cut, 3 on Llama, 0 on Mistral, 8 on DeepSeek and 4
on haiku and leaves the codable debates counted in Table~\ref{tab-routing}.

% None and the debate is skipped) (m-routing-23)
Every collective in the table passed its registered guards before any number
was read. At any stage and in either framing, at most 5 percent of any seat's
or the moderator's calls were truncated, and at most 5 percent were empty or,
where a verdict, label or vote is read from them, unparseable. At least 95
percent of debates were codable, the seats left a mean of at most 0.05
unparsed labels per debate, and each routing judge (a model whose majority
of three answers replaces the verdict of a debate sent to it, here sonnet,
the stronger judge, or the haiku judge of the comparison by verdict type
below) gave a parseable majority on
at least 95 percent of the debates that the counter flags or whose integrated
verdict blames both parties or neither. A flagged debate on which sonnet
yields no majority keeps the collective's verdict and stays in the count,
which happens on one flagged debate on Llama and five on Mistral, all on
pairs where each of sonnet's three answers declares the account undecidable,
and on none elsewhere. The first
Mistral collective failed its truncation guard and was rerun
(Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}).

% counted as NOVERDICT at the synthesis, label, integrated-verdict and vote rounds);

% MIN_JUDGE_EMISSION 0.95 on union-fired debates for both judge slots and on the solo signals' pairs);
% router_decomposition{,_noedge_k4,_haiku,_llama,_mistral,_deepseek}.json guards.passed true (codable
% share 0.990 to 1.000, sonnet emission on union-fired debates 0.987 to 1.000); routed_ok (S2 kept when
% the judge has no majority); prereg 16.26 amendment ~l.8363-8430 (m-routing-06)

% sonnet_judge_emission_on_union_fired (limit 0.95; union = counter fired or S2 verdict ESH or NAH);
% guards sentence split (clarity loop 2, appendix2-10); routing judge defined as in Table tab-app-models's caption
% ('routing judge one whose majority of three answers replaces the collective's verdict on the debates sent to it')
% (whole-20, round 3)
% m-routing round 2: recounted 2026-09-24 with scripts/verify_pillar3_headline.py load (fired and judge_ok None):
% Llama 1 of 71 flagged, Mistral 5 of 760, grok, edges off, haiku and DeepSeek 0;
% router_decomposition{,_noedge_k4,_llama,_mistral,_deepseek}.json composed_sonnet.block.routers.counter
% .n_fallback_to_s2 0, 0, 1, 5, 0 and router_decomposition_haiku.json composed_haiku (the sonnet slot) 0; the
% no-majority pairs are four as_asker pairs of cg_sonnet_actuator_rows.csv whose three answers are all
% UNRESOLVED (m-routing-11)

\paragraph{Debate, flag and judge.}
The writer's advocate puts the writer's case as strongly as the account
allows, the other party's advocate does the same for the person the writer is
in conflict with, and the neutral seat, called the Neutral Adjudicator in its
prompt, speaks for neither and asks for the verdict the account supports
whoever brought it forward.
% "neutral seat" is Section sec-collective's name (whole-27); the role header's name, Neutral Adjudicator,
% is printed verbatim in Appendix app-prompts (integrating editor, clarity loop 1, 2026-09-24)
% the comment above no longer swallows "Both advocates" (clarity loop 2, appendix2-01, collective-01,
% related-05)
Both advocates
are told not to invent facts, and only the writer's advocate is also told not
to claim the writer is not in the wrong unless the account supports it, so the
two briefs are not mirror images. The moderator is a fourth copy of the
seats' model under two system prompts of its own, one per moderator stage,
that carry neither the scaffold nor a side (Appendix~\ref{app-prompts}).
% clarity round 2 (2026-09-24, m-collective-09): scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py run_deliberation
% (mod_model = moderator_model or model; SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM at the synthesis and INTEGRATION_SYSTEM at the
% integration, agent_system on seat calls only); moderator_model "(agent model)" in
% cg_deliberation{,_noedge,_haiku_249,_llama3370binstruct,_mistrallarge32,_deepseekv4pro}_summary.json; both
% system prompts printed in figures/fig_collective_prompts.tex
Each debate runs seven stages in 17 calls,
five stages of one call per seat and two of one moderator call
(Table~\ref{tab-app-stages}, prompts in Appendix~\ref{app-prompts}). Each seat
opens with a verdict, rebuts after reading the other two openings and states a
final position after reading their rebuttals, the moderator reads the three
final positions and writes a synthesis with a verdict, and each seat, shown
its own final position and the synthesis, accepts it, accepts it with one
stated modification or rejects it with one stated concern, so that every
objection answers a concrete proposal the moderator can revise. The moderator
then writes the integrated verdict from its synthesis and the three labels
with any stated modification or concern, without the seats' earlier text, and
that verdict is scored as the collective's, while the seats' closing vote on
it enters no result in this paper. Every call sees the account in the debate's
framing and the verdict-label glossary, earlier text passes forward
untruncated, and a label that does not parse counts as no objection, a
registered guard allowing at most 0.05 unparsed labels per debate on average.
% scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py ROLES (only writer_advocate's brief adds 'you may not assert that

The moderator is shown a seat's modification or concern only when the seat
writes it after the marker that the label prompt asks for, on the marker's
own line, so a seat that writes it on the next line or without the marker
counts as objecting but reaches the moderator with its label alone, and the
counter, which reads labels alone, is unaffected. This left the moderator
without the text of 664 of Mistral's 2,108 objections, about a third, 660 of
them written on the line after the marker, and of 422 of the 1,926
objections on grok with CoT seats (Appendix~\ref{app-seat}), none of them
with the marker, against at most 3.2 percent of the objections in each other
collective of Table~\ref{tab-routing}.

Each seat's opening, rebuttal and final position is capped at 2,560 tokens,
4,096 on Mistral after the rerun recorded in Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}, each
moderator call at 1,024 tokens, and each label and vote at 1,024 and 512
tokens on grok, with the exchange kept or cut, and at 3,072 on haiku, Llama,
Mistral and DeepSeek. The higher caps date from the early screen of seat
stakes recorded among the spend overruns of Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}, in
which the 512-token vote cap, sized to grok's short votes, truncated most of
haiku's votes.

With the exchange cut, no seat reads another seat's
text, so the rebuttal becomes a development of the seat's own opening and the
final position is stated from its own text alone, while the moderator still
reads all three final positions and every later stage is unchanged, so each
debate still makes 17 calls and the condition tests whether the flag needs
the seats' exchange.

A seat objects when its label accepts the synthesis with a modification or
rejects it, whether or not the text of that modification or concern reaches
the moderator,
% round 3 methods review (m-collective-01): objection read from the label alone, scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py
% l.1211 objected = lab in (ACCEPT_WITH_MODIFICATION, REJECT); what the moderator sees is stated after the guard sentence
% above; supersedes the clarity loop 2 note that changed "its label accepts" to "it accepts"
and the counter flags a debate when two or more of the three seats object.
The counter is a fixed threshold, not a learned rule like that of
\citet{he2026minoritysentinel}, who train a classifier on features of
debates among agents with no assigned side to decide when the minority
should overturn the majority.
% clarity loop 3 (2026-09-24, collective-05): the gloss of the learned rule that Section sec-collective, paragraph
% "Dissent flags errors", names in the fewest words ("which the two-objection threshold, not a learned rule,
% approximates"); the section keeps the citation because Related Work's He et al. sentence moved to Appendix
% app-related (whole-14)
% collective-09 (2026-09-23): "the learned rule of He et al." so that the threshold is not read as theirs
% W40 (2026-09-23): He et al. 2026 (arXiv 2606.29270, https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29270, abstract fetched
% 2026-09-23) train a LightGBM classifier on a debate fingerprint from debate logs of three heterogeneous
% agents with no assigned side to decide when to flip a majority vote to the minority, and find an
% LLM-as-judge baseline gives negative net gain; the counter is a fixed threshold (n_objectors >= 2,
% prereg 16.1 ~l.3042-3084) on objections to the synthesis and routes to a judge. "The roles fix" the
% split on grok: majority of opening verdicts equals the neutral seat's on 1,642 of 1,642 debates
% (effective_voter.json, prereg 16.15.4 RESULTS ~l.4496). Existing bib key, no new citation.
Routing replaces a flagged debate's verdict with the majority of sonnet's
codable answers, those coded at fault or not at fault among the three it
gave on the debate's item and framing. Sonnet gave those answers once, apart
from any debate and reading only the account in that framing, under the
brief unscaffolded prompt used on the crowd-labelled set, the verdict-label
glossary with a request for at most four sentences of reasoning and no
scaffold (Appendix~\ref{app-prompts}), so every flagged debate on an item
and framing pair receives the same judge verdict. When those answers tie or
none is codable, the collective's verdict stands. Sonnet
answering every case from the same answers, with the seat model's solo
verdict kept on the item and framing pairs where sonnet has no majority, two
of 420 on grok and at most three on
any model, scores 0.969 to 0.976 on the five models' item sets,
above every seat model's solo (at most 0.959), which makes it the stronger
judge.

\paragraph{Estimators and the independent check.}
Every interval on the flag, on routing and on the comparison with the solo,
in Section~\ref{sec-collective} and here, is a 95 percent percentile bootstrap
with 4,000 draws that resamples items with replacement and keeps together all
debates of an item, under both framings and every repeat, because they share
one account and are not independent. In Section~\ref{sec-collective} and
here, a difference with an interval is detectable when its 95 percent
interval excludes zero, two systems are indistinguishable, or one does as
well as the other, when the interval of their difference includes zero, and
one routes at least as well as the other, or is at least as accurate, when
that interval lies wholly in its favour or includes zero, the reading
registered in advance for sample disagreement against the counter when the
solo's answers are routed. Comparisons without an interval, such as the
accuracy and cost frontier below, compare means alone.
% round 3 (routing reviewer, 2026-09-24): checked against every use in Sections 1, 6 and 7 and in this
% subsection; detectable = interval excluding zero (Mistral routed minus solo +12.3 [+8.1, +16.8],
% collective_vs_solo_paired.json); indistinguishable or as well = including zero (grok routed minus solo +0.1
% [-2.6, +3.0], haiku +3.1 [-0.8, +7.0], same artefact; haiku disagreement minus counter +0.6 [-1.6, +3.1],
% routing_certification_haiku.json); at least as well = prereg 16.22 R3 (~l.6722-6729, 'in favour of
% disagreement, or including 0'), grok +0.2 [-1.9, +2.4] (routing_certification.json
% routed_vs_solo.sonnet.pairwise.disagree_minus_counter); at least as accurate = solo minus collective excluding
% zero on four models, haiku -0.6 [-4.7, +3.5] (collective_vs_solo_paired.json); the frontier is R5, descriptive
% means (routing_certification.json cost_per_decision.note 'Means only, no intervals') (m-routing-02)
The statistic is a mean over the
resampled debates, or over item and framing pairs when the solo's answers are
routed, a difference between two systems is paired, each debate or pair
contributing its own difference, and a lift within a verdict type, the error
rate of flagged debates minus that of unflagged ones, resamples all of the
model's items and keeps only that type's debates inside each draw, so their
number varies from draw to draw. The routing gain in Table~\ref{tab-routing}
is the routed minus the collective accuracy over a condition's codable
debates, and each draw divides the summed per-debate differences by the
number of debates drawn, and Table~\ref{tab-app-routing-ci} gives its interval
with those of the two differences from the solo. The registered analyses use seed 13 and
supply the lifts, the router comparisons and the routing of the solo's
answers, and the independent verification script described below, the
pairing with the solo and the second-judge check use seed 101. The contrast of opposed and identical seats
in Section~\ref{sec-collective} is the exception, with 3,000 draws and seed
29, and the comparison of narrated and plain seats has its own estimator in
Appendix~\ref{app-seat}. The $n$, fire, collective, routed, routed minus
collective and precision and recall columns of Table~\ref{tab-routing} come
from the verification script, written apart from the registered analysis and
sharing no code with it so that an error in either would show as a
disagreement between them. The verification script reads, from the tables
the generation scripts wrote, each debate's two verdicts and count of
objecting seats, each seat's recorded label and sonnet's cached answers, and
recomputes each condition's codable
count, collective accuracy, fire rate, error rates of flagged and unflagged
one-party verdicts and their ratio, routed accuracy and routing gain with its
interval. On all six rows of Table~\ref{tab-routing} every value agrees with
the registered one,
which the script holds as a constant copied from the registered artefact,
counts exactly, rates and the gain within 0.15 points, the ratio within 0.05
and interval bounds within 0.6 points, a looser tolerance because the two
bootstraps draw different random streams, the largest difference in an
interval bound being 0.19 points, and its count of objecting seats matches
the seats' recorded labels in every debate. It does not recompute the lifts'
intervals, the comparisons between routers, the routing of the solo's
answers or the costs, and like the registered analysis it takes each verdict
and label as the generation scripts parsed them from the models' text.

\paragraph{Pairing with the solo.}
The solo and paired-difference columns of the same table come from a
comparison added after the results were seen and not pre-registered, which
asks whether the collective, before and after routing, is more accurate than
the same model answering alone. The solo is that model answering the same
account in the same framing alone in three separate calls, at the decoding
settings of Appendix~\ref{app-cost} and under the brief unscaffolded prompt
used on the crowd-labelled set, which asks for at most four sentences of
reasoning and carries neither NoT nor CoT (Appendix~\ref{app-prompts}).
Sonnet answers under the same prompt, so the solo and the routing judge
differ only in the model, while the solo differs from the collective in its
prompt as well as in its number of agents. Each answer is coded at fault or
not, and the solo's verdict is the majority of its codable answers, the
self-consistency baseline of Section~\ref{sec-related}
\citep{wang2023selfconsistency}. A pair whose answers tie or do not parse
therefore has no solo verdict, and the one haiku debate and the two Llama and
two DeepSeek debates on such pairs are left out of the difference columns.
% clarity loop 3 (m-routing-07, merged with appendix2-15's sentence split): three separate calls =
% scripts/run_crowdgold_aita.py generate_any(..., sample_idx=idx), decoding per scripts/generators.py as in
% Appendix app-cost; seats run NoT (scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py agent_system, default narrative_cot),
% solo the standard prompt (scripts/run_crowdgold_aita.py build_system); purpose from the script docstring ('the
% collective against the same model's solo majority-of-three, paired')
Each remaining debate, the unit of the routing gain, is set against
the solo's verdict on its own item and framing, and the paired difference is
bootstrapped over items with the same 4,000 draws and seed 101. The
collective and routed columns cover all codable debates, the solo column
averages over the item and framing pairs that have both a solo verdict and a
codable debate and the difference columns over paired debates, so a
difference can depart from the difference of the accuracy columns by up to
0.2 points, as on haiku. A variant that weights each item and framing pair
equally moves no bound by more than 0.2 points, and a rerun with seed 13
none by more than 0.25.

The comparison was not run on grok with the exchange cut, whose row of
Table~\ref{tab-routing} therefore leaves the solo and the two differences
from it blank.

% wording as in Section sec-collective after m-collective-08 (collective editor's appendix addition A4, round 3)

In an earlier collective on nano, the
collective's accuracy of 0.829 over 828 debates sits above its solo's 0.820
over 406 item and framing pairs, an unregistered readout, and in an earlier
haiku collective on 100 Scruples accounts the collective's 0.885 over 192
debates sits above the solo's 0.851 over 630
answers and above the solo's 0.862 on the 249-item set of
Table~\ref{tab-routing}, in each case debates set
against the solo's own answers on the same items with no paired interval.

\paragraph{Role-lock.}
Role-lock measures how far an advocate's assigned side fixes its first
verdict. It is the absolute difference between the two advocates' opening
at-fault rates, each the share of that seat's opening verdicts that blame the
writer alone or both parties, taken over every debate in which that seat's
opening verdict names one of the four outcomes rather than declaring the
account undecidable or giving none, so it is 1 when each advocate always
opens for its own side and 0 when the side makes no difference to the
opening. It is 0.971 on grok, 0.862 on Llama, 0.816 on DeepSeek, 0.708 on
Mistral and 0.419 on haiku, and the opening at-fault rates of the writer's
advocate, the other party's advocate and the neutral seat are 0.3, 97.4 and
43.0 percent on grok, 12.3, 98.5 and 73.3 on Llama, 6.5, 88.1 and 45.4 on
DeepSeek, 4.2, 75.0 and 52.9 on Mistral and 49.9, 91.8 and 68.2 on haiku. On
grok the advocates are so fixed that a majority of the three opening verdicts
equals the neutral seat's on all 1,642 debates with three codable openings,
and on haiku the writer's advocate blames the writer on half of its openings
and is the most accurate seat.

\paragraph{Identical against opposed seats.}
Section~\ref{sec-collective} reports that opposition itself costs accuracy on
grok. The contrast behind that finding reran the protocol on grok once per
item and framing with all three seats carrying the neutral seat's brief, the
references to the participants' stakes removed from the opening preamble and
from the moderator's two system prompts, and the exchange kept, and set its
integrated verdicts' accuracy, 0.863, against the opposed seats' 0.842 on the
415 item and framing pairs over 209 items that both cover
(Table~\ref{tab-routing}'s 0.843 is over all 1,677 debates), a difference of
$+2.1$ points $[+0.4, +3.8]$ from a 3,000-draw item bootstrap, a contrast read
after the data were seen rather than declared in advance.

% (m-collective-15); verify_pillar3_headline_grok.json collective 0.843 over 1,677 (appendix2-15)

% in scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py; checked 2026-09-24

\paragraph{The flag's lift.}
Within a set of codable debates, the flagged error rate is the share of
debates flagged by the counter whose integrated verdict is wrong, the lift is
that share minus the same share among unflagged debates, in points, and the
ratio of Section~\ref{sec-collective} divides the first share by the second.
A lift is read only when at least 60 debates in the set are flagged, a
minimum registered as a power gate, so that a lift is read only where enough
debates are flagged to estimate it usefully, and its 95 percent interval is a
percentile bootstrap of
4,000 draws that resamples all of a model's items, keeping every debate of an
item together, and applies any restriction by verdict type inside each draw,
since which verdict a debate reaches is itself an outcome of the debate.
% scripts/analyze_router_decomposition.py (stratum_lifts, concentration, _lift_stat, DEFAULT_DRAWS 4000,
% DEFAULT_SEED 13, RESAMPLING FRAME: full-population items resampled, stratum restriction inside each
% resample; n_items_frame 210, 210, 217, 210, 225, 249); minimum-fired 60 first registered in Addendum
% 16.8 (~l.3339) and 16.9 (~l.3485), and for the within-one-loser lift in 16.23 (~l.6754, ~l.6762),
% 16.24 ('exactly as 16.23'), 16.25 (~l.6792), 16.26, 16.27 and 16.28 (~l.8661);
% scripts/analyze_topology_2x2.py MIN_FIRED 60 (m-collective-04)
% power gate: prereg 16.8 gates (~l.3339, 'Minimum-fired gate', below it only fire rate, role-lock and
% localisation excess reported, beside the stated MDEs) and 16.26 readouts (~l.8161, 'registered power gate
% (60 fired)') (appendix2-08); heading renamed and the paragraph split into the lift, what drives the flag and
% operating points (appendix2-11)
A debate's verdict type, here and in Section~\ref{sec-collective}, is that of
its integrated verdict, the verdict scored, which the moderator writes after
reading the seats' labels, not that of the synthesis the seats labelled,
because the integrated verdict is the one the collective returns, the one
routing replaces and the one on which the split by verdict type was first
defined. The two differ in type on 112 of grok's 1,677 codable debates, whose
verdict type the moderator thus changed after reading the seats' labels, and
no lift split by the synthesis's type is reported.

The lift is read within verdict types because the argument for the counter in
Section~\ref{sec-collective} needs a verdict that blames exactly one party, so
that one objection, from that party's advocate, is expected and only a second
is left unexplained by the assigned sides. On grok's verdicts that blame both
parties or neither, which the argument does not cover, the two advocates'
objections are only weakly anticorrelated, by the phi coefficient defined in
the next paragraph, and two or more seats object on
235 of the 390 debates.

\paragraph{What drives the flag.}
The mechanism behind the dissent sensor of Section~\ref{sec-collective} is
model-dependent. On the against-interest explanation of the sensor, each advocate
objects when the verdict goes against its own side, so a verdict that blames
one party gives that party's advocate a reason to object and the informative
objection is a second one, from a seat the verdict does not go against.
% the reading as stated in Section sec-collective, sections/pillar3.tex l.195-197 ('A verdict blaming one party
% gives that party's advocate a reason to object, so the informative objection is a second one no assigned side
% explains') (appendix2-12)
On verdicts that blame exactly one party, the phi coefficient
between the two advocates' binary objection indicators, their Pearson
correlation over all such debates whatever the neutral seat does, which is
$-1$ when exactly one of them objects in every debate and $+1$ when in every
debate they object together or stay silent together, shows their objections strongly opposed on grok
($\phi = -0.90$ $[-0.93, -0.87]$), as the against-interest explanation expects,
but positively correlated on haiku
($+0.25$ $[+0.13, +0.36]$), Llama ($+0.15$ $[+0.07, +0.23]$) and Mistral
($+0.21$ $[+0.12, +0.30]$), and only weakly opposed on DeepSeek ($-0.09$
$[-0.18, -0.01]$), each interval from the same item bootstrap as the lift.
With $a$ the debates in which both advocates object, $b$ and $c$ those in
which only the writer's or only the other party's advocate does and $d$ those
in which neither does, $\phi = (ad - bc)/\sqrt{(a+b)(c+d)(a+c)(b+d)}$, which
is undefined when either advocate objects in every debate or in none, and a
bootstrap draw in which it is undefined is dropped from its interval.
The against-interest explanation is therefore supported on grok and not
established elsewhere, and on grok it rests mainly on which side the verdict
goes against, since once that side is held fixed the advocates' objections
are much less opposed, $\phi$ being $-0.34$ $[-0.66, +0.01]$ within verdicts
that blame the writer alone and $-0.16$ $[-0.32, -0.02]$ within those that
blame the other party alone.

% writer's and the other party's advocates' objected_r3 within the verdict type, phi = (ad - bc) /

% NTA -0.162 [-0.317, -0.022], prereg 16.12 RESULTS note ~l.4999-5006 (appendix2-26: against -0.90 pooled)
Role-lock is graded rather than present or absent, and it does not by itself
fix the sensor. Haiku, the least locked, is the one model on which the second
objection's lift in the collective's error rate on verdicts that blame one
party has an interval including zero, but Llama's seats lock nearly as
tightly as grok's while its advocates' objections are positively correlated
($\phi = +0.15$ above), so a tight lock appears necessary for opposed
objections and not sufficient, a pattern over five models that we report as
suggestive.

\paragraph{Operating points.}
An acceptance with a modification counts as an objection, and on every model
but grok most objections are such acceptances rather than rejections.
% clarity loop 3 (appendix2-22, merged with m-collective-01 (c)): 'stated' dropped, the counts are r3_label counts,
% which do not need the text (see 'Debate, flag and judge')
Rejections make up 90 percent of grok's
objections and 3 percent of Mistral's, 7 percent of Llama's, 18 percent of
haiku's and 19 percent of DeepSeek's, so Mistral's seats reject the
synthesis on only 2.5 percent of their responses while objecting on 81
percent.

% (0.898), Mistral 66 / 2042 (0.031, reject share of all 2604 responses 0.025, objected 0.810), Llama
% 24 / 332 (0.067), haiku 72 / 326 (0.181), DeepSeek 172 / 728 (0.191); objected_r3 = 1 iff r3_label in
% (ACCEPT_WITH_MODIFICATION, REJECT), scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py l.1211

On verdicts that blame one party the flag has precision 0.39, the share of
flagged verdicts that are wrong against the crowd verdict, and recall 0.43, the
share of wrong verdicts that are flagged, on grok, so it misses 47 of the 83
wrong verdicts. It catches 30 of 257 wrong verdicts on Llama (precision 0.47,
recall 0.12), where even unflagged verdicts are wrong 0.31 of the time, and
reaches recall 0.97 on Mistral (precision 0.13) only because Mistral's seats
object so often, two or more of them on 88 percent of its debates and 85
percent of these verdicts, that there the counter is close to a constant
(Table~\ref{tab-routing}).

\paragraph{Error by verdict type and direction.}
Every item's crowd verdict blames exactly one party, the writer alone or the
other party alone, so a verdict that blames both parties, scored as at fault,
or no one, scored as not at fault, can match it only through that coding, and
the collective is right on such verdicts far less often, 0.536 of the time
over 390 debates on grok, 0.532 over 412 with the exchange cut, 0.397 over 63
on haiku, 0.097 over 93 on Llama, 0.409 over 257 on DeepSeek and 0.433 over
254 on Mistral, against
0.936, 0.930, 0.922, 0.680, 0.943 and 0.884, in the same order, on verdicts
that blame one party.
% W23 (2026-09-23): flooding_analysis{,_noedge_k4,_haiku_249,_llama,_deepseek,_mistral}.json
% aita.populations.s1_s2_codable_16_15.groups {ESH, NAH, one_loser} n and n_wrong (grok ESH 272/120 +
% NAH 118/61 = 181 of 390, one_loser 83 of 1,287; edges off 123+70 = 193 of 412, 88 of 1,263; haiku
% 37+1 = 38 of 63, 33 of 424; Llama 84+0 = 84 of 93, 257 of 804; DeepSeek 141+11 = 152 of 257, 33 of
% 575; Mistral 131+13 = 144 of 254, 71 of 614); prereg 16.12 RESULTS note ~l.5001-5003 (wrong 0.064 /
% 0.464), 16.24 RESULTS ~l.7652, 16.26 RESULTS ~l.8483-8484, 16.27 RESULTS ~l.8288; the crowd labels are
% 99 writer-alone and 150 other-party-alone accounts (Item sets paragraph); "far less often" for "few"
% (clarity loop 2, appendix2-27)
The error's direction differs by model. On Llama the collective is right on
0.365 of the debates where the crowd found the writer not at fault and 0.989
where it did, so it blames the writer on 699 of 897 debates, 78 percent,
against the crowd's 366, 41 percent, while grok errs more on at-fault items,
right on 0.878 of not-at-fault items against 0.791 of at-fault ones, and
haiku, DeepSeek and Mistral, like Llama, err more on not-at-fault items,
right on 0.808, 0.686 and 0.676 of those against 0.923, 0.916 and 0.864 of
at-fault items.

\paragraph{The routing gain by verdict type.}
The routing gain of Section~\ref{sec-collective}, routed minus collective
accuracy over all debates, splits by verdict type. Under the sonnet judge of
Table~\ref{tab-routing} routing on the counter raises accuracy on grok's verdicts that
blame one party from 0.936 to 0.963, a net gain of 35 correct verdicts over
those 1,287 debates, 2.1 of the 7.0 points once divided by all 1,677
debates, and on verdicts that blame both parties or neither from 0.536 to
0.749, a net gain of 83 correct verdicts over 390 debates, 4.9 points, while
firing on 235 of those 390. Within those 390 the flagged debates are wrong on
0.426 and the unflagged on 0.523, a lift of $-9.7$ points $[-23.3, +4.3]$,
the lift that Section~\ref{sec-collective} reports as undetectable.
% W20 (2026-09-23): router_decomposition.json composed_sonnet.block.routers.counter.by_stratum (acc_s2
% 0.9355 and 0.5359, acc_composed 0.9627 and 0.7487, n_fired 93 and 235, n 1,287 and 390 of 1,677),
% prereg 16.15.1 RESULTS ~l.4261-4283 and ~l.4308-4325
% m-routing-14 (2026-09-23): router_decomposition.json counter_lift_by_stratum.both_party (n_fired 235 of 390,
% p_wrong_fired 0.4255, p_wrong_unfired 0.5226, lift -0.097 [-0.2332, +0.043], seed 13, full 210-item frame);
% 35 / 1,677 = 0.0209 and 83 / 1,677 = 0.0495 from composed_sonnet.block.routers.counter.by_stratum;
% (0.9627 - 0.9355) x 1,287 = 35 and (0.7487 - 0.5359) x 390 = 83 (appendix2-19)
The registered verdict-type router, which sends every verdict that blames
both parties or neither to the judge and uses no dissent signal, is right on
0.936 of debates after routing against the counter's 0.913, $+2.3$ points
$[+0.4, +4.3]$ with sonnet as the judge and $+1.4$ $[-0.3, +3.0]$ with haiku
in its place, and the union of the two signals,
which routes a debate when either fires, reaches 0.956, above the verdict
type alone by $+2.1$ $[+1.1, +3.3]$ with sonnet and $+1.1$ $[-0.1, +2.4]$
with haiku, so the two signals add detectably under the stronger judge only.
This comparison's registration named haiku, answering alone by majority of
three from cached answers, as the judge of its routing rule and added sonnet
where sonnet's cached answers covered the debates.
% prereg 16.15.1 registration (~l.3914-3920, composed accuracy at the deployed rule with the cached haiku majority
% of three, the same routers with the cached sonnet judge from 16.11a where its cache covers the debate);
% 16.15.1 RESULTS Rule 3 (~l.4370-4377, complementarity judge-dependent, name the judge) (appendix2-13)
As a baseline that separates the signals from the judge's own strength,
routing at random the same number of debates as the union, 28.8 percent,
reaches 0.881 with sonnet and 0.845 with haiku, averaged over 200 seeds,
against 0.956 and 0.905 for the union and 0.913 and 0.880 for the counter at
its lower coverage of 19.6 percent.

% ~l.4284-4331, Rules 2 and 3 ~l.4358-4377 (complementarity judge-dependent, name the judge)

% (prereg ~l.4284-4287) (appendix2-20)

\paragraph{Routing the solo's answers, and cost.}
The flags can also route the solo model's own answers, the solo giving the
answer and the debates deciding only which of its answers go to sonnet, a
test of whether the collective's dissent tells the solo anything that the
solo's own three answers do not. On grok the registered comparison replaces
grok's majority-of-three verdict on an item in one framing with sonnet's
majority of three on the same item and framing, grok's verdict standing
where sonnet has none, when the counter fires in more than half of the
codable debates on that item and framing, three of four when all four are
codable. The rule is set per item and framing because the solo gives one
verdict there, where the routed collective's rule acts on each debate. It
routes 51 of the 420 item and framing pairs, 12.1 percent, and improves grok
alone from 0.912 to 0.936, $+2.4$ points $[+0.5, +4.5]$.
% round 3 (routing reviewer, 2026-09-24): scripts/analyze_router_decomposition.py cell_composed_ok (judge majority
% if the signal fires, base verdict if the judge has none); routing_certification.json routed_vs_solo.sonnet
% .signals.counter n_fired 51, n_cells 420, coverage 0.1214, acc_base 0.9119, acc_routed 0.9357; purpose from
% prereg 16.22 R1 and R3 (~l.6712-6729, 'the counter carries information the samples do not') (m-routing-04,
% merged with appendix2-16's purpose clause and sentence split; prereg 16.22 registration ~l.6664-6726, the
% demotion test, the collective sensor scoped to the collective's own errors and dominated on cost for solo errors
% if the solo's own disagreement routes at least as well)
The registration allowed none of this comparison's numbers to be read until
the same analysis had reproduced, within 0.005, the routed accuracy and gain
over grok alone of an earlier readout that routed the same answers on the
flag registered first (Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}), which it did, and the
readouts on other models below are exploratory and carry no such gate.
% prereg 16.22 Gate (~l.6706-6710) and RESULTS (~l.6806-6816, GATE PASSED, 0.9354 / +0.0239 reproduced on 418
% cells, 0.9357 / +0.0238 on 420); routing_certification.json replay_gate_16_11a (tolerance 0.005, status passed,
% reproduces full and judge_codable); flag registered first = composite, scripts/analyze_loop_step.py flagged;
% routing_certification_{haiku,llama,mistral,deepseek}.json replay_gate_16_11a status not_applicable (m-routing-04)
Routing instead on
the solo's sample disagreement, whenever its codable answers include both an
at-fault and a not-at-fault verdict
\citep{wang2023selfconsistency,manakul2023selfcheck}, a confidence-based
deferral rule of the kind model cascades use
\citep{jitkrittum2023deferral,gupta2024cascades,yue2024cascades},
does as well, $+0.2$ points $[-1.9, +2.4]$ in accuracy relative to the
counter-routed solo, while routing 33 of the 420 pairs, 7.9 percent, at a
% round 3 (routing reviewer, 2026-09-24): routing_certification.json routed_vs_solo.sonnet.signals.disagree
% n_fired 33 of n_cells 420, coverage 0.0786 (counter 51, 0.1214), prereg 16.22 RESULTS ~l.6822-6824 (m-routing-03,
% merged with appendix2-17)
% W19 (2026-09-23): the cascade and self-consistency keys were verified at their primary pages on
% 2026-09-14 (papers/embodied_sensor/related_works_verified.json); yue2024cascades defers on the
% weaker model's sample consistency, jitkrittum2023deferral and gupta2024cascades on its confidence
seventh of the counter-routed solo's price in the registered projection,
which prices one debate per item and framing although four were run, and a
twenty-fifth of its price with all four debates behind each flag, and sonnet
answering every case is 4.0 points $[+1.4, +6.9]$ above the counter-routed
solo.
% R2 is the counter-routed solo (routing_certification.json routed_vs_solo.sonnet.signals.counter
% .routed_minus_judge_everywhere -0.0405 [-0.0690, -0.0143]) (m-routing-03 and appendix2-18)
Routing the same numbers of pairs at random, 51 and 33, reaches 0.920 and
0.917 on average over 200 seeds, against 0.936 for the counter and 0.938 for
disagreement, means with no interval.

Prices here are per item and framing, a case in Section~\ref{sec-collective},
the mean list-rate cost of the token
counts each provider reports, which for grok leave out any reasoning tokens
the provider does not report, so that grok's prices may be understated. The
registered accuracy and cost frontier for grok, the systems for which no
other system is at least as accurate at a lower price, runs from grok alone
(0.912 at \$0.0005) through routing on the solo's sample disagreement (0.938
at \$0.0014) and routing on the solo's verdict type, whenever any of its three
samples blames both parties or neither (0.960 at \$0.0031, with 22 percent of item and framing
pairs routed, $+4.8$ points $[+1.9, +7.9]$ over grok alone and $-1.7$ points
$[-4.0, +0.5]$ against sonnet answering every case) to sonnet answering every
case (0.976 at \$0.0115).
% "sonnet everywhere" renamed "sonnet answering every case", Section sec-collective's name (round 3, m-routing-11)
% integrating editor, clarity loop 3 (2026-09-24): the next sentence had been swallowed by the comment line above
% (its opening words ended that line), so it is restored on a line of its own, wording unchanged
Every system that uses the collective's flags lies inside that
frontier, that is, some system that does not use the collective is at least
as accurate at a lower price. The counter-routed solo reaches 0.936 for
\$0.0098 in the registered projection to one debate per case, which prices
that debate and the judge on the 12.1 percent of pairs flagged but not the
solo's own three calls (\$0.0005), and for \$0.0352 with the four debates
that produced its flags, and the routed collective of
Section~\ref{sec-collective} reaches 0.913 for \$0.0107, priced as one debate
plus the judge on the share of first debates flagged, 19.8 percent, the rate a
deployment running one debate per item and framing would see, against
\$0.0115 for sonnet answering every case. The routed collective's accuracy,
unlike the counter-routed solo's, needs no such projection, since each debate
is routed on its own flag, so its mean over all 1,677 debates is what one
debate per item and framing scores on average.

Every price and call count of the collective includes the seats' three
closing votes, which no result reads, so a collective stopped at its
integrated verdict would make 14 calls.

The routed collective's 6.3-point gap to sonnet answering every case in
Section~\ref{sec-collective}, like sonnet's lead over the routed
collective on all five models (0.976, 0.969, 0.976, 0.975 and 0.976 against
0.913, 0.891, 0.661, 0.967 and 0.850 on grok, haiku, Llama, Mistral and
DeepSeek), sets a mean over debates against a mean over item and framing
pairs and carries no paired interval.

In exploratory readouts on the other models, which hold at most two debates

alone on Mistral, again at high coverage ($+12.2$ points $[+8.1, +16.6]$ with

Mistral ($-5.5$ $[-9.0, -2.1]$), where the counter routes most answers.

% routing_certification_grok_stdcot_k4.json (status exploratory, R1 +0.019 [+0.007, +0.036],

\paragraph{A second judge.}
Sonnet is the judge in every routed number of Section~\ref{sec-collective}
and Table~\ref{tab-routing}, and it shares a developer with the haiku seats.
As a check that the routing gain does not depend on this one judge or on its
sharing a developer with the haiku seats, computed after the results were
seen, with no new model calls and not registered, we routed the same flagged
debates to DeepSeek answering alone by majority of three, from the cached
answers that serve as its solo comparator, through the same routing rule and
bootstrap. The gain over the collective holds on all six conditions, $+6.5$
points $[+4.6, +8.6]$ on grok with the exchange kept, $+4.5$ $[+3.2, +6.0]$
with it cut, $+3.5$ $[+1.4, +5.7]$ on haiku, $+3.6$ $[+2.1, +5.1]$ on Llama,
$+6.6$ $[+4.7, +8.6]$ on DeepSeek's own debates, where the judge is the
seats' own model, and $+20.4$ $[+16.4, +24.9]$ on Mistral. Each gain is 0.2
to 1.0 points below the sonnet judge's, with no paired difference between
the two judges excluding zero, DeepSeek being the slightly weaker judge
everywhere (0.959 against 0.976 when answering every grok debate), and the
two judges agree on 85 to 96 percent of the flagged item and framing pairs.
This readout shows that the gain's existence does not depend on the sonnet
judge, not that the judges are equivalent.

The registered analysis of grok's debates named haiku, answering alone by
majority of three, as its judge and sonnet as a second judge, sonnet having
been added, in a registration made before any of its answers were drawn,
because haiku is less accurate on grok's items than grok's own solo (0.850
against 0.912 on the 420 item and framing pairs). With haiku in sonnet's
place the routing gain over the collective is $+3.8$ points $[+1.6, +6.0]$ on
grok, $+2.7$ $[+1.3, +4.2]$ with the exchange cut, $+2.7$ $[+1.2, +4.1]$ on
Llama, $+10.6$ $[+6.6, +14.9]$ on Mistral and $+3.2$ $[+1.2, +5.2]$ on
DeepSeek, and no readout sends haiku's own debates to a haiku judge. Routing
grok's solo verdicts to haiku by the counter gains nothing detectable
($+0.2$ points $[-1.4, +1.9]$), as the registered comparison with the solo
expected of a judge weaker than the solo.

% ============================================================================================
\subsection{Narrated versus plain seats}
\label{app-seat}

This comparison asks whether the collective's accuracy, its dissent sensor
and its routing need the scaffold in the seats.
% moved here to open the subsection (clarity loop 2, m-seats-04); no number

Replacing the seats' NoT system prompt with the CoT one, narration with
plain chain-of-thought, makes the
collective of Section~\ref{sec-collective} more accurate on grok and Llama,
the two models the reading rule below was registered for. The collective
seats three copies of one model on a first-person conflict account, an
advocate for each party and a neutral seat, and a fourth copy moderates and
writes the integrated verdict that is scored as the collective's
(Table~\ref{tab-app-stages}). Its accuracy is the share of those verdicts
that match the crowd's verdict on whether the writer is at fault. A narrated
seat has the NoT text of Figure~\ref{fig:prompt} as its system prompt and a
plain seat the CoT text. Narrated here names the whole NoT prompt, its
sections and narrative form together, not the narrative form that
Appendix~\ref{app-form} separates from NoT's content, and the main text calls
these NoT seats and CoT seats.
% whole-08 (round 3, 2026-09-24): Section sec-collective now says NoT seats and CoT seats; registered names
% (PLAIN-BETTER) unchanged; "the seats' NoT system prompt with the CoT one" (clarity loop 3, appendix2-32)
The paragraphs below give the design, the measures,
the reading and a later run on haiku.

The plain-seat collectives
rerun the protocol of Section~\ref{sec-collective} with one change, the CoT
prompt in place of NoT as each seat's system prompt. The model, the items
that passed that model's content-filter screen, the two framings (the user
as the writer, and a third-person account), the roles, the seats' user-turn
instructions, the moderator's prompts and the number of debates are
unchanged, 210 items with four debates per item and framing on grok and 225
with two on Llama.

% scaffold and tag changed) and RESULTS header (~l.8977); 16.16 design (~l.5204-5210, same model, panel,

% so the moderator is the same model); 1,680 = 210 x 2 x 4, 900 = 225 x 2 x 2
% prereg 16.28 registration (~l.8609-8625) and RESULTS header (~l.8977); seat_scaffold_comparison.json
% completeness (1,680 and 900 rows)
In both collectives the seat's system prompt is the scaffold text alone, and
its role, the case, the verdict-label glossary and the forced verdict line,
all printed in Appendix~\ref{app-prompts}, arrive in the user turn.

Every registered check passed. The generation script's guards, each a cap of
5 percent on the share of one debate stage's outputs that are truncated or
give no verdict and each required to hold before any number is read
(Appendix~\ref{app-prereg}), held, at worst 0.7 percent of one stage's
outputs giving no verdict and none truncated, all 1,680 grok and 900
Llama plain debates are present, and dropping the debates whose synthesis or
integrated verdict does not parse leaves 1,676 and 898 plain debates against
1,677 and 897 narrated.

On grok the narrated debates were all collected by 1 September 2026 and the
plain ones in two runs, the first of the four debates per item and framing
on 12 September, reused from the earlier comparison described at the end of
this subsection, and the other three on 22 September, so a change in the
served model between the runs is not excluded there, while the two Llama
collectives were run a day apart.

A seat objects when it rejects the moderator's synthesis or accepts it with a
modification, and the counter fires and flags the debate when at least two
of the three seats object. Accuracy is the share of debates whose integrated
verdict matches the crowd's, the fire rate the share of debates flagged,
routed accuracy the accuracy after each flagged debate's verdict is replaced
by the majority, on whether the writer is at fault, of three answers from
sonnet, the stronger judge of Section~\ref{sec-collective}, the collective's
verdict standing where those answers tie or none parses, and the routing
gain routed minus collective accuracy.
% seats reviewer, round 3 (2026-09-24): scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py load_tag (sonnet answers coded by
% verify_pillar3_headline.code, YTA and ESH at fault, NTA and NAH not, UNRESOLVED uncodable; majority of the
% codable ones, jv None on a tie or when none is codable); cg_sonnet_actuator_rows.csv, three
% claude-sonnet-4-6 standard answers per item and framing (498 x 3) (m-seats-05)
% scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py per_item (acc, fire = n_objectors >= 2, routed via
% verify_pillar3_headline.routed_ok, gain = routed - acc); prereg 16.28 readouts (a)-(d) (~l.8629-8637)
% objection = ACCEPT_WITH_MODIFICATION or REJECT, scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py l.1211; counter
% n_objectors >= 2 and judge ties to S2, scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py load_tag and
% scripts/verify_pillar3_headline.py routed_ok (appendix2-02); definitions moved to open this paragraph
% and the judge clause reworded (clarity loop 2, m-seats-03 (2) and (3)); "the stronger judge" as in
% Section sec-collective (whole-05)
Each measure is computed for each item as the mean over the item's remaining
debates in that collective, both framings and every repeat together, and
each contrast is the mean over items of the narrated minus the plain value,
with a 95 percent percentile interval from 8,000 bootstrap resamples that
draw items with replacement and recompute that mean (seed 20260922). Pairing
on items keeps differences in difficulty between items out of the interval,
since both collectives debate the same items.
% scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py per_item and paired (DRAWS 8000, SEED 20260922, percentiles 2.5 and
% 97.5 of the mean per-item difference over the items present in both collectives); prereg 16.28 RESULTS
% (~l.8979-8980)
% scripts/verify_pillar3_headline.py routed_ok and analyze_seat_scaffold.py load_tag (jv None when no judge
% code parses or they tie); paired() resamples item indices with replacement and takes the mean per-item
% difference (m-seats-03, clarity round 2)
Because the judge sees only the account, each item and framing has one cached
majority-of-three answer from sonnet that serves both collectives, so the
routed comparisons differ only in the collectives' own verdicts and in which
debates they flag.
% scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py load_tag (judge verdicts keyed by (arm, item_id) from
% cg_sonnet_actuator_rows.csv, model claude-sonnet-4-6, scaffold standard, the same file for both
% collectives); routing rule scripts/verify_pillar3_headline.py routed_ok; moved here from the design
% paragraph so that it follows the definition of the judge (clarity loop 2, appendix2-04)
A collective that flags more buys routed accuracy by consulting the judge
more often, so routed accuracy is also compared at matched coverage. The
collective with the higher per-debate fire rate, the narrated one on both
models, keeps a random subset of its flags, drawn from all its flagged
debates at once rather than item by item and sized to the other's per-debate
rate (254 flags in 1,676 plain debates on grok, 69 in 898 on Llama), 254 of
grok's 328 and 69 of Llama's 71, a debate that loses its flag
% seats reviewer, round 3 (2026-09-24): seat_scaffold_comparison.json per_model.<m>.matched_fire_rate 0.1516
% (= 254 / 1,676) grok and 0.0768 (= 69 / 898) Llama; scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py run (target = min of the two
% per-debate fire rates) and thinned_routed (keep_n = round(target x narrated debates), round(0.1516 x 1,677) = 254,
% round(0.0768 x 897) = 69) (m-seats-07)
keeping the collective's own verdict, while the other collective keeps every
flag, so its routed accuracy is unchanged by matching. Each item's routed
accuracy is averaged over 200 such subsets (seed 20260922) so that no single draw decides
% seats reviewer, round 3 (2026-09-24): scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py thinned_routed (one generator
% np.random.default_rng(SEED), SEED 20260922, THIN_SEEDS 200 successive subsets) (m-seats-07)
the result, and the averages are paired as above. Since every flag is kept
with the same chance, 254 in 328 on grok and 69 in 71 on Llama, an item's
expected routed accuracy under this thinning is its collective accuracy plus
its routing gain times that chance, so the comparison asks what the narrated
flags would buy if they fired only as often as the plain ones.
% identity (m-seats-02, clarity round 2, 2026-09-24): each of the F flags is kept with probability k/F, so by
% linearity E[routed_matched_i] = acc_i + (k/F) gain_i, which scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py
% thinned_routed estimates over THIN_SEEDS 200 (draws over all fired debates of the cell, not per item;
% an unkept flag routes as unfired, routed_ok returns ok); checked 2026-09-24 against
% seat_scaffold_comparison.json, grok 0.8971 closed form against routed_matched.narrated 0.8970, Llama
% 0.660 against 0.660; the plain (lower-firing) collective is passed through unthinned (keep_n equals its
% fired count, 254 of 1,676 grok and 69 of 898 Llama), so its routed_matched equals its routed (0.9166
% grok, 0.7056 Llama)
% prereg pre-spend review amendment to 16.28 (~l.8894-8899); scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py run
% (target = min of the two per-debate fire rates, seat_scaffold_comparison.json matched_fire_rate 0.1516
% grok = 254 / 1,676, 0.0768 Llama = 69 / 898) and thinned_routed (keep_n = round(target x debates),
% THIN_SEEDS 200, per-item mean over draws); narrated 328 of 1,677 (verify_pillar3_headline_grok.json)
% and 71 of 897 (verify_pillar3_headline_llama.json), recomputed with load_tag 2026-09-23
% prereg 16.28 RESULTS (~l.8979-8983) and pre-spend amendment (~l.8894-8899); scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py

The reading rule was registered before the new plain-seat debates were run
but after the earlier grok comparison's result had been seen, and its
matched-coverage measure was added by an amendment recorded before any of
their results was read.
% seats reviewer, round 3 (2026-09-24): prereg 16.28 registration ~l.8602-8607 cites the 16.16 result
% (+0.010 [-0.010, +0.029], fire 0.196 against 0.131) it was written after (m-seats-04)
It reads two measures on each model, the collective's accuracy and its
routed accuracy at matched coverage, and returns the first of four
readings, named at the head of this appendix, that holds. The first holds
when either measure favours plain seats with an interval excluding zero on
either model, the second when either favours narrated seats in that way,
the third when neither does but the routing gain or the fire rate differs
with an interval excluding zero, and the fourth, that nothing differs,
otherwise. Because the plain-seat reading is checked first, a
plain-favouring interval on one model would decide it even if the other
model's intervals favoured narrated seats, a case that did not arise, since
all four intervals favour plain seats. Its four intervals carry no
multiplicity correction. The readings as first registered, which counted
routed accuracy without matching, give the same result, since the
collective's accuracy alone favours plain seats on both models.
% seats reviewer, round 3 (2026-09-24): the four readings in words in the order of scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py
% reading() (fav_p, fav_n, gain or fire, INERT), names left to the glossary at the head of this appendix (style
% rule); "lie below zero" -> "favour plain seats" because the narrated-minus-plain convention is stated only in the
% next paragraph; readings as first registered = prereg 16.28 registration ~l.8641-8656 ((a) collective accuracy or
% (b) unmatched routed accuracy; PLAIN-BETTER on either model, reported as a reversal, ~l.8646-8648), under which
% seat_scaffold_comparison.json per_model.{grok,llama}.acc hi -0.0026 and -0.0144 (both favour plain) give
% PLAIN-BETTER, and no measure favours narrated seats (routed grok [-0.023, +0.015], Llama [-0.077, -0.011])
% (m-seats-03)

% routed_matched, PLAIN-BETTER checked first, no multiplicity adjustment) over REGISTERED_1628 = (grok, llama)

% ~l.8641-8648 (NOT-LOAD-BEARING, later NARRATION-LOAD-BEARING, requires that neither measure favour plain
% seats on the other model); seat_scaffold_comparison.json acc and routed_matched hi < 0 on grok and Llama
% (-0.0026, -0.0009, -0.0144, -0.0125) (m-seats-07)
Unless stated otherwise, every contrast in this subsection is narrated minus
plain, so a negative difference in accuracy favours plain seats. The
accuracy of the collective's own verdict differs by $-2.0$ points
$[-3.7, -0.3]$ on grok and $-4.2$ $[-7.0, -1.4]$ on Llama, and the accuracy
of the routed verdict at matched coverage by $-2.0$ $[-3.8, -0.1]$ and
$-4.6$ $[-7.8, -1.2]$, so plain chain-of-thought seats make the collective
more accurate on grok and Llama, the two models the rule was registered for,
the pre-declared plain-seat reading (Table~\ref{tab-app-seat}).

A third model, haiku, was added later under an amendment registered
before its run, with the same change on its 249-item set and
one debate per item and framing, and the rule above is applied to haiku on
its own, which leaves the registered reading on grok and Llama unchanged.
Read on the 491 of 498 debates both collectives completed, the narrated one
having lost seven to connection failures, 487 once
unparseable verdicts are dropped, it also finds plain seats better, carried
by routed accuracy at matched coverage ($-3.2$ $[-6.0, -0.3]$), while the
collective's own accuracy ($-3.0$ $[-6.4, +0.4]$) and its routed accuracy
without matching ($-1.0$ $[-3.8, +1.8]$) include zero, and narration
roughly doubles how often the flag fires (0.197 against 0.090, $+10.6$
$[+6.6, +14.5]$).

already includes zero.

With plain seats haiku's routing still raises accuracy, in readouts reported
without a registered reading. The counter fires on 45 of haiku's 498 plain
debates, and routing those to sonnet raises the collective's accuracy from
0.883 to 0.904, $+2.0$ $[+0.8, +3.4]$ points, against $+3.7$
$[+1.4, +5.9]$ with narrated seats, whereas the routing-gain row of
Table~\ref{tab-app-seat}, $+2.0$ $[-0.4, +4.6]$, is the narrated-minus-plain
difference in this gain on the 491 debates both collectives completed.

Descriptively, on verdicts that blame exactly one party the plain collective
is wrong 3.6 times as often where at least two seats object, a lift of
$+18.1$ $[+3.9, +34.1]$ points on 32 flagged debates, under the registered
minimum of 60 for reading a lift, and there the two advocates' objections
are positively correlated, $\phi = +0.419$ $[+0.285, +0.540]$ against
$+0.246$ with narrated seats, so the opposition found on grok does not
appear on haiku with either kind of seat.

Narration makes grok's counter fire more often, with a fire rate of 0.195 against
% seats reviewer, round 3 (2026-09-24): fire = n_objectors >= 2 per debate (scripts/analyze_seat_scaffold.py
% load_tag), not a per-seat objection rate; 'seats object more' -> 'counter fire more often' (m-seats-09)
0.152 ($+4.3$ $[+1.8, +6.9]$), which moves its routing gain, routed accuracy
minus the collective's own accuracy, from 5.4 to 7.0
points, a difference of $+1.6$ $[+0.0, +3.3]$ whose interval reaches zero,
and, without matched coverage, brings its routed accuracy to within 0.4 points
of the plain seats' ($-0.4$ $[-2.3, +1.5]$). On Llama narration has no
detectable effect on the fire rate ($+0.2$ $[-2.2, +2.7]$) or the routing gain
($-0.2$ $[-1.9, +1.4]$), the latter a contrast that, by a calculation made
after the results were seen, would detect with 80 percent power a difference
of 2.4 points, which is 2.80 times the standard error its interval implies,
half the interval's width over 1.96.
Every readout in this comparison is averaged over items, so the narrated
collective's fire rate reads 0.195 here and 19.6 percent, or 328 of 1,677
debates, in Section~\ref{sec-collective} and Table~\ref{tab-routing}.

The dissent sensor of Section~\ref{sec-collective}, the finding that the
collective's verdict is wrong far more often when a second seat objects,
survives without narration on grok. Plain seats lock to their sides as
tightly as narrated ones, the two advocates' opening at-fault rates lying
0.977 apart against 0.971 narrated (role-lock, defined in
Appendix~\ref{app-collective}). On verdicts that blame exactly one party the
advocates' objections stay opposed, with a phi coefficient, the correlation
between the writer advocate's and the other advocate's binary objection
indicators, of $-0.905$ against $-0.901$ narrated. On those verdicts the
collective is still wrong 6.4 times as often where at least two seats object
as where at most one does (0.319 against 0.050), a lift, the first error rate
minus the second, of $+26.9$ points $[+11.8, +41.2]$ over 72 flagged debates,

Appendix~\ref{app-collective}, against 9.8 times with narration. Surviving
here means that the plain-seat lift's interval still lies above zero, not
that it matches the narrated lift, and the two lifts are not compared with a
paired interval. On Llama the same readout had 53 flagged debates, under
the registered minimum of 60 flagged debates for reading a lift, and is not
read.

Neither model shows a difference between narrated and plain seats in the
verdict's shift toward the user. The shift is measured here not by the
criterion $c$ of Table~\ref{tab-app-z3} but as the share of verdicts excusing
the writer when the user is the writer minus that share in the third-person
account, on each item where the crowd found the writer at fault, and its
difference, narrated minus plain, is $-2.7$ $[-8.4, +2.5]$ on all 86 such
items for grok and $-2.2$ $[-6.5, +1.6]$ on all 92 for Llama.

\caption{\textbf{Plain chain-of-thought seats make the collective more

Each readout is averaged first over an item's debates whose verdicts parse
and then over items, and is shown for each collective as a proportion and as

8,000 bootstrap resamples of the items both collectives share (grok 210,

thins the flags of the collective that fires more, the narrated one on all
three models, at random to the other's per-debate fire rate, averaging over 200
draws, so that both route the same share of debates, and leaves the plain
collective's flags as they are, so its two routed rows agree. The routing
gain is routed minus collective accuracy, and the fire rate the share of
debates flagged. The registered reading rests on collective accuracy and
routed accuracy at matched coverage.}

\subsection{The collective's prompts, verbatim}
\label{app-prompts}

Every prompt of the collective of Section~\ref{sec-collective}, of its
routing judge and of the validation judge of Section~\ref{sec-single} is
printed below from the modules that ran them, with the account replaced by
the placeholder \texttt{<POST TEXT>} and prior-round text by placeholders in
angle brackets. Each debate makes 17 calls in seven stages
(Table~\ref{tab-app-stages}), the seats' system prompt is the scaffold text
alone, and everything else, the framing wrapper, the glossary, the role
brief, the prior rounds and the forced verdict line, arrives in the user
turn. The routing judge, sonnet, the stronger judge of
Section~\ref{sec-collective}, answers the account in the debate's framing
three times under the brief unscaffolded prompt used on the crowd-labelled
set, under which the solo of Section~\ref{sec-collective} also answers.
That prompt asks for at most four sentences of reasoning and carries neither
NoT nor CoT. It is the prompt of the set's original baseline run on haiku,
nano and grok, kept unchanged so that the run's answers could be reused, as
they are for the solo on haiku and grok. The judge's verdict is the
majority of those of its three answers coded at fault or not at fault, a tie
or no codable answer
keeping the collective's verdict, and because it answers each item and
framing once, that verdict replaces the collective's on every flagged debate
of the pair. Lines longer than 78 characters are wrapped for print, and each
continuation line is indented four spaces, an indent that is not part of the
prompt, whose own lines are indented by at most two.

\subsection{Pre-registration, deviations and corrections}
\label{app-prereg}

Most results in this paper are read from pre-registered analyses. The
exceptions are the original four-model single-agent comparison and the
false-theorem affirmation contrast on nano, whose responses predate the
pre-registration, and the readouts the text labels exploratory, not
pre-registered, or proposed after the data were seen. Several registered
numbers were also corrected or withdrawn along the way. This subsection
records each correction under a heading naming what was corrected, and
Table~\ref{tab-app-provenance} names the pre-registration entry behind each
result.
% entry numbers moved from the paragraph headings to a comment under each heading (clarity loop 2,
% appendix2-18; addendum numbers only in comments and Table tab-app-provenance)
% prereg 16.15.7 registration (~l.3987-3991, no new claim) and 16.15.5 registration (~l.3969-3976,
% reporting rule written after the BrokenMath runs); pillar1_headline.json (haiku, sonnet, nano and
% grok June responses)

\paragraph{Practice and scope.}
The pre-registration is one append-only document begun on 31 August 2026,
called the log in this appendix. It is our own log, kept under version
control in the project repository rather than filed with a public registry,
so the order of each registration, its model calls and its results rests on
the log's dates and commit history, and the log will be released, redacted,
with the code (Reproducibility statement).
% Guidance_Documents/prereg_embodiment_community.md under git (first commits ca5e805 and 07e597d, 2026-08-31),
% e.g. 5056365 2026-09-22 20:31 registering 17.7, 17.8 and 17.9 BEFORE any call, b4f3d23 21:14 pre-spend
% review amendment, 5df9352 21:56 17.7 amendment 2 and 17.10, 2325168 23:49 17.8 RESULTS; the log header
% (l.1-4) names no public registry and OSF appears in Guidance_Documents only as the ELEPHANT data source (PI
% to confirm no registry filing); release as in sections/statements.tex Reproducibility statement ("The log
% will be released with names ... redacted", PI todo there) (clarity loop 2, m-stats-03); "called the log"
% (appendix2-34)
Each entry, called an addendum, states its hypothesis, estimator, spending
ceiling, stop rule, which says when a run stops,
guards and pre-declared readings before any model call, results are appended
below it, and results blocks end with the statement that nothing above them
is edited. Registered guards, typically shares of truncated or verdict-less
outputs of at most 5 percent, must pass before any number is read, and a run
that fails is not read. The single-agent guards registered before the form tests of
Appendix~\ref{app-form} did not check truncation during generation, and until
the audit of the log's statements against their sources that led to the
4,096-token regeneration of Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} the log noted it only for grok's chain-of-thought responses
and nano's empty ones, which is how most of the defect of
Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} went unrecorded.

% replay); scripts/run_crowdgold_aita.py MAX_TRUNCATION_SHARE = MAX_NOVERDICT_SHARE = 0.05;
% 17.7 registration guards (~l.8780-8782, "truncation (finish reason) reported per arm");
% pre-spend amendment (~l.8826-8838, grok CoT 42 percent, nano budget); 17.10 registration
% (~l.8947-8953); registration blocks such as 17.7 (~l.8782) and 17.8 (~l.8731) end without the
% "Nothing above this line is edited" line
The CoT and NoT responses of the four original generators (haiku, sonnet,
nano and grok) were generated in June 2026 and their scoring was corrected
in August, both before the log existed, so their drops enter the log only
as a results note that made no model calls and makes no new claim.

% prereg 16.15.7 registration (~l.3987-3991, "No new claim") and RESULTS (~l.4593-4623)
Prospectively registered evidence for the single-agent validation result
begins with the log entries of 21 September 2026, which added Llama, Mistral
and DeepSeek, and the judge panel, regeneration, form, persona, length,
framing, baseline, human-reference, section-knockout, one-line and pushback
tests of Appendices~\ref{app-judges} to~\ref{app-pushback} are all
prospectively registered, the second 4,096-token run and the framing,
baseline and human-reference tests after earlier results had been read but
before any of their calls (Table~\ref{tab-app-provenance} lists each entry).

\begin{table}[t]
\caption{\textbf{Verdict shift toward the user on the crowd-labelled
accounts.} Signal-detection criterion $c = -(z(H) + z(F))/2$ and
discriminability $d' = z(H) - z(F)$ in the third person and with the user as
the writer, with the hit rate $H$ and false-alarm rate $F$, where
$z$ is the inverse of the standard normal cumulative distribution function. A verdict of at fault counts as a hit
on a crowd-at-fault item and as a false alarm on a crowd-not-at-fault item,
so a higher $c$ means the model calls the writer at fault less readily. The
shift is $c$ with the user as the writer minus $c$ in the third person, with
its 95 percent item-bootstrap interval (249 items, three samples per item,
1,000 draws), and a positive shift means fewer at-fault verdicts once the
user is the writer. Brief is the unscaffolded prompt used on this set,
capped at four sentences of reasoning, not the chain-of-thought (CoT) prompt
of Figure~\ref{fig:prompt}. The registered coding counts the forum's verdict
that everyone is at fault as at fault, and the two alternative codings,
which leave that verdict out or count it as not at fault, are in the
artefacts.}
\label{tab-app-z3}
\centering

% \footnotesize and groups c, d', H and F under the two framings with the shift last

\end{table}

% '|as_asker' criterion, d_prime, hit_rate, fa_rate; decomposition '<model>|<prompt>|PERSON' delta,
% ci_low, ci_high); n_rows 3,735 = 249 items x 3 samples x 5 conditions per model; n_boot 1,000
% (scripts/analyze_crowdgold_sdt.py l.183, seed 44); sdt() c = -(z(H)+z(F))/2, d' = z(H)-z(F),
% log-linear correction, hit = at-fault verdict on a crowd-at-fault (YTA) item; ESH_CODINGS published,
% dropped, not_at_fault; scripts/run_crowdgold_aita.py BREVITY ("at most four sentences of
