\paragraph{The verdict shift toward the user and false-theorem affirmation.}
% analysis block Z3 and Addendum 16.15.5 (appendix2-18)
Two single-agent results of Section~\ref{sec-single} were registered only
after their responses existed. The comparison on the verdict's shift toward the
% clarity loop 3 (appendix2-37): prereg Z3 registration ~l.7217-7225 (analysis of responses generated earlier for the
% collective) and 16.15.5 registration ~l.3969-3976 (reporting rule written after the BrokenMath runs)
user, the shift in the criterion $c$ of Table~\ref{tab-app-z3}, reuses two
sets of responses generated earlier for the collective, the NoT answers of
the content-filter screen that fixed each model's item set and the brief
unscaffolded answers that serve as the solo of Appendix~\ref{app-collective},
and was registered before analysis, not before generation. Its comparator is
the brief unscaffolded prompt used on the crowd-labelled set, which asks for
at most four sentences of reasoning and has no step-by-step instruction, and
Mistral's shift under that comparator appears only under the registered
coding of ESH, the forum's verdict that everyone involved is at fault.
% prereg Z3 registration ~l.7217-7220 (NoT = cg_screen_* narrative records k = 3, CoT =
% cg_<short>_standard_k3_rows.csv) (appendix2-28, whole-32); sentence restructured (appendix2-19) and ESH
% named as in the glossary of Appendix app-pushback (appendix2-31)
Because ESH blames everyone involved rather than the writer alone, how to
code it is a choice. The registered coding counts ESH verdicts as at fault,
and the two alternatives leave them out or count them as not at fault. Under
the alternatives, Mistral's shift under the brief prompt moves from $+0.13$
$[+0.01, +0.27]$ to $+0.05$ $[-0.11, +0.22]$ and $-0.03$ $[-0.18, +0.12]$,
while its shift under NoT, $+0.30$ under the registered coding
(Table~\ref{tab-app-z3}), is $+0.27$ $[+0.11, +0.48]$ and $+0.24$
$[+0.08, +0.44]$. On Llama both prompts keep the shift when ESH is left out
($+0.15$ for the brief prompt and $+0.22$ for NoT) and lose it when ESH
counts as not at fault ($+0.09$ $[-0.07, +0.24]$ for the brief prompt and
$+0.15$ $[-0.02, +0.32]$ for NoT) (Table~\ref{tab-app-z3}).
% ESH coding choice: scripts/analyze_crowdgold_sdt.py ESH_CODINGS published (at fault), dropped, not_at_fault
% (appendix2-38; the proposed rationale 'whether it finds the writer at fault is a choice' reworded, since ESH does
% find the writer at fault and the choice is how to score a verdict that does not single the writer out)
% figures/tab_z3.tex Mistral NoT shift +0.30 [+0.17, +0.46]; z3_sdt_mistral.json codings.published decomposition
% 'mistral|narrative_cot|PERSON' +0.299 (appendix2-30)
% z3_sdt_{mistral,llama}.json codings.{published,dropped,not_at_fault}.decomposition
% "<model>|standard|PERSON (3rd -> as_asker)" and "|narrative_cot|PERSON" (Mistral standard +0.127
% [+0.008, +0.267], +0.046 [-0.107, +0.218], -0.033 [-0.184, +0.116]; Mistral narrative_cot +0.299,
% +0.272 [+0.112, +0.480], +0.238 [+0.076, +0.440]; Llama standard +0.256, +0.154 [+0.012, +0.315],
% +0.086 [-0.067, +0.236]; Llama narrative_cot +0.270, +0.221 [+0.043, +0.405], +0.148 [-0.021,
% +0.316]); scripts/analyze_crowdgold_sdt.py ESH_CODINGS published, dropped, not_at_fault; prereg Z3
% RESULTS ~l.7294 (published coding registered) (W46)
The false-theorem affirmation result, that NoT raises the share of false
theorem statements from the BrokenMath benchmark that nano affirms as true
(Section~\ref{sec-single}), comes from responses that predate the
pre-registration, was read under a reporting rule registered afterwards, and
was measured on nano alone. NoT affirmed the false statement on 0.223 of its
600 attempts against 0.157 under CoT, $+6.7$ points $[+3.3, +10.3]$ with
answers giving no verdict counted as not affirming and $+7.0$
$[+3.0, +11.5]$ among answers that give one, both intervals from 10,000
draws, but no verdict came on 34.5 percent of CoT's attempts and 27.8
percent of NoT's, and worst-case bounds that score those answers in
whichever direction lowers the difference most and then in whichever raises
it most run from $-27.8$ to $+34.5$ points and cross zero.
% prereg 16.15.5 RESULTS (~l.4531-4560; reading asks for the registered contrast, the committed-only
% contrast and the Manski bounds in one sentence); bm_embody_analysis.json S0_narrative_minus_standard
% (0.0667 [0.0333, 0.1033], draws 10000), reference.standard_cot p 0.157 missing_rate 0.345 n 600,
% reference.narrative_cot p 0.2233 missing_rate 0.2783 n 600; committed-only +0.070 [+0.030, +0.115]
% (10,000 draws, seed 13) and Manski [-0.278, +0.345] from the 16.15.5 RESULTS (m-stats-12)
The effect belongs to the first answer. Asked to narrate a second time with
its first narration in view, nano affirms fewer false statements ($-7.6$
points $[-10.4, -4.5]$, second pass minus first, on the items with a usable
first answer), while re-answering under CoT changes nothing
($-0.3$ $[-2.5, +2.0]$). In an
older test on BrokenMath proofs from June, scored by a haiku judge alone, NoT
raised the share judged to accept the false statement on nano from CoT's
0.547 to 0.693 but lowered it on grok from CoT's 0.507 to 0.388.

\paragraph{The split by verdict type and the two-objection threshold.}
% Addenda 16.1, 16.12 and 16.15.1 (appendix2-18)
The split of the dissent counter by verdict type, whose stratum of verdicts
that blame exactly one party supplies the 9.8 times figure of
Section~\ref{sec-collective}, was not declared before the grok debates
existed. It was proposed on 12 September 2026 in a note that made no model
calls, which predicted, and confirmed on the grok debates
already collected, that verdicts blaming both parties or neither fire the
counter far more often than the rest. The lift within verdicts that blame
exactly one party, called one-loser verdicts in the log, was read the same
day under a registration made in answer to a finding of an internal
adversarial audit of the drafted results, a different review from the
pre-spend review above. That audit's recomputation from the cached debates
had already produced the values before the registration was written (0.387
against 0.039 over 93 flagged debates), and the registration records them
above its reading rule. That rule requires this paper to say that
the pooled lift over all verdict types, 4.4 times ($+32.0$ points
$[+23.9, +40.4]$ over 328 flagged debates of 1,677), mixes the dissent on
one-party verdicts with the verdict type on the rest.
On grok the split is therefore a reading proposed after the data were
seen. The prospective test is the five later conditions, grok with the exchange cut,
haiku, Llama, Mistral and DeepSeek, each registered with that one-party lift as a named readout before
any call, and on haiku the interval includes zero.
% W13 (2026-09-23): prereg 16.12 header and table (~l.3653-3681, zero spend, 2026-09-12, cached 1,680
% debates, "exactly ONE loser" prediction, CONFIRMED); 16.15 header and 16.15.1 registration
% (~l.3888-3931, "registered 2026-09-12, $0", hostile-reviewer finding C3, "[replay: 0.387 vs 0.039,
% +0.348, 93 firings]", Rule 1 "the pooled 4.4x ... mixes two signals"); 16.15.1 RESULTS pooled row
% (~l.4277) and router_decomposition.json counter_lift_by_stratum.pooled (0.415 vs 0.095, 4.37x,
% +0.320 [+0.239, +0.404], 328 of 1,677); grok panel L1 RESULTS 2026-09-01 (~l.580) and Addendum 16
% (~l.3027, 2026-09-09) predate both; prospective registrations 16.23 (~l.6735-6763, "within-one-loser
% lift at >= 60 fired" as a named readout), 16.24 (~l.6876, 2026-09-14, "exactly as 16.23"), 16.25
% (~l.6773), 16.26 (~l.8142, 2026-09-22), 16.27 (~l.8178, 2026-09-22, "identical in kind to 16.26");
% haiku interval verify_pillar3_headline_haiku_249.json (+0.035 [-0.041, +0.120]) and prereg 16.23
% RESULTS ~l.6937; the "proposed after the data were seen" label for this stratum is now in
% Section sec-collective ("proposed and first read on grok's debates after they were collected")
% (collective-11, AA-1); split sentence (AA-5, whole-14) and wording (appendix2-29)
% the audit: prereg 16.15 header (~l.3888-3898, "An adversarial audit of the drafted results section (three
% auditors ...) working only from the record plus replays of cached calls", 2026-09-12), distinct from the
% pre-spend review of 2026-09-22 (appendix2-21)
% the note is Addendum 16.12, the registration Addendum 16.15.1, and the five conditions 16.25 (exchange
% cut), 16.23 (haiku), 16.24 (Llama), 16.26 (Mistral) and 16.27 (DeepSeek)
% entry numbers moved from the prose to this comment (house rule: addendum numbers only in comments
% and Table tab-app-provenance; headings cleared in clarity loop 2), integrating editor, clarity loop 1 (2026-09-24)
The two-objection threshold was likewise fixed after grok's debates had been
collected. The flag registered first, in an early entry of the log, fired
when the neutral seat objected or an advocate objected to a synthesis that
did not go against its side. An audit of the collected grok debates that
made no model calls, on 9 September 2026, then found that the plain count of
at least two objecting seats fires on 328 of the 1,677 debates, every one of them also flagged by
the earlier rule, which flags 9 more, and routes to the haiku judge nearly as
well (routed accuracy 0.880 against the earlier rule's 0.881), so the count
became the registered flag for every later condition.
% prereg Addendum 3 gate ~l.629-634 (registered 2026-09-01, analyze_loop_step.flagged: neutral objected
% OR a stake-bearing seat objected although the synthesis did not undermine its stake); Addendum 16
% (~l.3027-3041, registered and run 2026-09-09) and 16.1 (~l.3042-3084: n_objectors >= 2 fires 328,
% composite 337, intersection 328, blind-only 0, composite-only 9, composed accuracy 0.8801 and 0.8813
% with the cached haiku majority-of-3); the count as the registered flag for later conditions: 16.5

% (m-collective-06; placed at the end of the paragraph rather than inside it, for the flow); composed
% accuracy 0.8801 for the count and 0.8813 for the composite (appendix2-32)
% the early gate is Addendum 3's and the audit Addendum 16.1
% grok debates collected by 2026-09-01 (E5 RESULTS ~l.526), the count registered after the 2026-09-09 audit (16.1
% ~l.3042-3084) (clarity loop 3, appendix2-41); "grok with the exchange cut" (appendix2-42); sentence split so that

\paragraph{Judge input truncation (August 2026 correction).}
% and Addendum 16.15.7 (appendix2-18)
The production scorer at first showed the judge only the first 4,000
characters of each response. Far more NoT than CoT responses exceed that
length, on haiku 121 of 150 against 0 of 150 and on sonnet 140 of 150 against
5 of 150, and NoT places its decision last, so the cut removed the closing
affirmation and inflated the reductions as first computed.
% prereg CORRECTION to 17.2 and 17.6 (~l.8688-8693, over-4,000 counts); scripts/elephant_scorers.py
% advice[:4000]; Guidance_Documents/sycophancy_scenario_redesign.md 1b-RESULT-3
% the uncorrected reductions predate the log (Guidance_Documents/sycophancy_scenario_redesign.md
% 1b-RESULT-3, 2026-08-18, before the log opened 2026-08-31) (appendix2-30)
Every judge-scored single-agent number in this paper comes from the judge
reading the full generated text. The
four-model drops as recorded in the log substituted fresh full-text scores only for
responses over 4,000 characters and kept stored scores for shorter ones, and
the uniform full-text rescore behind the five-judge panel of
Appendix~\ref{app-judges} reproduces them to within 3.5
points, the largest gap being nano's, where the panel pairs 116 items against
the log's unpaired comparison (Appendix~\ref{app-judges}).
% "as recorded in the log" and "the log's" for "registered": these drops were first computed in August, before the
% log opened (1b-RESULT-3), and enter the log only as a results note, prereg 16.15.7 (clarity loop 3, appendix2-43)
% integrating editor, clarity loop 3 (2026-09-24): "A claim that" had been swallowed by the comment line above, so
% it is restored at the head of the next sentence, wording unchanged
A claim that
NoT lowers the validation rate on ELEPHANT's own split of forum posts judged
at fault, a split the crowd-labelled set of Section~\ref{sec-collective} does
not use, did not survive the correction, the reduction collapsing on haiku
and reversing sign on sonnet, and is withdrawn.
% prereg 16.15.7 RESULTS (~l.4596-4603, 936 replaced); 17.8 RESULTS (~l.9067-9070, nano -37.9 on 116
% paired vs -41.4 unpaired = 3.5, sonnet -19.3 vs -22.3 = 3.0); pillar1_headline.json aita_yta_status WITHDRAWN
% W39 (2026-09-23): 1b-RESULT-4 ~l.283-296 (corrected NoT minus CoT on aita_yta: haiku -4.7 from -20.0,
% sonnet +10.7 from -6.7, nano -27.2, grok -18.9; 93-99 percent of NoT responses truncated there);
% fact_sheet topics[0].facts[51]; claim named (haiku -20.0 to -4.7, sonnet -6.7 to +10.7) (appendix2-20)

\paragraph{The withdrawn alternate-judge tables.}
% correction to Addenda 17.2 and 17.6 (appendix2-18)
The first alternate-judge tables, one with nano and Llama judges and a later
one with a grok judge, re-scored with the new judge only the responses over
4,000 characters and left shorter ones at the production judge's score.
Because the share over that length differs sharply between CoT and NoT, each
such drop compared a mostly production-judge-scored CoT rate with a mostly
alternate-scored NoT rate, most extremely on haiku and sonnet, the two
generators on which both the Llama and the grok judge appeared to reverse the
sign (the Llama judge also appeared to reverse it on nano).
% prereg CORRECTION to 17.2 and 17.6 (~l.8682-8701); scripts/analyze_length_matched_elephant.py
% apply_corrected_scores (skips len <= 4000); 17.2 RESULTS table (~l.7768-7776, Llama judge haiku
% +18.7, sonnet +19.3, nano +11.7); 17.6 RESULTS (~l.8213-8215, grok judge haiku +18.0, sonnet +20.6).
% The CORRECTION text itself says "the two generators", omitting nano
Both tables are withdrawn as evidence either way, together with a diagnosis
that the Llama and grok judges had an acquiescence defect, a validation rate
pinned near its ceiling whatever the response's content, and the five-judge
panel of Appendix~\ref{app-judges}, in which every judge scores every
response, replaces them.
% acquiescence defect: prereg 17.6 header ~l.8111 ("validation rate pinned near ceiling regardless of
% content") (appendix2-31)
The panel's rule, a negative drop with an interval excluding zero on at
least six of seven generators under every judge, was registered after the
withdrawal, and the withdrawn comparison's stricter rule, every generator
under every judge, reads the panel the same way, as judge-dependent.
% prereg 17.2 registration (~l.7125-7127, JUDGE-ROBUST iff every judge gives a negative drop with a CI
% excluding 0 on every generator), CORRECTION to 17.2 and 17.6 (~l.8682, before 17.8 in the append-only
% log), 17.8 registration (~l.8721-8724, at least six of seven) and its pre-spend amendment
% (~l.8858-8860, all five judges read under the same six-of-seven rule); the panel fails both rules,
% sonnet reversed under four judges in both sets (judge_panel_full_oeq_<judge>.json,
% headline_rep4k_readout.json) (W17)
That panel still finds rises with intervals above
zero on sonnet and nano under the Llama judge and on haiku and sonnet under
the grok judge (Table~\ref{tab-app-judge-drops}), smaller than the withdrawn
tables showed except on nano under the Llama judge ($+13.8$ against $+11.7$).
% judge_panel_full_oeq_{Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct,grok-4-1-fast-reasoning}.json per_generator.<gen>
% .narrative_cot (Llama judge sonnet +7.4, nano +13.8; grok judge haiku +11.4, sonnet +14.0)

\paragraph{The removed judge screen.}
% Addendum 17.8 and its pre-spend amendment (appendix2-18)
As registered, the five-judge panel carried an acquiescence screen that
would have excluded any judge whose validation rate
exceeded 0.85 on at least 80 percent of the generator-by-prompt rates or whose
rates spanned less than 0.20. The pre-spend review removed this screen as
circular, since it is computed on the rates whose difference is tested and
would exclude exactly the judges that see no reduction, and applied as written
it would have excluded the Llama judge, whose rates on all scored responses
span 0.17, though not on the paired rates, which span 0.21.
% prereg 17.8 registration (~l.8715-8717) and pre-spend amendment (~l.8854-8861);
% judge_panel_full_oeq_Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct.json ceiling_descriptive (range 0.173 on
% per_generator.<gen>.rates, would_have_been_excluded true; grok false); paired rate_a / rate_b span
% 0.759 to 0.971 (0.213), 11 of 14 (78.6 percent) above 0.85, so neither screen condition is met
The registered threshold for JUDGE-ROBUST, a negative drop excluding zero on
at least six of seven generators under every judge, is looser than the rule
registered for the first alternate-judge tables, which required every
generator, and it was written after the now withdrawn table had shown sonnet
failing under the nano judge. Under either
rule the reading is JUDGE-DEPENDENT. The reference-label check was registered
as an amendment after the five drop tables were seen and before it was
computed.
% prereg 17.2 registration (~l.7125-7127), 17.8 registration (~l.8723-8725), pre-spend amendment
% (~l.8858-8860), 17.8 amendment 2 header (~l.9032); "Acquiescence screen" 17.8 registration ~l.8715
% (appendix2-31, m-judges-14)

\paragraph{Generation-cap truncation, DeepSeek's missing floor and the persona line.}
% Addendum 17.7 amendment 2 and Addendum 17.10 (appendix2-18)
The audit that checked the log's statements against their sources found two
confounds in the single-agent result itself, truncation during generation, to
which DeepSeek's missing token floor belongs, and the persona line. The
original responses had been cut at their generation caps far more often than
the log stated (Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}), so the claim that every response
was scored in full was true of the judge's input and not of the responses,
and the regeneration of Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} generated the responses of
all seven generators again at 4,096 tokens.
% prereg 17.7 amendment 2 and Addendum 17.10 (~l.8931-8975); 17.10 header (~l.8931, 'a sourced fact-sheet audit
% of the record found two confounds'), the persona line one block and the truncation block (~l.8947-8955)
% including the missing DeepSeek floor (appendix2-22); "the log" (appendix2-34); naming (appendix1-03)
The same audit found that DeepSeek never received the 8,192-token completion
floor its registration said it carried, because the list of floored
deployments in the generation code is empty, so its original responses ran at
1,024 tokens for CoT and 2,048 for NoT.
% prereg 17.1 registration (~l.7091-7092) against 17.10 registration (~l.8952-8954);
% scripts/generators.py FOUNDRY_V1_REASONING = ()
The second confound is the persona line. CoT and NoT differ in it as well as
in the five sections, and no earlier condition isolated it. The section
knockouts and the checklist keep NoT's persona while the one-line and
length-matched prompts keep CoT's, so comparisons across those tests also
differ in persona, and the second amendment to the form tests added CoT with
only the persona swapped
(Appendix~\ref{app-form}).
% prereg 17.7 amendment 2 (~l.8933-8945); scripts/scaffold_permutations.py and scripts/run_elephant.py

\paragraph{Pooling over runs.}
% Addendum 17.10 amendment, run 2 (appendix2-44)
The registration of the second 4,096-token run asked for the sonnet and haiku
drops to be stated as estimates pooled over the two 4,096-token runs, and
after that run was seen the paper instead pools over every run of each
generator (Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}).
% prereg "Addendum 17.10 amendment, run 2" registration and RESULTS; run_stability.json pooled.runs
% (m-replicate-06)

\paragraph{Sample seed.}
% Addendum 17.7 registration (appendix2-18)
Earlier text in the log, including its shared description of the
single-agent tests' setup and the first results entry on the original
responses, calls the 150-item sample seed 42. The cached items match
seed 44 on all 150 items and seed 42 on 4, and the loader sets seed 44, so
the sample is seed 44. The bootstrap seed 20260822 is unrelated to it.

\paragraph{The length control's launch.}
% Addendum 17.4 (appendix2-18)
The length control was registered on 21 September with a condition that it
run only if the three tests registered just before it finished with at most
\$95 of measured spend between them. It
was launched under the principal investigator's batch authorisation of 22
September, which superseded that condition, and the pre-spend review changed its
comparator to the same-run CoT and, before any call, made its three
registered readings mutually exclusive and fixed the order in which they are
checked.
% prereg 17.4 registration header (~l.7180), 17.4 launch (~l.8733), pre-spend amendment (~l.8870-8875);
% the three tests are Addenda 17.1 to 17.3; 17.4 header 'runs only if 17.1 to 17.3 close at or under $95 in
% measured spend' (appendix2-42)
% entry numbers moved from the prose to this comment (house rule: addendum numbers only in comments
% and Table tab-app-provenance; headings cleared in clarity loop 2), integrating editor, clarity loop 1 (2026-09-24)

\paragraph{The pushback guard.}
% Addendum 17.9 guard note (appendix2-18)
Haiku's pushback responses failed the no-verdict guard, and the rule for what
a failed model means for the pooled readout was fixed after the haiku and nano
results had been read and before any grok or Llama response existed
(Appendix~\ref{app-pushback}).
% prereg 17.9 guard note (~l.8910-8928, recorded after the haiku and nano cells, with nano's NULL
% reading, and before any grok or Llama 17.9 call)

\paragraph{The one-line instruction's reading.}
% Addendum 17.5 (appendix2-18)
The one-line instruction to consider everyone affected raised validation,
$+6.1$ points $[+2.1, +10.2]$ pooled over haiku, grok and nano. The registered
rule returns STRUCTURE-NEEDED, its branch for an instruction that produces no
drop from CoT with an interval excluding zero, and a rise meets that
condition. The log labels the outcome REVERSED, a label added after the run
and marked as such in the analysis script, and this paper reports the rise
itself rather than either label.
% prereg 17.5 registration (~l.7996-8009, STRUCTURE-NEEDED "iff not_counterparty_only shows no drop
% from CoT with a CI excluding zero at all") and RESULTS (~l.8022-8041, which call the outcome
% unanticipated); scripts/analyze_counterparty_causal.py reading() docstring (l.122-131, unpatched
% logic returns STRUCTURE-NEEDED); counterparty_causal_analysis.json
The section-knockout and one-line tests of Appendix~\ref{app-form} compare
new responses with the June ones on all three generators, and the nano and
grok contrasts also carry the generation-cap truncation described in
Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}, nano's also a budget mismatch, since its June
responses shared a 2,048-token budget with its hidden reasoning while its new
ones have a floor of 8,192 tokens.

% TOTAL budget before the 8,192 reasoning floor, any new nano arm gets the floor; 'The 17.3 and 17.5 nano contrasts
% against the June cells carry the same budget mismatch'); scripts/generators.py eff_tokens = max(max_tokens, 8192);
% grok's is the CoT cut at 1,024 tokens (17.10 registration ~l.8948-8950), as in Appendix app-form

% counterparty_causal_analysis.json per_generator.grok n_cot 146 (the June cell); all three generators'

Their pooled intervals resample generator-by-item pairs, although the
section-knockout registration asked for an item's responses on all three
generators to be resampled together (Appendix~\ref{app-form}).
% prereg 17.3 registration (~l.7153-7155); scripts/analyze_knockout_social.py and
% scripts/analyze_counterparty_causal.py build() (item_id f"{gen}:{i}") (m-form-06 (c))

\paragraph{Analyses added after the data were seen.}
Seven analyses in this appendix were written after every
result they read had been seen, each replaying cached scores, verdict rows
and existing artefacts with no model call, and each is recorded in a dated
note at the end of the pre-registration log. They are the paired comparison
of the collective with its own solo model and the DeepSeek-judge check of
the routing gain (Appendix~\ref{app-collective}), the inter-judge kappa and
the production judge's test-retest agreement (Appendix~\ref{app-judges}),
the run-to-run comparison (Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k}), the descriptives and
brackets of the knockout and one-line tests (Appendix~\ref{app-form}) and
the Holm corrections and minimum detectable effects reported beside the
registered readings. None changes a registered reading. They follow three
earlier additions made after data were seen, the reference-label check of
Appendix~\ref{app-judges}, the REVERSED label of the one-line instruction and
the pooling rule of the pushback guard. A readout labelled not
pre-registered or exploratory is reported with no decision rule registered
for it, and one labelled proposed after the data were seen is read under a
rule written after its data had been seen.
% clarity loop 3 (appendix2-46): prereg ~l.723 ('Exploratory (not registered, reported for the record)');
% routing_certification_{haiku,llama,mistral,deepseek}.json status 'not_applicable (exploratory)'; post hoc block ~l.9394
% ('descriptive ... None of them changes a registered reading'); 16.10 RESULTS ~l.4190 ('Observed, not pre-declared');
% the verdict-type split, prereg 16.12 and 16.15.1 (~l.3653-3681, ~l.3888-3931, rule written after the audit's replay)
Other readouts that the text labels
not pre-registered, exploratory or proposed after the data were seen, such
as the contrast of identical and opposed seats and the exploratory routing
readouts of Appendix~\ref{app-collective} and the split by verdict type
above, are marked where they appear.

% earlier additions as in the Section 4 source comment beside "later additions" (17.8 amendment 2,

% and Table tab-app-provenance; headings cleared in clarity loop 2), integrating editor, clarity loop 1 (2026-09-24)

\paragraph{An earlier verbose control.}
Earlier design notes and entries in the log described a chain-of-thought
variant that asks for detailed
reasoning from every relevant perspective as a length-matched or token-matched
control. It is neither. It contains three of NoT's sections in prose, it ran
at 1,024 tokens against NoT's 2,048, and every one of grok's 139 responses to
it ended at that cap. This paper does not use it, and its length control is
the length-matched prompt of Appendix~\ref{app-form}.
% scripts/run_phase1_quartet.py PROMPTS['standard_cot_verbose']; scripts/run_elephant.py
% _max_tokens_for; prereg 17.4 registration (~l.7193-7194); completion tokens in
% elephant_gen_oeq_grok-4-1-fast-reasoning_standard_cot_verbose_*.json (139 of 139 at 1,024)
% prereg ~l.3001 ("verbose (matched length to narrative ...)"); "token-matched" in
% Guidance_Documents/audience_paraphrase_taxonomy_proposal.md ~l.389 (appendix2-30)

\paragraph{Guard failures and spend overruns.}
Three addenda overran their ceilings, and each overrun is recorded where it
happened. An early screen of how firmly each model's seats hold to their assigned
stakes, not reported here, spent \$70.47 against \$40 because
its correction required regenerating most calls at higher token caps, and the
principal investigator authorised the overrun. The second stage of a routing
test on a second task family, the Scruples Dilemmas, a part of Scruples
separate from the Anecdotes of Section~\ref{sec-collective} and also not
reported here, spent about \$9.41 against \$8, a cost-model miss. The DeepSeek collective
spent \$93.70 against
\$90 and reports the \$3.70 as an overage.
% prereg Addendum 6 (~l.1297-1301), Addendum 12 Stage 2 (~l.2578-2589), 16.27 RESULTS header (~l.8275);
% prereg Addendum 6 heading ~l.1161 ("the stake-grip screen") and final total ~l.1297-1301; Addendum 12
% heading ~l.2265 ("a second task family for Addendum 4's actuator ladder", Scruples Dilemmas ~l.2284),
% Stage 2 ~l.2486 and spend ~l.2578-2589 (appendix2-34); the three are Addendum 6 (stake screen),
% Addendum 12 Stage 2 (Scruples dilemmas) and Addendum 16.27 (DeepSeek collective)
% entry numbers moved from the prose to this comment (house rule: addendum numbers only in comments
% and Table tab-app-provenance; headings cleared in clarity loop 2), integrating editor, clarity loop 1 (2026-09-24)
Guard failures left several runs unread rather than repaired after the fact.
The first Mistral collective failed its truncation guard on one debate stage at a
2,560-token cap and was regenerated at 4,096 under a separately authorised
ceiling, with its \$35.90 first pass sunk and unread, and a 40-item screen of
gpt-4o in the collective failed its no-verdict guard at 15.0 percent, because the
platform's content filter blocked some posts, and was never read.
% prereg 16.26 amendment (~l.8363-8430) and RESULTS (~l.8462); 16.11b registration (~l.3596-3600)
% and RESULTS (~l.3639-3651)
% prereg 16.26 amendment ~l.8363-8375: the other party's advocate's opening stage in the third-person framing
% truncated on 7.6 percent of calls (clarity loop 3, appendix2-26, appendix2-48)
The overnight batch of 22 and 23 September, the registered runs launched
under one spending authorisation of the principal investigator with a
cumulative cap of \$250, cost about \$125, inside that cap, and additions registered on 23 September, among them the second
4,096-token run of Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} and the haiku plain-seat
collective of Appendix~\ref{app-seat}, took it to about \$270, above the cap
by the principal investigator's authorisation (Appendix~\ref{app-cost}).
% prereg overnight batch header (~l.8591-8597) and batch spend ledger (~l.9229-9234, total ~$125, inside the
% cap); 17.10 amendment run 2 RESULTS (~l.9628, about $22, batch about $182); 16.28 amendment E-D registration
% header (~l.9523, PI authorised spend above the $250 batch cap, ceiling $60) and RESULTS spend ledger
% (~l.9819, E-D $32.03 measured, batch total about $270); the 23 September additions also include 17.11, its
% two amendments and 17.12 (~l.9237-9790) (appendix2-02 (a))

% ============================================================================================
\subsection{Models and cost}
\label{app-cost}

Seven generators from six developers, three of them open-weight, and one
further judge, gpt-4o, appear in this paper (Table~\ref{tab-app-models}). Every call
went through Microsoft Azure, so a developer here is the company that built
the model, not the API provider, and open-weight status is as recorded in the
pre-registration and was not independently verified.
% prereg 17.1 RESULTS (~l.7736-7743); scripts/generators.py docstring (Azure OpenAI, Anthropic on
% Azure Foundry, Foundry /models, Foundry v1); scratchpad fact_sheet.json methods "Models"
Decoding settings differ by provider route. The Anthropic models run at the
provider's default temperature with no seed and no extended thinking, gpt-4o
and the three open-weight deployments at temperature 0.7, grok at the
provider's default temperature with no seed, and nano with medium reasoning effort when generating
and minimal effort when judging, with a floor of 8,192 completion tokens on
every call after 21 August 2026, a floor its June responses of
Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} predate.
% scripts/generators.py _call_anthropic, _call_openai (l.282-290), _call_xai, Foundry v1 (l.588),
% generate() floor (l.817, l.843); prereg 16.24 probe RESULTS (~l.6880-6882)
% scripts/generators.py generate() reasoning_effort default "medium" (l.777, used by run_elephant.py,
% run_pushback.py and run_crowdgold_*), scripts/elephant_scorers.py _judge_text reasoning_effort "minimal"
% (l.151); floor note CONFIRMED 2026-08-21 (l.832-843) (appendix2-35)
% scripts/generators.py _call_xai sets temperature only for non-reasoning models, and grok-4-1-fast-reasoning is a
% reasoning model; the Reproducibility statement says the same (clarity loop 3, appendix2-51)
The judges return 0 or 1, the production judge within 8 output tokens at the
provider's default temperature, so repeated judge calls on the same text can
disagree. For that reason every comparison in Appendices~\ref{app-judges}
and~\ref{app-rep4k} and the form, persona and length tests of
Appendix~\ref{app-form} score both conditions through the same path with the
same judge. The section-knockout and one-line tests instead keep stored
scores for responses of up to 4,000 characters and use fresh full-text
scores above that, so in those tests two conditions of different lengths can
be scored in different passes of the same judge, whose agreement with itself
Appendix~\ref{app-judges} measures.
% sentence split (clarity loop 3, appendix2-49)
% scripts/elephant_scorers.py _judge_text (max_tokens 8; reasoning judges 2,048 with minimal effort);
% scripts/analyze_knockout_social.py l.205 and scripts/analyze_counterparty_causal.py l.194 call
% apply_corrected_scores (scripts/analyze_length_matched_elephant.py, skips len <= 4000); the knockout and
% one-line tests use the production judge alone, whose test-retest agreement is in Appendix app-judges
% (judge_test_retest.json) (appendix2-37)
Measured cost figures apply the list prices in Table~\ref{tab-app-models} to
token counts read from the caches after each run, and the estimated figures
are identified below.
% scripts/run_crowdgold_deliberation.py PRICES (l.2161-2192); ledger (~l.9229-9234, judge calls and
% E1 grok estimated)

The overnight batch of 22 and 23 September, which produced the judge panel,
the first 4,096-token regeneration, the form, persona and length tests, the
pushback test and the grok and Llama plain-seat collectives, cost about \$125
against a \$250 cap. The grok and Llama plain-seat collectives cost about
\$26, Llama's \$16.4 measured and
grok's about \$9.5 estimated from the mean cost of the cached deliberation
calls, where the seat comparison's own results entry in the log gives about
\$8. Generation
for the 4,096-token regeneration and the form, persona and length tests cost
\$16.52, and judge calls about \$73 (haiku about \$26.7 including the
production-judge scores that the generation script computes as it generates, gpt-4o
about \$29.4, nano about \$6.5, Llama about \$6.6 and grok about \$3.6, each
rounded).
% prereg batch spend ledger (~l.9229-9234, judge calls ~$72.7, whose rounded parts sum to $72.8);
% 16.28 RESULTS header (~l.8977, grok ~$8, Llama ~$16.4); the batch's contents named in place of the
% 'Appendices app-judges to app-seat' range, which the move of Appendix app-collective would have broken
% (appendix2-02 (b), appendix2-03); scripts/run_elephant.py INLINE_METRICS (l.156-159, inline production-judge
% scoring) (appendix2-38)
The pushback test cost \$10.29 in the batch's spend record in the log, \$6.87 of it on haiku's
unread responses, although the four per-model figures in its artefacts sum to
\$9.94. The generation, pushback and Llama figures are measured from token
counts, the judge figures are estimated from characters sent and grok's from
the mean cost of cached calls, so the total is approximate. Additions
registered on 23 September brought the batch to about \$270, above its cap by
the principal investigator's authorisation, among them the second 4,096-token
run of Appendix~\ref{app-rep4k} at about \$22 and the haiku plain-seat
collective of Appendix~\ref{app-seat} at \$32.03 measured, inside its own
\$60 ceiling.
% prereg 17.9 RESULTS header (~l.9106, $10.29 with haiku 6.87, nano 1.39, Llama 1.23, grok 0.45);
% pushback_readout_{haiku,nano,llama,grok}.json measured_spend_usd (6.87 + 1.39 + 1.23 + 0.45 = 9.94);
% ledger (~l.9229-9234)
% prereg 17.10 amendment run 2 RESULTS (~l.9628, about $22 at list prices, inside its $25 ceiling, batch about
% $182); 16.28 amendment E-D registration (~l.9523, ceiling $60, PI authorisation above the batch cap) and
% RESULTS (~l.9789, 8,466 calls, $32.03 measured; spend ledger ~l.9819, batch about $270) (appendix2-02 (c))
The log holds no total for the whole study, only the spend of each addendum
against its ceiling.

\subsection{Further related work}
\label{app-related}
% whole-14 (round 3, 2026-09-24): the persona-cost clause and the topology sentence moved here from Section
% sec-related (main-text page budget), each key still cited in the paper (shah2026toonice in both places); the
% topology sentence carries clarity fix related-06 (loop 3, Related Work reviewer, 2026-09-24), its one-verb
% list split into what the work studies and what it finds, as finite clauses, same claims and all thirteen keys
Besides amplifying sycophancy when agreeable \citep{shah2026toonice},
persona prompts explain little variance in simulated surveys
\citep{hu2024persona}, can degrade zero-shot reasoning \citep{kim2025persona}
and carry implicit biases \citep{gupta2024bias,deshpande2023toxicity}.
Work on multi-agent topology scales and optimises agent graphs
\citep{qian2025macnet,zhuge2024gptswarm,liu2024dylan} and studies team size,
unguided homogeneous debate and when a minority should overturn the majority
\citep{bertalanic2026ringelmann,bertalanic2026costconsensus,he2026minoritysentinel}.
It finds that sparse graphs are competitive at lower cost \citep{li2024sparse},
topology governs how errors propagate
\citep{shen2025propagation,han2026conformity}, agents conform to majorities
\citep{zhang2024socialpsych,weng2025conformity}, correlated errors limit
aggregation \citep{kim2025correlated} and heterogeneous panels beat
homogeneous ones \citep{wang2025moa}.
Aggregation theory states when pooling judgements helps, under correlated
votes \citep{ladha1992condorcet,boland1989majority}, unequal competence
\citep{shapley1984optimizing,nitzan1982optimal,bahrami2010optimally}, social
influence in networks
\citep{degroot1974reaching,golub2010naive,lorenz2011social,becker2017network},
diversity \citep{zollman2010epistemic,hong2004groups,thompson2014diversity},
small deliberating groups \citep{navajas2018aggregated} and shared biases
\citep{prelec2017solution}, and where it cannot \citep{list2002aggregating}.
% moved verbatim from Section sec-related, paragraph "Topology and aggregation ask when pooling helps"
% (integrating editor, clarity loop 2, 2026-09-24, main-text page budget); every key cited there is cited
% here, so the bibliography is unchanged, and the Related Work sentence that points here keeps
% ladha1992condorcet and list2002aggregating
Mediation and mechanism design study agents with divergent interests
\citep{dafoe2020cooperative,akata2025repeated,piatti2024cooperate,leon2026personas,tewolde2026coopeval},
among them a mediator who privately recommends an action to each player and
so can reach outcomes no equilibrium of the players alone reaches
\citep{aumann1974subjectivity}, the position of the collective's neutral seat,
which speaks for neither party, and of the stronger judge of
Section~\ref{sec-collective}.
